Uttama Carita · Chapter 5 of 13 · 129 mantras (5.1–5.129)

देव्या दूतसंवादः

devyā dūtasaṃvādaḥ

The Devī's Dialogue with the Messenger · Complete with Noted Variants

Introduction

Summary
Medhas begins the third story (5.1). The asura brothers Śumbha and Niśumbha, trusting in pride and strength, seize from Indra the three worlds and the shares of the sacrifice (5.2), and take over the offices of the Sun, the Moon, Kubera, Yama, Varuṇa, Wind and Fire (5.3–4). The gods, defeated and driven out, remember the Unconquered Goddess and the boon she gave them: remembered in calamity, she would destroy their calamities at once (5.5–6). They go to the Himālaya and praise her as Viṣṇumāyā (5.7). Their hymn (5.9–82) opens with salutations to her names (5.9–13), runs through the yā devī stanzas, which find her in all beings as consciousness, intelligence, sleep, hunger, shadow, power, thirst and the rest, down to delusion (5.14–76), sums them up in a śloka on the all-pervading Goddess (5.77) and a last stanza on her as consciousness pervading the world (5.78–80), and closes, in a new metre, with a prayer that she bring good and strike down calamity (5.81–82). While they praise, Pārvatī comes to bathe in the Gaṅgā and asks whom they are praising; Śivā comes forth from the sheath of her body and answers that the hymn is hers (5.84–86). She is called Kauśikī, and Pārvatī, now dark, is called Kālikā and stays on the Himālaya (5.87–88). Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, Śumbha’s servants, see Ambikā’s beauty and report it to their master, listing every treasure he has already taken from the gods and asking why he does not take this ‘jewel among women’ too (5.89–100). Śumbha sends the asura Sugrīva as envoy (5.102–104). The envoy boasts of his master’s power and offers her either brother as husband (5.106–114). Grave, but smiling within, the Devī agrees that all he says is true, but she has a vow: whoever conquers her in battle, humbles her pride and matches her strength shall be her husband (5.116–121). The envoy calls her proud and threatens that she will be dragged by the hair (5.123–126); she repeats that her vow was made long ago and sends him back to tell Śumbha (5.128–129).
Forms of the Devī
Aparājitā, the Unconquered, remembered by the conquered gods (5.5); Viṣṇumāyā, the name with which the hymn begins and the yā devī series opens (5.7, 5.14–16); in the salutations, Devī, Mahādevī, Śivā, Prakṛti and Bhadrā (5.9), Raudrā, Nityā, Gaurī, Dhātrī and the Moon’s light (5.10), Kalyāṇī, Nairṛtī, the fortune of kings and Śarvāṇī (5.11), Durgā, Kṛṣṇā and Dhūmrā (5.12), the most gentle and most terrible at once (5.13); in the yā devī stanzas, the powers present in every being, from consciousness (5.17–19) to delusion (5.74–76); Vyāptidevī, presiding over the senses and the elements (5.77); citi, consciousness pervading the world (5.78–80); the Sovereign (Īśvarī, Īśā) of the closing prayer (5.81–82). In the narrative: Pārvatī (5.84), Śivā who comes forth from her (5.85), Ambikā called Kauśikī (5.87), Kālikā, Pārvatī turned dark (5.88), and, at the reply, Durgā, Bhagavatī, Bhadrā, by whom the world is upheld (5.116).
Significance of the events
The chapter holds the most recited hymn of the whole text, the gods’ praise with its refrain नमस्तस्यै, and the turn from hymn to story is immediate: the Goddess the gods have just found in all beings steps out of Pārvatī’s body in answer to her own question. The rest of the chapter sets the terms of the third story. The asuras see her only as a treasure to add to their plunder; she answers their claim of possession with a vow of battle. Nothing is fought in this chapter, but the war of chapters 6–10 is already decided by its two speeches.
Sanskrit patterns to watch
The hymn is built on the dative of homage with नमः (देव्यै, शिवायै, and the -ai datives of i-stems: प्रकृत्यै, कृत्यै, 5.9, 5.13) and on the relative-correlative frame या … तस्यै with the instrumental of manner …रूपेण संस्थिता (5.20–76). The narrative has perfects in both voices of √कृ (चक्राते, चक्रतुः, 5.3–4), a genitive absolute (5.84) and a locative absolute (5.88), and taddhitas of belonging (ऐन्दवम्, कौबेरम्, याम्यम्, 5.3; कौशिकी, 5.87). The speeches use passive imperatives as polite commands (ज्ञायताम्, गृह्यताम्, 5.91; श्रूयताम्, 5.119), gerundives (वक्तव्या, कार्यम्, 5.103), the optative in rhetorical questions (तिष्ठेत्, 5.123), मा with the imperative and the future (5.123, 5.126), and some epic forms beside the classical ones (शृणुष्व, 5.107; प्राप्स्यसे, 5.114). The closing prayer leaves the anuṣṭubh for vaṃśastha and indravaṃśā (5.81–82).
The teaching (as distinct from the narrative)
Literally, dispossessed gods sing a hymn, a goddess comes out of Pārvatī, and two demons hear of her beauty and send a messenger to claim her, who is refused with a vow. As teaching, the hymn says that the Goddess is not found only in what is noble: intelligence and faith, but also sleep, hunger, thirst and error, are named as her forms in every being (5.20–76), and all of them are states of one consciousness (5.78–80). The story then shows what it is to miss this: the asuras, who own every treasure of the three worlds, can see her only as one more treasure (5.100, 5.112), and offer her a sovereignty that is already hers (5.114, 5.116). Her answer, that only one who conquers her may have her, is read in the Śākta tradition as a statement that she is not possessed.

Themes

  • Remembrance (smṛti) and the promise of help
  • The Goddess in all beings: the yā devī hymn
  • Kauśikī: the Goddess who comes from Pārvatī’s body
  • Possession and its blindness: the logic of jewels
  • The vow: sovereignty that cannot be owned
Recited before the chapter (liturgical, from the Gita Press pāṭha)

These lines belong to the recitation, not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras. They are given here in Sanskrit and IAST only.

Viniyoga

ॐ अस्य श्रीउत्तरचरित्रस्य रुद्र ऋषिः, महासरस्वती देवता, अनुष्टुप् छन्दः, भीमा शक्तिः, भ्रामरी बीजम्, सूर्यस्तत्त्वम्, सामवेदः स्वरूपम्, महासरस्वतीप्रीत्यर्थे उत्तरचरित्रपाठे विनियोगः।

ॐ asya śrīuttaracaritrasya rudra ṛṣiḥ, mahāsarasvatī devatā, anuṣṭup chandaḥ, bhīmā śaktiḥ, bhrāmarī bījam, sūryastattvam, sāmavedaḥ svarūpam, mahāsarasvatīprītyarthe uttaracaritrapāṭhe viniyogaḥ|

Dhyāna

ॐ घण्टाशूलहलानि शङ्खमुसले चक्रं धनुः सायकं
हस्ताब्जैर्दधतीं घनान्तविलसच्छीतांशुतुल्यप्रभाम्।
गौरीदेहसमुद्भवां त्रिजगतामाधारभूतां महा-
पूर्वामत्र सरस्वतीमनुभजे शुम्भादिदैत्यार्दिनीम्॥

ॐ ghaṇṭāśūlahalāni śaṅkhamusale cakraṃ dhanuḥ sāyakaṃ
hastābjairdadhatīṃ ghanāntavilasacchītāṃśutulyaprabhām|
gaurīdehasamudbhavāṃ trijagatāmādhārabhūtāṃ mahā-
pūrvāmatra sarasvatīmanubhaje śumbhādidaityārdinīm||

5.1Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ॐ क्लीं ऋषिरुवाच ॥ १ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15) does not apply before a vowel
    The final s of ऋषिस् becomes ru (r). After i (not a/ā) the r simply stands before the vowel; it would become visarga only at a pause or before an unvoiced consonant.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicated syllable (व → उ): उ-वाच, ‘(he) said’, as in 1.1.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe seer said:

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas, the seer, resumes his account to King Suratha: with this mantra the Uttama Carita, the third and longest story (chapters 5–13), begins. In the Gita Press pāṭha the mantra carries the bīja ॐ क्लीं, which belongs to the recitation, not to the Purāṇa, and the carita is preceded by its own viniyoga and dhyāna.

5.2Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पुरा शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्यामसुराभ्यां शचीपतेः।
त्रैलोक्यं यज्ञभागाश्च हृता मदबलाश्रयात् ॥ २ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

पुरा | शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम् | असुराभ्याम् | शचीपतेःत्रैलोक्यम् | यज्ञभागाः | च | हृताः | मदबलाश्रयात्

3IAST

As recitedpurā śumbhaniśumbhābhyāmasurābhyāṃ śacīpateḥ |
trailokyaṃ yajñabhāgāśca hṛtā madabalāśrayāt ||

Separatedpurā | śumbhaniśumbhābhyām | asurābhyām | śacīpateḥ
trailokyam | yajñabhāgāḥ | ca | hṛtāḥ | madabalāśrayāt

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पुराpurāformerly, onceindeclinable (adverb of time)
शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम्śumbhaniśumbhābhyāmby Śumbha and Niśumbham. instr. du.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of the passive हृताः
असुराभ्याम्asurābhyāmby the two asurasm. instr. du. of असुर; in apposition to शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम्
शचीपतेःśacīpateḥof Śacī’s lord (Indra)m. gen. sg. of शचीपति (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); depends on त्रैलोक्यम् and यज्ञभागाः
त्रैलोक्यम्trailokyamthe three worlds (rule over them)n. nom. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोक); subject of हृताः
यज्ञभागाःyajñabhāgāḥthe shares in the sacrificem. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of हृताः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction); joins त्रैलोक्यम् and यज्ञभागाः
हृताःhṛtāḥwere taken away, were seizedpast passive participle (kta) of √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take’), m. nom. pl.; agrees with the nearer subject यज्ञभागाः
मदबलाश्रयात्madabalāśrayātrelying on their pride and strengthm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); ablative of cause

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्यामसुराभ्यां śumbhaniśumbhābhyāmasurābhyāṃ → शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम् + असुराभ्याम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m stands before the vowel of असुराभ्याम् and is written with it. The m of असुराभ्याम् becomes anusvāra before the ś of शचीपतेः (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • यज्ञभागाश्च yajñabhāgāśca → यज्ञभागाः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.
  • हृता मदबलाश्रयात् hṛtā madabalāśrayāt → हृताः + मदबलाश्रयात्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as ru) becomes y, and before a consonant that y is always lost, leaving -ā.
  • मदबलाश्रयात् madabalāśrayāt → मदबल + आश्रयात्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम् śumbhaniśumbhābhyām — dvandva (itaretara)
    Members: शुम्भ + निशुम्भ · Vigraha: शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, ताभ्याम् śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tābhyām
    Literally “by Śumbha and Niśumbha”; in the verse: the two asura brothers who rule the third story; the dual ending covers both
  • शचीपतेः śacīpateḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शची + पति · Vigraha: शच्याः पतिः, तस्य śacyāḥ patiḥ, tasya
    Literally “of the husband of Śacī”; in the verse: of Indra, king of the gods
  • यज्ञभागाः yajñabhāgāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: यज्ञ + भाग · Vigraha: यज्ञस्य भागाः yajñasya bhāgāḥ
    Literally “portions of the sacrifice”; in the verse: the offerings that belong to the gods
    Alternative: Also यज्ञे भागाः (saptamī tatpuruṣa), ‘shares in the sacrifice’; the sense is the same.
  • मदबलाश्रयात् madabalāśrayāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, its first member a dvandva
    Members: मदबल + आश्रय · Vigraha: मदबलस्य आश्रयः, तस्मात् (मदः च बलं च = मदबलम्) madabalasya āśrayaḥ, tasmāt (madaḥ ca balaṃ ca = madabalam)
    Literally “from resort to pride and strength”; in the verse: because they trusted in their arrogance and their might
    Alternative: मदबल can also be read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, मदस्य बलम्, ‘the strength of arrogance’; the dvandva gives the more natural pair.

C. Morphology

  • हृताः hṛtāḥ — Past passive participle of √हृ used as a finite verb (‘were seized’). With subjects of different gender and number (त्रैलोक्यम् n. sg., यज्ञभागाः m. pl.) it agrees with the nearer one.
  • त्रैलोक्यम् trailokyam — Taddhita with vṛddhi of the first syllable: त्रिलोक → त्रैलोक्य, ‘the three worlds’ taken as one whole, i.e. sovereignty over them.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पुरा मदबलाश्रयात् शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम् असुराभ्याम् शचीपतेः त्रैलोक्यम् यज्ञभागाः च हृताः।

purā madabalāśrayāt śumbhaniśumbhābhyām asurābhyām śacīpateḥ trailokyam yajñabhāgāḥ ca hṛtāḥ |

A passive sentence: the agents in the instrumental (शुम्भनिशुम्भाभ्याम् असुराभ्याम्), the things seized in the nominative (त्रैलोक्यम् यज्ञभागाः च), the cause in the ablative (मदबलाश्रयात्).

6Translation

LiteralFormerly, by the two asuras Śumbha and Niśumbha, through reliance on (their) pride and strength, the three worlds and the sacrificial shares of Śacī’s lord were taken away.

ReadableLong ago the asuras Śumbha and Niśumbha, trusting in their pride and their strength, seized from Indra, Śacī’s lord, the three worlds and his share of the sacrifices.

The passive is turned into an active sentence; ‘from Indra’ renders the genitive शचीपतेः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas opens the third story with a usurpation, as he opened the second (2.2–3): the demons take Indra’s rule over the three worlds and the gods’ shares of the sacrifice. This time the enemies are two brothers, Śumbha and Niśumbha, and their strength is said to rest on pride (मद).

Traditional interpretationIn the Gita Press pāṭha the Uttama Carita has its own viniyoga: Rudra is its ṛṣi, Mahāsarasvatī its devatā, anuṣṭubh its metre; its dhyāna pictures Mahāsarasvatī as born from the body of Gaurī and as the destroyer of Śumbha and the other daityas. This assignment is liturgical tradition, not part of the Purāṇa’s narrative.

5.3Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तावेव सूर्यतां तद्वदधिकारं तथैन्दवम्।
कौबेरमथ याम्यं च चक्राते वरुणस्य च ॥ ३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तौ | एव | सूर्यताम् | तद्वत् | अधिकारम् | तथा | ऐन्दवम्कौबेरम् | अथ | याम्यम् | च | चक्राते | वरुणस्य | च

3IAST

As recitedtāveva sūryatāṃ tadvadadhikāraṃ tathaindavam |
kauberamatha yāmyaṃ ca cakrāte varuṇasya ca ||

Separatedtau | eva | sūryatām | tadvat | adhikāram | tathā | aindavam
kauberam | atha | yāmyam | ca | cakrāte | varuṇasya | ca

4Word by word

चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
चक्रातेcakrāte(the two) took over, made their own√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, ātmanepada
वरुणस्यvaruṇasyaof Varuṇa (the lord of the waters)m. gen. sg. of वरुण; (अधिकारम्) understood
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तावेव tāveva → तौ + एव
    vowel sandhi (ayādi) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    Final au before a vowel becomes āv: tāv eva. The v is kept here (the optional loss of 8.3.19 applies to y/v after a/ā, and is not taken).
  • तद्वदधिकारं tadvadadhikāraṃ → तद्वत् + अधिकारम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.
  • तथैन्दवम् tathaindavam → तथा + ऐन्दवम्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + ai → ai.
  • कौबेरमथ kauberamatha → कौबेरम् + अथ
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Final m is written together with the following vowel; before य of याम्यम् and च the m becomes anusvāra.

C. Morphology

  • चक्राते cakrāte — Perfect of √कृ, third person dual, ātmanepada: च-क्र-आते. √कृ is ubhayapadī; the next verse uses the parasmaipada dual चक्रतुः for the same subject. The ātmanepada suits the sense ‘made (it) their own’.
  • सूर्यताम् sūryatām — The abstract suffix -tā (tal) makes a feminine noun of state from सूर्य: ‘Sun-ness’, i.e. the Sun’s function.
  • ऐन्दवम् aindavam — Taddhita with vṛddhi of the first vowel (इ → ऐ) and guṇa of the final u (u → av): इन्दु → ऐन्दव ‘belonging to the Moon’. कौबेर and याम्य are formed the same way from कुबेर and यम.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तौ एव सूर्यताम्, तद्वत् ऐन्दवम् अधिकारम्, तथा कौबेरम् अथ याम्यम् वरुणस्य च (अधिकारम्) चक्राते।

tau eva sūryatām, tadvat aindavam adhikāram, tathā kauberam atha yāmyam varuṇasya ca (adhikāram) cakrāte |

One verb (चक्राते) with a list of objects: the Sun’s state, and the offices of the Moon, Kubera, Yama and Varuṇa; अधिकारम् is understood with each adjective, and वरुणस्य is a genitive standing for a fifth.

6Translation

LiteralThose two themselves took over the state of the Sun, likewise the office of the Moon, so too that of Kubera, and of Yama, and of Varuṇa.

ReadableThey seized for themselves the Sun’s place, and in the same way the offices of the Moon, of Kubera, of Yama and of Varuṇa.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe brothers do not merely defeat the gods; they take over their functions in the cosmos one by one: the Sun, the Moon, Kubera (wealth), Yama (death) and Varuṇa (the waters).

Traditional interpretationThe list repeats the complaint of the gods against Mahiṣa in 2.6 (सूर्येन्द्राग्न्यनिलेन्दूनां यमस्य वरुणस्य च … अधिकारान्). Each carita begins with the order of the worlds overturned; the Goddess appears to restore it.

5.4Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तावेव पवनर्द्धिं च चक्रतुर्वह्निकर्म च।
ततो देवा विनिर्धूता भ्रष्टराज्याः पराजिताः ॥ ४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तौ | एव | पवनर्द्धिम् | च | चक्रतुः | वह्निकर्म | चततः | देवाः | विनिर्धूताः | भ्रष्टराज्याः | पराजिताः

3IAST

As recitedtāveva pavanarddhiṃ ca cakraturvahnikarma ca |
tato devā vinirdhūtā bhraṣṭarājyāḥ parājitāḥ ||

Separatedtau | eva | pavanarddhim | ca | cakratuḥ | vahnikarma | ca
tataḥ | devāḥ | vinirdhūtāḥ | bhraṣṭarājyāḥ | parājitāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. nom. du.; subject of चक्रतुः
एवevathey themselves, they aloneindeclinable (emphatic particle)
पवनर्द्धिम्pavanarddhimthe might of the Windf. acc. sg. of पवनर्द्धि (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); object of चक्रतुः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
चक्रतुःcakratuḥ(the two) took over, made their own√कृ (tanādi, 8U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, parasmaipada
वह्निकर्मvahnikarmathe work of Firen. acc. sg. of वह्निकर्मन्; compound (see Samāsa); object of चक्रतुः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
ततःtataḥthen, thereuponindeclinable (adverb of sequence)
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject
विनिर्धूताःvinirdhūtāḥdriven out, shaken offpast passive participle (kta) of वि + निर् + √धू (‘to shake’), m. nom. pl.; predicate
भ्रष्टराज्याःbhraṣṭarājyāḥhaving lost their kingdomm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवाः
पराजिताःparājitāḥdefeatedpast passive participle (kta) of परा + √जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवाः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तावेव tāveva → तौ + एव
    vowel sandhi (ayādi) · एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    As in 5.3: au before a vowel becomes āv.
  • चक्रतुर्वह्निकर्म cakraturvahnikarma → चक्रतुः + वह्निकर्म
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After u the final s (as ru) stays r before the voiced v; it becomes visarga only at a pause or before an unvoiced consonant (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15).
  • ततो देवा tato devā → ततः + देवाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.
  • देवा विनिर्धूता भ्रष्टराज्याः devā vinirdhūtā bhraṣṭarājyāḥ → देवाः + विनिर्धूताः + भ्रष्टराज्याः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga becomes y, which is lost before a consonant, leaving -ā. Before the unvoiced p of पराजिताः the visarga of भ्रष्टराज्याः stays.
  • पवनर्द्धिं pavanarddhiṃ → पवन + ऋद्धिम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ṛ → ar. The ddh belongs to ऋद्धि itself (√ऋध् + ktin); the final m is anusvāra before च.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • पवनर्द्धिम् pavanarddhim — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पवन + ऋद्धि · Vigraha: पवनस्य ऋद्धिः, ताम् pavanasya ṛddhiḥ, tām
    Literally “the prosperity (power) of the Wind”; in the verse: the function and might of Vāyu
  • वह्निकर्म vahnikarma — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: वह्नि + कर्मन् · Vigraha: वह्नेः कर्म vahneḥ karma
    Literally “the action of Fire”; in the verse: Agni’s office, chiefly carrying the offerings
  • भ्रष्टराज्याः bhraṣṭarājyāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: भ्रष्ट + राज्य · Vigraha: भ्रष्टं राज्यं येषां ते bhraṣṭaṃ rājyaṃ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose kingdom has fallen away”; in the verse: the gods, stripped of their rule

C. Morphology

  • चक्रतुः cakratuḥ — Perfect of √कृ, third person dual, parasmaipada (च-क्र-अतुः). The previous verse used the ātmanepada चक्राते for the same two subjects; both voices of this ubhayapadī root are used side by side.
  • भ्रष्टराज्याः bhraṣṭarājyāḥ — भ्रष्ट is the past participle of √भ्रंश् ‘to fall’: the nasal is lost and ś + t gives ṣṭ.
  • विनिर्धूताः vinirdhūtāḥ — Past participle of √धू with two prefixes, वि and निर्: ‘shaken off completely’, i.e. expelled. The participles serve as predicates; the verb ‘were’ is understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तौ एव पवनर्द्धिम् च वह्निकर्म च चक्रतुः। ततः देवाः पराजिताः भ्रष्टराज्याः विनिर्धूताः (अभवन्)।

tau eva pavanarddhim ca vahnikarma ca cakratuḥ | tataḥ devāḥ parājitāḥ bhraṣṭarājyāḥ vinirdhūtāḥ (abhavan) |

The first line completes the list of 5.3 with a new verb; the second is a nominal sentence of three participles with देवाः as subject.

6Translation

LiteralThose two themselves took over the might of the Wind and the work of Fire. Then the gods, defeated, their kingdom lost, were driven out.

ReadableThey took the Wind’s power too, and the office of Fire. So the gods, defeated and stripped of their kingdom, were cast out.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe usurpation is complete: Wind and Fire follow Sun, Moon, Kubera, Yama and Varuṇa. The second line gives the result in three participles: defeated, kingdomless, expelled.

Traditional interpretationFire (वह्नि) carries the offerings to the gods; its work in demon hands means the sacrifice itself no longer reaches them, which completes the loss of the यज्ञभागाः named in 5.2.

5.5Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

हृताधिकारास्त्रिदशास्ताभ्यां सर्वे निराकृताः।
महासुराभ्यां तां देवीं संस्मरन्त्यपराजिताम् ॥ ५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

हृताधिकाराः | त्रिदशाः | ताभ्याम् | सर्वे | निराकृताःमहासुराभ्याम् | ताम् | देवीम् | संस्मरन्ति | अपराजिताम्

3IAST

As recitedhṛtādhikārāstridaśāstābhyāṃ sarve nirākṛtāḥ |
mahāsurābhyāṃ tāṃ devīṃ saṃsmarantyaparājitām ||

Separatedhṛtādhikārāḥ | tridaśāḥ | tābhyām | sarve | nirākṛtāḥ
mahāsurābhyām | tām | devīm | saṃsmaranti | aparājitām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
हृताधिकाराःhṛtādhikārāḥdeprived of their officesm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्रिदशाः
त्रिदशाःtridaśāḥthe gods (lit. ‘the thirty’)m. nom. pl. of त्रिदश; compound (see Samāsa); subject of संस्मरन्ति
ताभ्याम्tābhyāmby those twopronoun तद्, m. instr. du.; agent of निराकृताः
सर्वेsarveallpronominal adjective सर्व, m. nom. pl.; qualifies त्रिदशाः
निराकृताःnirākṛtāḥcast out, rejectedpast passive participle (kta) of निर् + आ + √कृ (tanādi, 8U), m. nom. pl.; qualifies त्रिदशाः
महासुराभ्याम्mahāsurābhyāmby the two great asurasm. instr. du.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ताभ्याम्
ताम्tāmthat (Goddess)pronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; qualifies देवीम्
देवीम्devīmthe Goddessf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of संस्मरन्ति
संस्मरन्तिsaṃsmaranticall to mind, rememberसम् + √स्मृ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to remember’), laṭ (present, here historic), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
अपराजिताम्aparājitāmthe Unconqueredf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवीम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • हृताधिकारास्त्रिदशास्ताभ्यां hṛtādhikārāstridaśāstābhyāṃ → हृताधिकाराः + त्रिदशाः + ताभ्याम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, twice over. The m of ताभ्याम् becomes anusvāra before स (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • संस्मरन्त्यपराजिताम् saṃsmarantyaparājitām → संस्मरन्ति + अपराजिताम्
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.
  • हृताधिकारा hṛtādhikārā → हृत + अधिकार
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • हृताधिकाराः hṛtādhikārāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: हृत + अधिकार · Vigraha: हृतः अधिकारः येषां ते hṛtaḥ adhikāraḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose office has been taken”; in the verse: the gods, robbed of their cosmic functions (5.3–4)
  • त्रिदशाः tridaśāḥ — bahuvrīhi (of number)
    Members: त्रि + दशन् · Vigraha: त्रिः आवृत्ताः दश (परिमाणम् एषाम्) ते triḥ āvṛttāḥ daśa (parimāṇam eṣām) te
    Literally “those whose count is ten thrice”; in the verse: the gods, ‘the Thirty’ (a round number for the thirty-three)
    Alternative: Also explained from दशा ‘stage of life’ (the gods who remain always in the third stage, youth); the numerical sense is the usual one.
  • महासुराभ्याम् mahāsurābhyām — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तौ च तौ असुरौ च, ताभ्याम् mahāntau ca tau asurau ca, tābhyām
    Literally “by the two great asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha and Niśumbha
  • अपराजिताम् aparājitām — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ (नञ्) + पराजिता · Vigraha: न पराजिता, अपराजिता, ताम् na parājitā, aparājitā, tām
    Literally “not conquered”; in the verse: the Unconquered: her epithet, set against the defeated gods (पराजिताः, 5.4)

C. Morphology

  • संस्मरन्ति saṃsmaranti — Present of सम् + √स्मृ (guṇa स्मर-), used as a historic present in narrative: ‘they remembered’.
  • निराकृताः nirākṛtāḥ — Past participle of निर् + आ + √कृ, ‘thrust away, expelled’; the same word describes the gods driven from heaven by Mahiṣa (2.7).

D. Anvaya — prose order

ताभ्याम् महासुराभ्याम् हृताधिकाराः निराकृताः (च) सर्वे त्रिदशाः ताम् अपराजिताम् देवीम् संस्मरन्ति।

tābhyām mahāsurābhyām hṛtādhikārāḥ nirākṛtāḥ (ca) sarve tridaśāḥ tām aparājitām devīm saṃsmaranti |

The participles हृताधिकाराः and निराकृताः (with the agent ताभ्याम् महासुराभ्याम्) describe the subject त्रिदशाः; the main verb is संस्मरन्ति with its object देवीम्.

6Translation

LiteralDeprived of their offices, all the Thirty, cast out by those two great asuras, call to mind that Goddess, the Unconquered.

ReadableRobbed of their offices and driven out by the two great asuras, all the gods turned their minds to that Goddess, the Unconquered One.

The historic present संस्मरन्ति is rendered as a past.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods do not fight back; they remember. The verb सं-√स्मृ prepares the next verse, where the boon they recall was given precisely for remembrance. The Goddess is called Aparājitā, ‘unconquered’, in the verse after the gods were called पराजिताः, ‘conquered’.

Traditional interpretationThe remembrance answers the end of the fourth chapter: there the gods asked that whenever she was remembered she would destroy their calamities (4.35–36), and she consented (4.39). The third story begins by invoking that promise.

5.6Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तयास्माकं वरो दत्तो यथाऽऽपत्सु स्मृताखिलाः।
भवतां नाशयिष्यामि तत्क्षणात्परमापदः ॥ ६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तया | अस्माकम् | वरः | दत्तः | यथा | आपत्सु | स्मृता | अखिलाःभवताम् | नाशयिष्यामि | तत्क्षणात् | परमापदः

3IAST

As recitedtayāsmākaṃ varo datto yathā''patsu smṛtākhilāḥ |
bhavatāṃ nāśayiṣyāmi tatkṣaṇātparamāpadaḥ ||

Separatedtayā | asmākam | varaḥ | dattaḥ | yathā | āpatsu | smṛtā | akhilāḥ
bhavatām | nāśayiṣyāmi | tatkṣaṇāt | paramāpadaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
तयाtayāby herpronoun तद्, f. instr. sg.; agent of the passive दत्तः
अस्माकम्asmākamto us (lit. ‘of us’)pronoun अस्मद्, gen. pl.; the recipient of the boon
वरःvaraḥa boonm. nom. sg. of वर; subject of the passive
दत्तःdattaḥwas givenpast passive participle (kta) of √दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), m. nom. sg.; agrees with वरः
यथाyathāthat, to this effectindeclinable; introduces the words of the boon
आपत्सुāpatsuin calamitiesf. loc. pl. of आपद् (consonant stem)
स्मृताsmṛtā(I,) remembered, when called to mindpast passive participle (kta) of √स्मृ (bhvādi, 1P), f. nom. sg.; refers to the Goddess, the subject of नाशयिष्यामि
अखिलाःakhilāḥall, without exceptionf. acc. pl. of अखिल; qualifies परमापदः
भवताम्bhavatāmyour (of you, sirs)pronoun भवत् (respectful second person), m. gen. pl.; depends on परमापदः
नाशयिष्यामिnāśayiṣyāmiI shall destroycausative of √नश् (divādi, 4P ‘to perish’), lṛṭ (simple future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तत्क्षणात्tatkṣaṇātat that very moment, instantlym. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); used adverbially
परमापदःparamāpadaḥextreme calamitiesf. acc. pl. of परमापद्; compound (see Samāsa); object of नाशयिष्यामि

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तयास्माकं tayāsmākaṃ → तया + अस्माकम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The final m becomes anusvāra before व (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • वरो दत्तो यथा varo datto yathā → वरः + दत्तः + यथा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, twice.
  • यथाऽऽपत्सु yathā''patsu → यथा + आपत्सु
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press marks the merged long ā with a double avagraha (ऽऽ), a printing convention only.
  • स्मृताखिलाः smṛtākhilāḥ → स्मृता + अखिलाः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā; the two words belong to different parts of the sentence (स्मृता to the speaker, अखिलाः to परमापदः).
  • तत्क्षणात्परमापदः tatkṣaṇātparamāpadaḥ → तत्क्षणात् + परमापदः
    no change (final t before an unvoiced consonant) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    Final t stays unvoiced before p; the words are only written together. Inside परमापद्, परम + आपद् gives ā (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • तत्क्षणात् tatkṣaṇāt — karmadhāraya
    Members: तद् + क्षण · Vigraha: सः चासौ क्षणः च, तस्मात् saḥ cāsau kṣaṇaḥ ca, tasmāt
    Literally “from that moment”; in the verse: instantly, the very moment I am remembered
  • परमापदः paramāpadaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: परम + आपद् · Vigraha: परमाः च ताः आपदः च paramāḥ ca tāḥ āpadaḥ ca
    Literally “supreme calamities”; in the verse: the gravest misfortunes; the first member stands in its stem form परम-

C. Morphology

  • नाशयिष्यामि nāśayiṣyāmi — Simple future of the causative stem नाशय- (√नश् ‘perish’ → ‘cause to perish’) with the future marker -इष्य-: नाशय् + इष्य + आमि.
  • स्मृता smṛtā — A feminine participle with the force of a condition: ‘(I,) being remembered’, i.e. ‘whenever I am remembered’. The Goddess is quoted speaking of herself.
  • अस्माकम् asmākam — Genitive for the recipient: वरः अस्माकं दत्तः, ‘a boon was given of us’ = ‘to us’, a common use of the genitive in place of the dative.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तया अस्माकम् वरः दत्तः, यथा — आपत्सु स्मृता (अहम्) भवताम् अखिलाः परमापदः तत्क्षणात् नाशयिष्यामि (इति)।

tayā asmākam varaḥ dattaḥ, yathā — āpatsu smṛtā (aham) bhavatām akhilāḥ paramāpadaḥ tatkṣaṇāt nāśayiṣyāmi (iti) |

A passive main clause (तया … वरः दत्तः) followed by the boon in direct speech, introduced by यथा: the Goddess is the unexpressed subject of नाशयिष्यामि, and स्मृता is a participle of condition.

6Translation

LiteralBy her a boon was given to us, that: ‘Remembered in calamities, I shall at that very moment destroy all your extreme calamities.’

ReadableShe gave us a boon: ‘When I am remembered in your misfortunes, I will that very instant destroy all your direst calamities.’

The verse is the gods’ own thought, closed by इति in 5.7; the passive ‘was given by her’ is rendered as active.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods recall the promise made at the end of the second story: they asked, संस्मृता संस्मृता त्वं नो हिंसेथाः परमापदः (4.36), and she consented (4.39). Here the same promise is quoted in her own voice, with the same word परमापदः.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the gods have lost everything except a promise, and their first act is to remember it. In this text remembrance is not a feeling but the condition on which help arrives.

5.7Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इति कृत्वा मतिं देवा हिमवन्तं नगेश्वरम्।
जग्मुस्तत्र ततो देवीं विष्णुमायां प्रतुष्टुवुः ॥ ७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इति | कृत्वा | मतिम् | देवाः | हिमवन्तम् | नगेश्वरम्जग्मुः | तत्र | ततः | देवीम् | विष्णुमायाम् | प्रतुष्टुवुः

3IAST

As recitediti kṛtvā matiṃ devā himavantaṃ nageśvaram |
jagmustatra tato devīṃ viṣṇumāyāṃ pratuṣṭuvuḥ ||

Separatediti | kṛtvā | matim | devāḥ | himavantam | nageśvaram
jagmuḥ | tatra | tataḥ | devīm | viṣṇumāyām | pratuṣṭuvuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
इतिitithus (closing the thought of 5.6)indeclinable (quotative particle)
कृत्वाkṛtvāhaving madeabsolutive (ktvā) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U)
मतिम्matimresolve, a decisionf. acc. sg. of मति (i-stem); object of कृत्वा
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of जग्मुः and प्रतुष्टुवुः
हिमवन्तम्himavantamHimavat, the Himālayam. acc. sg. of हिमवत् (matup: ‘having snow’); goal of जग्मुः
नगेश्वरम्nageśvaramthe lord of mountainsm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to हिमवन्तम्
जग्मुःjagmuḥwent√गम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to go’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of sequence)
देवीम्devīmthe Goddessf. acc. sg. of देवी; object of प्रतुष्टुवुः
विष्णुमायाम्viṣṇumāyāmViṣṇumāyā, the Māyā of Viṣṇuf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to देवीम्
प्रतुष्टुवुःpratuṣṭuvuḥpraised, began to praiseप्र + √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मतिं देवा हिमवन्तं matiṃ devā himavantaṃ → मतिम् + देवाः + हिमवन्तम्
    anusvāra and visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    Final m becomes anusvāra before a consonant. -āḥ before the voiced h: the visarga becomes y, which is lost before a consonant.
  • जग्मुस्तत्र jagmustatra → जग्मुः + तत्र
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • ततो देवीं tato devīṃ → ततः + देवीम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.
  • नगेश्वरम् nageśvaram → नग + ईश्वरम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नगेश्वरम् nageśvaram — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: नग + ईश्वर · Vigraha: नगानाम् ईश्वरः, तम् nagānām īśvaraḥ, tam
    Literally “lord of the mountains”; in the verse: the Himālaya, king of mountains
  • विष्णुमायाम् viṣṇumāyām — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विष्णु + माया · Vigraha: विष्णोः माया, ताम् viṣṇoḥ māyā, tām
    Literally “the māyā of Viṣṇu”; in the verse: the Goddess as the māyā belonging to Viṣṇu (compare Hari’s Mahāmāyā, 1.55); the name returns in the first yā devī stanza (5.14–16)

C. Morphology

  • प्रतुष्टुवुः pratuṣṭuvuḥ — Perfect of √स्तु: the reduplicating syllable keeps the stop (तु-, not स्तु-), and the root’s s becomes ṣ after u (तुष्टु-); before the ending -उः the u becomes uv. With प्र: ‘they praised forth’.
  • जग्मुः jagmuḥ — Perfect of √गम्: reduplication ज- (g → j) and loss of the root vowel before the weak ending: ज-ग्म्-उः.
  • कृत्वा kṛtvā — Absolutive: ‘having made (up their) mind’; the action precedes that of the main verb जग्मुः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

इति मतिम् कृत्वा देवाः नगेश्वरम् हिमवन्तम् जग्मुः। ततः तत्र विष्णुमायाम् देवीम् प्रतुष्टुवुः।

iti matim kṛtvā devāḥ nageśvaram himavantam jagmuḥ | tataḥ tatra viṣṇumāyām devīm pratuṣṭuvuḥ |

Two main verbs with one subject (देवाः): जग्मुः with its goal हिमवन्तम्, and प्रतुष्टुवुः with its object देवीम्; कृत्वा marks the prior resolve.

6Translation

LiteralHaving made up their mind thus, the gods went to Himavat, lord of mountains; there, then, they praised the Goddess Viṣṇumāyā.

ReadableResolved on this, the gods went to the Himālaya, king of mountains, and there they sang the praises of the Goddess, Viṣṇumāyā.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods go to the Himālaya and begin the hymn that runs from 5.9 to 5.82. The place matters to the story: it is there that Pārvatī comes to bathe (5.84) and that Ambikā comes forth from her body (5.85–87).

Traditional interpretationThe name Viṣṇumāyā links this hymn to the first story, where the Goddess was Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā and Mahāmāyā (1.54–55). The hymn’s first yā devī stanza names her by this very title (5.14–16).

5.8Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

देवा ऊचुः ॥ ८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवाः | ऊचुः

3IAST

As reciteddevā ūcuḥ ||

Separateddevāḥ | ūcuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
ऊचुःūcuḥsaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • देवा ऊचुः devā ūcuḥ → देवाः + ऊचुः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y, the y is dropped, and the two vowels stand apart. The gap is not closed again.

C. Morphology

  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ — Perfect plural of √वच् with samprasāraṇa (va → u) in both the root and the reduplication: उ + उच् + उः → ऊचुः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवाः ऊचुः।

devāḥ ūcuḥ |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe gods said:

ReadableThe gods said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods, on the Himālaya, begin their hymn to the Goddess (5.9–82), as Medhas reports it to Suratha.

5.9Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नमो देव्यै महादेव्यै शिवायै सततं नमः।
नमः प्रकृत्यै भद्रायै नियताः प्रणताः स्म ताम् ॥ ९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नमः | देव्यै | महादेव्यै | शिवायै | सततम् | नमःनमः | प्रकृत्यै | भद्रायै | नियताः | प्रणताः | स्म | ताम्

3IAST

As recitednamo devyai mahādevyai śivāyai satataṃ namaḥ |
namaḥ prakṛtyai bhadrāyai niyatāḥ praṇatāḥ sma tām ||

Separatednamaḥ | devyai | mahādevyai | śivāyai | satatam | namaḥ
namaḥ | prakṛtyai | bhadrāyai | niyatāḥ | praṇatāḥ | sma | tām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homageindeclinable नमस् ‘bow, homage’; takes the dative of the one honoured (अस्तु ‘be’ understood)
देव्यैdevyaito the Goddessf. dat. sg. of देवी (ī-stem)
महादेव्यैmahādevyaito the Great Goddessf. dat. sg. of महादेवी; compound (see Samāsa)
शिवायैśivāyaito Śivā, the Auspicious Onef. dat. sg. of शिवा (ā-stem)
सततम्satatamalways, constantlyindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत ‘continual’)
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
प्रकृत्यैprakṛtyaito Prakṛti, primordial Naturef. dat. sg. of प्रकृति (i-stem)
भद्रायैbhadrāyaito Bhadrā, the Gracious, the Benignf. dat. sg. of भद्रा (ā-stem)
नियताःniyatāḥrestrained, with minds held in checkpast participle (kta) of नि + √यम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to hold’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies the speakers (वयम्, understood)
प्रणताःpraṇatāḥbowed downpast participle (kta) of प्र + √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. nom. pl.; predicate of the understood वयम्
स्मsma(we) areas printed the indeclinable स्म; here understood, as in 4.3 and 4.5, as स्मः (√अस्, adādi 2P, laṭ, uttama-puruṣa bahuvacana ‘we are’) without its visarga, completing प्रणताः: ‘we are bowed’ (see flags)
ताम्tāmto her (lit. ‘her’)pronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; object of प्रणताः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमो देव्यै namo devyai → नमः + देव्यै
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o. The same change gives every नमो before a voiced sound in the hymn.
  • सततं नमः satataṃ namaḥ → सततम् + नमः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • नमः प्रकृत्यै namaḥ prakṛtyai → नमः + प्रकृत्यै
    visarga before an unvoiced consonant (no change) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before p the visarga stays. Before the s of स्म (प्रणताः स्म) it also stays, as optionally allowed before a sibilant.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महादेव्यै mahādevyai — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + देवी · Vigraha: महती चासौ देवी च, तस्यै mahatī cāsau devī ca, tasyai
    Literally “to the great goddess”; in the verse: the Goddess as supreme among deities; महत् takes the form महा- as first member of a karmadhāraya

C. Morphology

  • प्रकृत्यै prakṛtyai — Feminine i-stems may take the dative ending -ai (प्रकृत्यै) beside the general -aye (प्रकृतये). The hymn uses the -ai forms throughout (वृद्ध्यै, सिद्ध्यै, ख्यात्यै, कृत्यै), matching the ā- and ī-stems (शिवायै, देव्यै).
  • प्रणताः praṇatāḥ — Past participle of प्र + √नम्: the nasal of the root is lost before -ta (नत), and n becomes ṇ after the r of प्र. With the accusative ताम्: ‘bowed to her’.
  • स्म sma — The same formula नताः स्म occurs in chapter 4 (4.3, 4.5). The participle needs a copula ‘we are’; the full verb appears elsewhere as स्मः (प्रपन्नाः स्मः, 2.8).

D. Anvaya — prose order

देव्यै नमः, महादेव्यै शिवायै सततम् नमः। प्रकृत्यै भद्रायै नमः। नियताः (वयम्) ताम् प्रणताः स्म।

devyai namaḥ, mahādevyai śivāyai satatam namaḥ | prakṛtyai bhadrāyai namaḥ | niyatāḥ (vayam) tām praṇatāḥ sma |

Three salutations with नमः and the dative, then a statement of the speakers: (वयम्) नियताः ताम् प्रणताः स्म ‘we, restrained, are bowed to her’.

6Translation

LiteralSalutation to the Goddess, to the Great Goddess; to Śivā always salutation. Salutation to Prakṛti, to Bhadrā. Self-restrained, we have bowed to her.

ReadableHomage to the Goddess, to the Great Goddess; to the Auspicious One, homage for ever. Homage to Nature, the Primal One, to the Gracious One. With minds held still we bow before her.

‘The Primal One’ makes explicit that प्रकृति here is the cosmic principle, not ‘nature’ in the everyday sense.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods’ hymn opens with salutations in the dative: the Goddess is named as Devī, Mahādevī, Śivā (the auspicious), Prakṛti (the source of the manifest world) and Bhadrā (the benign). The last pāda turns from address to statement: the gods describe themselves as restrained and bowed.

Traditional interpretationThe hymn of 5.9–82 is recited on its own in the Śākta tradition as the Tantrokta Devīsūkta. Its opening verse is among the most widely recited in the text.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the names 209, महादेवी, and 53, शिवा, and the related 397, मूलप्रकृतिः. The shared epithets belong to a common Śākta vocabulary; the two works are separate.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: स्म: the printed short form is required by the metre (the seventh syllable of an even anuṣṭubh pāda must be light, which स्मः before ताम् would not be). As in 4.3 and 4.5 it is taken as स्मः ‘we are’ with the visarga lost metri causa; it may also be the particle स्म with the copula understood. The sense, ‘we bow to her’, is the same either way.

5.10Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

रौद्रायै नमो नित्यायै गौर्यै धात्र्यै नमो नमः।

2Padaccheda — the words separated

रौद्रायै | नमः | नित्यायै | गौर्यै | धात्र्यै | नमः | नमःज्योत्स्नायै | च | इन्दुरूपिण्यै | सुखायै | सततम् | नमः

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • इन्दुरूपिण्यै indurūpiṇyai — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (इन्दुरूप) with the possessive taddhita -in
    Members: इन्दु + रूप (+ इन्, feminine -ई) · Vigraha: इन्दोः रूपम् इन्दुरूपम्; तत् अस्याः अस्ति इति इन्दुरूपिणी, तस्यै indoḥ rūpam indurūpam; tat asyāḥ asti iti indurūpiṇī, tasyai
    Literally “to her who has the Moon’s form”; in the verse: the Goddess as the Moon itself, cool and gentle
    Alternative: इन्दुरूप can also be a karmadhāraya, इन्दुः एव रूपम् ‘whose form is the Moon itself’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • धात्र्यै dhātryai — धात्री is the feminine of the agent noun धातृ (√धा ‘to place, support’ + tṛc): ‘she who upholds’. Dative singular of the ī-stem: धात्र्यै.
  • रौद्रायै raudrāyai — A taddhita with vṛddhi of the first vowel (u → au): रुद्र → रौद्र ‘of Rudra, fierce’; feminine रौद्रा.
  • गौर्यै gauryai — ī-stem dative: गौरी + ऐ → गौर्यै (ī becomes y before the vowel of the ending).

D. Anvaya — prose order

रौद्रायै नमः। नित्यायै गौर्यै धात्र्यै नमः नमः। ज्योत्स्नायै इन्दुरूपिण्यै च सुखायै सततम् नमः।

raudrāyai namaḥ | nityāyai gauryai dhātryai namaḥ namaḥ | jyotsnāyai indurūpiṇyai ca sukhāyai satatam namaḥ |

A series of datives, each group closed by नमः; च joins ज्योत्स्नायै and इन्दुरूपिण्यै.

6Translation

LiteralTo the Fierce One salutation; to the Eternal, to Gaurī, to the Supporter salutation, salutation. To Moonlight, and to her whose form is the Moon, to Happiness, always salutation.

ReadableHomage to the Fierce One; homage and homage to the Eternal, to Gaurī, to her who upholds all. To the Moonlight, to her whose form is the Moon, to Joy itself, homage for ever.

‘All’ is supplied with ‘upholds’; the Sanskrit has the bare agent noun धात्री.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn sets terrible and gentle names side by side: Raudrā, of Rudra’s fierceness, beside the cool Moon and its light, and Sukhā, happiness. The pairing is stated outright in 5.13 (अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रा). Gaurī, ‘the Fair’, is Pārvatī’s name; the story will soon show the bright Ambikā coming forth from Pārvatī and Pārvatī herself turning dark (5.85–88).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 136, नित्या, and 635, गौरी, and the related 935, जगद्धात्री. The names are shared epithets of the Goddess; the hymns are separate.

5.11Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कल्याण्यै प्रणतां वृद्ध्यै सिद्ध्यै कुर्मो नमो नमः।
नैर्ऋत्यै भूभृतां लक्ष्म्यै शर्वाण्यै ते नमो नमः ॥ ११ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कल्याण्यै | प्रणताम् | वृद्ध्यै | सिद्ध्यै | कुर्मः | नमः | नमःनैर्ऋत्यै | भूभृताम् | लक्ष्म्यै | शर्वाण्यै | ते | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedkalyāṇyai praṇatāṃ vṛddhyai siddhyai kurmo namo namaḥ |
nairṛtyai bhūbhṛtāṃ lakṣmyai śarvāṇyai te namo namaḥ ||

Separatedkalyāṇyai | praṇatām | vṛddhyai | siddhyai | kurmaḥ | namaḥ | namaḥ
nairṛtyai | bhūbhṛtām | lakṣmyai | śarvāṇyai | te | namaḥ | namaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कल्याण्यैkalyāṇyaito the Auspicious One, Kalyāṇīf. dat. sg. of कल्याणी (ī-stem)
प्रणताम्praṇatāmof those who bowgen. pl. (see Morph and Flags); depends on वृद्ध्यै and सिद्ध्यै
वृद्ध्यैvṛddhyaito (her who is) the increase, prosperityf. dat. sg. of वृद्धि (i-stem)
सिद्ध्यैsiddhyaito (her who is) fulfilment, successf. dat. sg. of सिद्धि (i-stem)
कुर्मःkurmaḥwe make, we offer√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्; object of कुर्मः
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नैर्ऋत्यैnairṛtyaito Nairṛtī, the power of Nirṛtif. dat. sg. of नैर्ऋती (taddhita from निरृति, feminine in -ī)
भूभृताम्bhūbhṛtāmof kings (lit. ‘of earth-bearers’)m. gen. pl. of भूभृत्; compound (see Samāsa); depends on लक्ष्म्यै
लक्ष्म्यैlakṣmyaito (her who is) the fortune, the Lakṣmīf. dat. sg. of लक्ष्मी (ī-stem)
शर्वाण्यैśarvāṇyaito Śarvāṇī, consort of Śarva (Śiva)f. dat. sg. of शर्वाणी (from शर्व with the feminine suffix -ānī)
तेteto youpronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic form)
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रणतां वृद्ध्यै praṇatāṃ vṛddhyai → प्रणताम् + वृद्ध्यै
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so also भूभृतां लक्ष्म्यै.
  • कुर्मो नमो नमः kurmo namo namaḥ → कुर्मः + नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o, twice.
  • ते नमो नमः te namo namaḥ → ते + नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    As in the first line; ते itself stands unchanged before a consonant.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • भूभृताम् bhūbhṛtām — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: भू + भृत् · Vigraha: भुवं बिभर्ति इति भूभृत्, तेषाम् bhuvaṃ bibharti iti bhūbhṛt, teṣām
    Literally “of those who bear the earth”; in the verse: of kings
    Alternative: भूभृत् also means ‘mountain’ (the earth-bearer); ‘the splendour of mountains’ is possible, but the pairing with Lakṣmī, royal fortune, favours ‘kings’.

C. Morphology

  • प्रणताम् praṇatām — Best taken as a genitive plural, ‘of those who bow’, parallel to भूभृताम् लक्ष्म्यै in the second line: वृद्धि and सिद्धि are the prosperity and fulfilment of the devotee. The regular genitive plural of प्रणत is प्रणतानाम्; the short form is explained either as a shortened (ārṣa) form of it, or from a root-noun stem प्रणत्. An accusative singular feminine does not fit the series of datives.
  • कुर्मः kurmaḥ — Present of √कृ, first person plural: the weak stem कुरु- loses its u before m (कुर्मः). नमः कुर्मः ‘we make obeisance’.
  • ते te — Enclitic dative of the second person. Up to here the gods spoke of the Goddess in the third person; ते is the first direct address in the hymn.

D. Anvaya — prose order

कल्याण्यै प्रणताम् वृद्ध्यै सिद्ध्यै (च) नमः नमः कुर्मः। नैर्ऋत्यै भूभृताम् लक्ष्म्यै शर्वाण्यै ते नमः नमः।

kalyāṇyai praṇatām vṛddhyai siddhyai (ca) namaḥ namaḥ kurmaḥ | nairṛtyai bhūbhṛtām lakṣmyai śarvāṇyai te namaḥ namaḥ |

The first line has a verb (कुर्मः ‘we make’) with नमः as its object and three datives; प्रणताम् qualifies वृद्ध्यै and सिद्ध्यै. The second line is verbless; ते sums up the three datives before it.

6Translation

LiteralTo the Auspicious One, to the prosperity and the success of those who bow, we make salutation, salutation. To Nairṛtī, to the fortune of kings, to Śarvāṇī — to you salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Auspicious One, who is the increase and the fulfilment of all who bow to her, we offer homage and homage. To Nairṛtī, to the royal fortune of kings, to Śarvāṇī — to you, homage and homage.

प्रणताम् is taken with वृद्ध्यै and सिद्ध्यै; it could also be taken with कल्याण्यै (‘to her who is the welfare of those who bow’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods name opposed aspects together. She is the welfare, increase and fulfilment of those who bow; she is also Nairṛtī, the power of Nirṛti, the deity of decay and misfortune and guardian of the south-west; she is the fortune of kings, and Śarvāṇī, wife of Śiva. With ते the hymn turns to address her directly.

Traditional interpretationOne traditional gloss takes नैर्ऋती as the fortune of the rākṣasas (Nirṛti being their lord), set against the fortune of kings in the same line: she is the prosperity of both. The orthography नैर्ऋत्यै is the Gita Press’s; GRETIL and Freund print नैरृत्यै, the same word.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 324, कल्याणी, and 124, शर्वाणी. Shared names, separate works.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: प्रणताम्: a short genitive plural (for प्रणतानाम्), taken with वृद्ध्यै and सिद्ध्यै; its exact derivation is uncertain (ārṣa shortening, or a root-noun प्रणत्). Variant (orthographic): नैर्ऋत्यै (Gita Press, kept) / नैरृत्यै (GRETIL, Freund).

5.12Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दुर्गायै दुर्गपारायै सारायै सर्वकारिण्यै।
ख्यात्यै तथैव कृष्णायै धूम्रायै सततं नमः ॥ १२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दुर्गायै | दुर्गपारायै | सारायै | सर्वकारिण्यैख्यात्यै | तथा | एव | कृष्णायै | धूम्रायै | सततम् | नमः

3IAST

As reciteddurgāyai durgapārāyai sārāyai sarvakāriṇyai |
khyātyai tathaiva kṛṣṇāyai dhūmrāyai satataṃ namaḥ ||

Separateddurgāyai | durgapārāyai | sārāyai | sarvakāriṇyai
khyātyai | tathā | eva | kṛṣṇāyai | dhūmrāyai | satatam | namaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दुर्गायैdurgāyaito Durgā, the Hard-to-Approachf. dat. sg. of दुर्गा (ā-stem)
दुर्गपारायैdurgapārāyaito her who is the far shore of difficultyf. dat. sg. of दुर्गपारा; compound (see Samāsa)
सारायैsārāyaito the Essencef. dat. sg. of सारा (ā-stem; from सार ‘essence, core’)
सर्वकारिण्यैsarvakāriṇyaito the Maker of allf. dat. sg. of सर्वकारिणी; compound (see Samāsa)
ख्यात्यैkhyātyaito Khyāti, renown (also ‘knowledge, discernment’)f. dat. sg. of ख्याति (i-stem)
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एवevaalso, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
कृष्णायैkṛṣṇāyaito the Dark Onef. dat. sg. of कृष्णा (ā-stem)
धूम्रायैdhūmrāyaito the Smoke-hued Onef. dat. sg. of धूम्रा (ā-stem)
सततम्satatamalwaysindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत)
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तथैव tathaiva → तथा + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai.
  • सततं नमः satataṃ namaḥ → सततम् + नमः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दुर्गपारायै durgapārāyai — bahuvrīhi
    Members: दुर्ग + पार · Vigraha: दुर्गस्य पारः यया (प्राप्यते) सा, तस्यै durgasya pāraḥ yayā (prāpyate) sā, tasyai
    Literally “she by whom the far side of what is hard to cross (is reached)”; in the verse: she who carries her devotees across every difficulty
    Alternative: Also read as a kṛt formation from the causative पारय- ‘to bring across’: दुर्गं पारयति इति दुर्गपारा, ‘she who brings (one) across difficulty’. The sense is the same.
  • सर्वकारिण्यै sarvakāriṇyai — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: सर्व + कारिन् · Vigraha: सर्वं करोति इति सर्वकारिणी, तस्यै sarvaṃ karoti iti sarvakāriṇī, tasyai
    Literally “to her who makes all”; in the verse: the Goddess as the agent of everything that is done

C. Morphology

  • सर्वकारिण्यै sarvakāriṇyai — कारिन् is √कृ with the kṛt affix ṇini (vṛddhi: कृ → कार्), ‘doer’; feminine कारिणी (n → ṇ after r in the same word).
  • दुर्गायै durgāyai — दुर्ग ‘hard to go to, a fortress, a difficulty’ (दुस् + √गम्); as a name, दुर्गा is the Goddess who is hard to approach. The next word explains the name by its other sense: she is the crossing of difficulty.

D. Anvaya — prose order

दुर्गायै दुर्गपारायै सारायै सर्वकारिण्यै ख्यात्यै तथा एव कृष्णायै धूम्रायै सततम् नमः।

durgāyai durgapārāyai sārāyai sarvakāriṇyai khyātyai tathā eva kṛṣṇāyai dhūmrāyai satatam namaḥ |

Eight datives, all governed by the one closing नमः.

6Translation

LiteralTo Durgā, to her who is the far shore of difficulty, to the Essence, to the Maker of all, to Renown, and likewise to the Dark One, to the Smoke-hued One, always salutation.

ReadableTo Durgā, who brings us across every hardship; to the Essence, the Maker of all; to Renown, to the Dark One, to the Smoke-hued: homage for ever.

‘Brings us across every hardship’ renders the compound दुर्गपारा; ‘us’ is supplied.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningDurgā is explained by the next word: दुर्ग is what is hard to pass, and she is its far shore. The hymn of the second story said the same (दुर्गासि दुर्गभवसागरनौरसङ्गा, 4.11), and the battle will call her दुर्गा दुर्गार्तिनाशिनी (9.31). The dark and smoky names (कृष्णा, धूम्रा) anticipate 5.88, where Pārvatī becomes dark (कृष्णाभूत्) and is called Kālikā.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 190, दुर्गा. The name is common to Śākta hymns; the two texts are distinct.

5.13Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायै नतास्तस्यै नमो नमः।
नमो जगत्प्रतिष्ठायै देव्यै कृत्यै नमो नमः ॥ १३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायै | नताः | तस्यै | नमः | नमःनमः | जगत्प्रतिष्ठायै | देव्यै | कृत्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedatisaumyātiraudrāyai natāstasyai namo namaḥ |
namo jagatpratiṣṭhāyai devyai kṛtyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedatisaumyātiraudrāyai | natāḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | namaḥ
namaḥ | jagatpratiṣṭhāyai | devyai | kṛtyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायैatisaumyātiraudrāyaito her who is most gentle and most fiercef. dat. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
नताःnatāḥ(we,) bowedpast participle (kta) of √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies the speakers (वयम्, understood)
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
जगत्प्रतिष्ठायैjagatpratiṣṭhāyaito the foundation of the worldf. dat. sg. of जगत्प्रतिष्ठा; compound (see Samāsa)
देव्यैdevyaito the Goddessf. dat. sg. of देवी
कृत्यैkṛtyaito Kṛti, (she who is) actionf. dat. sg. of कृति (i-stem; √कृ + ktin)
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायै atisaumyātiraudrāyai → अतिसौम्य + अतिरौद्रायै
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā: the two halves of the compound fuse.
  • नतास्तस्यै natāstasyai → नताः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o; so also नमो जगत्प्रतिष्ठायै.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायै atisaumyātiraudrāyai — karmadhāraya of two qualifiers, each a prādi tatpuruṣa with अति
    Members: अतिसौम्य + अतिरौद्र · Vigraha: अतिसौम्या च असौ अतिरौद्रा च, तस्यै (अतिसौम्या = अतिशयेन सौम्या; अतिरौद्रा = अतिशयेन रौद्रा) atisaumyā ca asau atiraudrā ca, tasyai (atisaumyā = atiśayena saumyā; atiraudrā = atiśayena raudrā)
    Literally “to her who is exceedingly gentle and exceedingly fierce”; in the verse: the Goddess in whom the extremes of grace and terror are one person
    Alternative: Not a dvandva: the two adjectives describe one and the same person, which is the karmadhāraya relation.
  • जगत्प्रतिष्ठायै jagatpratiṣṭhāyai — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: जगत् + प्रतिष्ठा · Vigraha: जगतः प्रतिष्ठा, तस्यै jagataḥ pratiṣṭhā, tasyai
    Literally “to the foundation of the world”; in the verse: she on whom the world rests
    Alternative: As a bahuvrīhi, जगति प्रतिष्ठा यस्याः, ‘she whose abode is in the world’; the tatpuruṣa fits the hymn’s praise of her as support (धात्री, 5.10).

C. Morphology

  • नताः natāḥ — Past participle of √नम् (the nasal drops before -ta). In 5.9 the gods were प्रणताः with the accusative ताम्; here they are नताः with the dative तस्यै, the case that नमः governs.
  • कृत्यै kṛtyai — Dative of the i-stem कृति ‘doing, action’ (√कृ + ktin), with the feminine ending -ai. Not from कृत्या, whose dative would be कृत्यायै.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अतिसौम्यातिरौद्रायै तस्यै नताः (वयम्) नमः नमः (कुर्मः)। जगत्प्रतिष्ठायै देव्यै कृत्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

atisaumyātiraudrāyai tasyai natāḥ (vayam) namaḥ namaḥ (kurmaḥ) | jagatpratiṣṭhāyai devyai kṛtyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The first line has the participle नताः for the speakers and the dative तस्यै with its long epithet; the second is a string of datives with नमः.

6Translation

LiteralBowed, (we say) salutation, salutation to her who is most gentle and most fierce. Salutation to the foundation of the world, to the Goddess, to Action: salutation, salutation.

ReadableBowing, we offer homage and homage to her who is at once most gentle and most terrible. Homage to the ground on which the world stands, to the Goddess, to Action itself: homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe verse states the paradox the earlier names had set side by side (रौद्रा and इन्दुरूपिणी, 5.10): she is both supremely gentle and supremely fierce. She is also the world’s foundation and action itself (कृति).

Traditional interpretationThe tradition reads the same pairing in the story that follows: the beautiful Ambikā who dazzles the asuras’ servants (5.89–92) and the terrible Kālī who springs from her brow in the battle (chapter 7) are one Goddess. Praise and narrative tell one thing twice.

5.14–16Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु विष्णुमायेति शब्दिता।
नमस्तस्यै। १४। नमस्तस्यै। १५। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ १६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | देवी | सर्वभूतेषु | विष्णुमाया | इति | शब्दितानमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedyā devī sarvabhūteṣu viṣṇumāyeti śabditā |
namastasyai | 14 | namastasyai | 15 | namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedyā | devī | sarvabhūteṣu | viṣṇumāyā | iti | śabditā
namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject of the relative clause
सर्वभूतेषुsarvabhūteṣuin all beingsn. loc. pl. of सर्वभूत; compound (see Samāsa)
विष्णुमायाviṣṇumāyāViṣṇumāyā, the Māyā of Viṣṇuf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the name, marked by इति
इतिitithus, as (marking the name)indeclinable (quotative particle)
शब्दिताśabditāis called, is namedpast participle (kta) of √शब्द् (curādi, 10 ‘to sound, to call’), f. nom. sg.; predicate of या देवी
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्; takes the dative (तस्यै); अस्तु ‘be it’ understood

नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; correlative of या
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विष्णुमायेति viṣṇumāyeti → विष्णुमाया + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the same in all three नमस्तस्यै.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every living being without exception
  • विष्णुमाया viṣṇumāyā — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विष्णु + माया · Vigraha: विष्णोः माया viṣṇoḥ māyā
    Literally “the māyā of Viṣṇu”; in the verse: the power belonging to Viṣṇu, the name under which the gods began to praise her (5.7)

C. Morphology

  • शब्दिता śabditā — Past participle of the curādi root √शब्द् (stem शब्दय-): the -ay- drops and the participle takes the connecting vowel i (शब्द् + इ + त). Used as a finite verb: ‘(is) called’.
  • तस्यै tasyai — Dative of the feminine pronoun तद्, the correlative of या: ‘to her (who …)’. नमः governs the dative of the one honoured; the verb अस्तु is understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु विष्णुमाया इति शब्दिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu viṣṇumāyā iti śabditā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … शब्दिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः. The pattern is the same in every yā devī stanza.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who in all beings is called ‘Viṣṇumāyā’ — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who in all beings is named Viṣṇu’s Māyā: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first of the yā devī stanzas, the most recited part of the hymn. Each names one form under which the Goddess is present in every being and answers it with a threefold salutation. The series opens with the name the gods used when they began (विष्णुमाया, 5.7), and its first verb, शब्दिता, is about naming: she is what she is called in all beings.

Traditional interpretationIn the Gita Press count each नमस्तस्यै is a separate mantra, so each yā devī stanza is three mantras; the chapter’s colophon counts 66 such mantras, 22 stanzas (5.14–76 and 5.78–80). The Goddess was first named Viṣṇu’s power in chapter 1, as the Yoganidrā that holds Viṣṇu and the Mahāmāyā of Hari (1.54–55).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same name, 339, विष्णुमाया. The shared title is part of a common Śākta vocabulary; the texts are separate.

5.17–19Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु चेतनेत्यभिधीयते।
नमस्तस्यै। १७। नमस्तस्यै। १८। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ १९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | देवी | सर्वभूतेषु | चेतना | इति | अभिधीयतेनमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedyā devī sarvabhūteṣu cetanetyabhidhīyate |
namastasyai | 17 | namastasyai | 18 | namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedyā | devī | sarvabhūteṣu | cetanā | iti | abhidhīyate
namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चेतनेत्यभिधीयते cetanetyabhidhīyate → चेतना + इति + अभिधीयते
    vowel sandhi (guṇa, then yaṇ) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + i → e (चेतनेति); then the i of इति before a becomes y (इत्यभि-).
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the same in all three नमस्तस्यै.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: as in 5.14-16

C. Morphology

  • अभिधीयते abhidhīyate — Passive present of अभि + √धा: before the passive -ya- the root’s ā becomes ī (धीयते). अभि-√धा ‘to name’, passive ‘is called’.
  • चेतना cetanā — From the causative stem of √चित् ‘to perceive’ (चेतय-) with -anā: ‘awareness, the state of being conscious’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु चेतना इति अभिधीयते, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu cetanā iti abhidhīyate, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who in all beings is called ‘consciousness’ — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who in all beings is called Consciousness: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second stanza moves from a title to what makes a being a being at all: awareness. Like the first it is a stanza of naming (अभिधीयते, ‘is called’); from the next stanza on the verb is संस्थिता and the formula …रूपेण, ‘abides in the form of’.

Traditional interpretationThe series ends where this stanza begins, with consciousness: in 5.78–80 she is the one who, in the form of citi (चितिरूपेण), pervades the whole world.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has 417, चेतनारूपा, ‘whose form is consciousness’, and 362, चितिः. The idea is shared; the works are distinct.

5.20–22Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। २०। नमस्तस्यै। २१। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ २२ ॥

या | देवी | सर्वभूतेषु | बुद्धिरूपेण | संस्थितानमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

बुद्धिरूपेणbuddhirūpeṇain the form of intelligence, as understandingn. instr. sg. of बुद्धिरूप; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner

नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्
नमःnamaḥsalutationindeclinable नमस्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā → सम् + स्थिता
    anusvāra (prefix before a consonant) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The m of the prefix सम् becomes anusvāra before स.
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the same in all three नमस्तस्यै.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: as in 5.14-16
  • बुद्धिरूपेण buddhirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: the first member is the form itself)
    Members: बुद्धि + रूप · Vigraha: बुद्धिः एव रूपम्, तेन buddhiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is intelligence”; in the verse: as intelligence in every creature
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, बुद्धेः रूपम्, ‘the form of …’; the karmadhāraya fits the series, where she is that state itself.

C. Morphology

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past participle of सम् + √स्था: before the participle -ta the root’s ā becomes i (स्थित). The m of सम् is written as anusvāra before स. The participle serves as the verb: ‘abides, is established’.
  • बुद्धिरूपेण buddhirūpeṇa — Instrumental of manner: ‘by way of the form of intelligence’, i.e. ‘as intelligence’. The n of the ending becomes ṇ after the r of रूप (internal retroflexion). The same construction runs through the following stanzas.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu buddhirūpeṇa saṃsthitā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of intelligence — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as Intelligence: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe formula that carries the rest of the series appears here: …रूपेण संस्थिता, ‘abides in the form of …’. The first such form is बुद्धि, the understanding that discerns and decides.

Traditional interpretationThe Devī Māhātmya returns to the line itself in the Nārāyaṇī hymn: सर्वस्य बुद्धिरूपेण जनस्य हृदि संस्थिते (11.8). Brahmā’s hymn had already called her बुद्धिः, ‘intelligence, whose mark is awakening’ (1.79).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the related name 223, महाबुद्धिः. The works are separate.

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the same in all three नमस्तस्यै.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: as in 5.14-16
  • निद्रारूपेण nidrārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: the first member is the form itself)
    Members: निद्रा + रूप · Vigraha: निद्रा एव रूपम्, तेन nidrā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is sleep”; in the verse: as sleep in every creature
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, निद्रायाः रूपम्, ‘the form of …’; the karmadhāraya fits the series, where she is that state itself.

C. Morphology

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — As in 5.20-22: past participle of सम् + √स्था, the root’s ā becoming i before -ta (स्थित); used as a finite verb, ‘abides’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु निद्रारूपेण संस्थिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu nidrārūpeṇa saṃsthitā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of sleep — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as Sleep: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter intelligence, sleep: the state in which understanding is withdrawn is also a form of the Goddess. The series does not praise only what is noble; it finds her in every condition of a living being.

Traditional interpretationThe name recalls the first story, where the Goddess is Viṣṇu’s Yoganidrā, the sleep that holds the god until she withdraws (1.54, 1.69–71). Here the same power is present as sleep in all beings.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: as in 5.14-16
  • क्षुधारूपेण kṣudhārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: the first member is the form itself)

    Literally “by the form that is hunger”; in the verse: as hunger in every creature
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, क्षुधायाः रूपम्, ‘the form of …’; the karmadhāraya fits the series, where she is that state itself.

C. Morphology

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — As in 5.20-22: past participle of सम् + √स्था, the root’s ā becoming i before -ta (स्थित); used as a finite verb, ‘abides’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु क्षुधारूपेण संस्थिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kṣudhārūpeṇa saṃsthitā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of hunger — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as Hunger: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningHunger, a want rather than a virtue, is named as her form. The series is deliberately inclusive: after intelligence and sleep come hunger, shadow and power, and later thirst (5.35–37) and even error (5.74–76). In chapter 1 hunger already belonged to Mahāmāyā’s work: birds, though tormented by hunger (पीड्यमानानपि क्षुधा), feed their young out of delusion (1.51–52).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: to bow to the Goddess as hunger is to refuse to divide life into a sacred part and a merely bodily part. The need that drives every creature is itself where she is met.

5.29–31Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

या देवी सर्वभूतेषुच्छायारूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। २९। नमस्तस्यै। ३०। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ३१ ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वभूतेषुच्छायारूपेण sarvabhūteṣucchāyārūpeṇa → सर्वभूतेषु + छायारूपेण
    consonant sandhi (ch doubled after a short vowel) · छे च (6.1.73)
    After a short vowel an initial ch takes the augment t, which becomes c next to ch (स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः, 8.4.40): -u + ch- → -uccha-. After a word-final short vowel the doubling is obligatory (पदान्ताद्वा, 6.1.76, makes it optional only after a long vowel). The Gita Press therefore prints the two words together — the only yā devī stanza where सर्वभूतेषु is joined to the next word.
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s; the same in all three नमस्तस्यै.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि च तानि भूतानि च, तेषु sarvāṇi ca tāni bhūtāni ca, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: as in 5.14-16
  • छायारूपेण chāyārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa: the first member is the form itself)
    Members: छाया + रूप · Vigraha: छाया एव रूपम्, तेन chāyā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is shadow”; in the verse: as shadow in every creature
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, छायायाः रूपम्, ‘the form of …’; the karmadhāraya fits the series, where she is that state itself.

C. Morphology

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — As in 5.20-22: past participle of सम् + √स्था, the root’s ā becoming i before -ta (स्थित); used as a finite verb, ‘abides’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु छायारूपेण संस्थिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu chāyārūpeṇa saṃsthitā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of shadow (shade) — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as Shadow: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningछाया is shadow and shade: the cool shelter from heat and the dark likeness that follows every body; the word can also mean a reflection. The text does not say which sense is meant, and the stanza names the form without explaining it.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the shadow is what a being cannot leave behind. To find the Goddess there is to find her in what accompanies a person unnoticed, as well as in what is seen.

5.32–34Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शक्तिरूपेण संस्थिता।

    Members: शक्ति + रूप · Vigraha: शक्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन śaktiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is power”; in the verse: as power in every creature
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, शक्तेः रूपम्, ‘the form of …’; the karmadhāraya fits the series, where she is that state itself.

C. Morphology

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — As in 5.20-22: past participle of सम् + √स्था, the root’s ā becoming i before -ta (स्थित); used as a finite verb, ‘abides’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शक्तिरूपेण संस्थिता, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः नमः (अस्तु)।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śaktirūpeṇa saṃsthitā, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ namaḥ (astu) |

A relative clause (या देवी … संस्थिता) and its correlative in the dative (तस्यै), governed by the repeated नमः; the same frame as 5.14-16.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of power — to her salutation, to her salutation, to her salutation, salutation.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as Power: homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚakti is the power or capacity by which anything acts at all. It is also the word the Śākta tradition uses for the Goddess herself, so this stanza names her by her most general name: she is the power in every being.

Traditional interpretationBrahmā’s hymn in chapter 1 said the same: ‘whatever is the power of all that exists, that is you’ (तस्य सर्वस्य या शक्तिः सा त्वम्, 1.83). The Nārāyaṇī hymn calls her वैष्णवी शक्तिः (11.5).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the related names 217, महाशक्तिः, and 572, पराशक्तिः. The works are separate.
