नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; the salutation repeated (third mantra of the three)
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; dative with नमः
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; with the next word a doubled salutation
नमःnamaḥhomage (again)n. nom. sg. of नमस्; the repetition (नमो नमः) expresses intensity: ‘homage, homage’, i.e. again and again

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā → सम् + स्थिता
    anusvāra sandhi at the verbal prefix · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The final m of the prefix सम् (a pada for sandhi purposes) becomes anusvāra before the consonant s of स्थिता. The prefix stays on its participle in the padaccheda.
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. The pāṭha sets a number after each नमस्तस्यै: the first two salutations are mantras of their own (here 35 and 36).
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o: the s of नमस् turns to ru, ru to u, and a + u gives o. The last नमः stands at the pause and keeps its visarga (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (the pronominal adjective सर्व qualifying भूत)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature — भूत is ‘what has come to be’, a living being
  • तृष्णारूपेण tṛṣṇārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: तृष्णा + रूप · Vigraha: तृष्णा एव रूपम्, तेन tṛṣṇā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is thirst”; in the verse: as thirst, in the shape of thirst
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, तृष्णायाः रूपम् ‘the form of thirst’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to thirst but to be present as thirst itself.

C. Morphology

  • तृष्णारूपेण tṛṣṇārūpeṇa — तृष्णा is a feminine noun from √तृष् ‘to be thirsty’ (divādi, 4P, तृष्यति) with the suffix -na and feminine -ā: bodily thirst first, and by extension craving, longing for what one lacks.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था: the root’s ā becomes i before the -त of the participle (स्थित). With अस्ति understood it is the finite verb of the relative clause, ‘(who) abides, stands established’; feminine to agree with या देवी.
  • रूपेण rūpeṇa — Instrumental singular of रूप in -एन; the n becomes ṇ because of the r earlier in the same word (internal retroflexion, which carries across vowels and labials). It is the instrumental of manner: ‘in the form of’.
  • नमः namaḥ — Neuter s-stem नमस् ‘bowing, homage’, nominative singular. The one honoured stands in the dative (तस्यै); the verb (अस्तु) is not expressed. Each of the first two नमस्तस्यै is counted as a mantra of its own; the third, with नमो नमः, closes the three-part mantra.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु तृष्णारूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu tṛṣṇārūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

A relative clause (या … संस्थिता) with its correlative तस्यै; each नमः takes the dative. The second line is three mantras in the pāṭha: नमस्तस्यै, नमस्तस्यै, and नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of thirst — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as thirst: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods name the Goddess as thirst (तृष्णा) present in every creature. In the hymn’s sequence it follows hunger (क्षुधा, 5.26-28): the two basic drives of an embodied being.

Traditional interpretationThirst in its wider sense, craving, is usually counted among the things that bind. The hymn does not exempt it. Medhas has already taught that Mahāmāyā herself draws even the minds of the wise into delusion (1.55–56) and is also the cause of release (1.57–58); in the Śākta reading the same Goddess rules what binds and what frees.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: to bow to the Goddess as thirst is not to praise craving. It is to see that even longing points beyond itself, and that the force which drives a creature toward what it lacks is not outside the divine.

5.38–40Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु क्षान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ३८। नमस्तस्यै। ३९। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ४० ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • क्षान्तिरूपेण kṣāntirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: क्षान्ति + रूप · Vigraha: क्षान्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन kṣāntiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is forbearance”; in the verse: as forbearance, in the shape of forbearance
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, क्षान्तेः रूपम् ‘the form of forbearance’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to forbearance but to be present as forbearance itself.

C. Morphology

  • क्षान्तिरूपेण kṣāntirūpeṇa — क्षान्ति is an action noun from √क्षम् ‘to endure, bear patiently’ (bhvādi 1Ā क्षमते; also divādi 4P क्षाम्यति) with the suffix -ति (क्तिन्); a root ending in a nasal lengthens its vowel before this suffix (क्षम् → क्षान्ति), as in शान्ति and भ्रान्ति later in the hymn.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु क्षान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kṣāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of forbearance — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as forbearance: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as forbearance (क्षान्ति): the patience that bears injury without returning it.

Traditional interpretationBrahmā’s hymn in the first carita already calls her forbearance, in the same breath as modesty, contentment and peace (1.80: लज्जा पुष्टिस्तथा तुष्टिस्त्वं शान्तिः क्षान्तिरेव च). Several of those names return in this hymn as yā devī mantras (5.44, 5.47, 5.68).

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the gods praise forbearance while they are themselves dispossessed and waiting (5.2–7). Patience here is not passivity; it is the strength that holds out until help comes.

5.41–43Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु जातिरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ४१। नमस्तस्यै। ४२। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ४३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | देवी | सर्वभूतेषु | जातिरूपेण | संस्थितानमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • जातिरूपेण jātirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: जाति + रूप · Vigraha: जातिः एव रूपम्, तेन jātiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is birth”; in the verse: as birth, in the shape of birth
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, जातेः रूपम् ‘the form of birth’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to birth but to be present as birth itself.

C. Morphology

  • जातिरूपेण jātirūpeṇa — जाति is an action noun from √जन् ‘to be born’ (divādi, 4Ā, जायते) with -ति (क्तिन्); before this suffix the root जन् takes the form जा. Its meanings run from ‘birth’ to what birth confers: lineage, kind, species, class.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु जातिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu jātirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of birth — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as birth (and the kind each creature is born into): salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as जाति: birth, and the kind a being is born into. The word covers both the event (being born) and its result (belonging to a species, a lineage).

Traditional interpretationTwo readings are current. One takes जाति as birth, the power by which beings come into existence; the other as the ‘kind’ that makes each creature what it is — in the philosophers’ language, the universal shared by all members of a class. Since the hymn speaks of ‘all beings’, the wider senses fit better than the later narrow sense of a social group.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: whatever one is born as, the hymn places the Goddess in that very fact of birth. No kind of being is outside her.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: जाति can mean ‘birth’ or ‘kind, class’; the edition gives both and prefers neither strongly.

    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • लज्जारूपेण lajjārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: लज्जा + रूप · Vigraha: लज्जा एव रूपम्, तेन lajjā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is modesty”; in the verse: as modesty, in the shape of modesty
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, लज्जायाः रूपम् ‘the form of modesty’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to modesty but to be present as modesty itself.

C. Morphology

  • लज्जारूपेण lajjārūpeṇa — लज्जा is a feminine action noun in -ā from √लस्ज् ‘to be ashamed’ (tudādi, 6Ā, लज्जते): the sense of shame that restrains, hence modesty, propriety.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु लज्जारूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu lajjārūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of modesty — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as modesty: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as modesty (लज्जा): the inner restraint that keeps a person from what is unworthy.

Traditional interpretationThe gods’ hymn in chapter 4 had already said she is ‘modesty in one born of a good family’ (4.5: लज्जा कुलजनप्रभवस्य), and Brahmā names her लज्जा in 1.80. Modesty is praised as a power, not a weakness: it guards conduct.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same word as a name: 740, लज्जा. The texts are separate; the shared name shows a shared Śākta idiom.

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता।

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • शान्तिरूपेण śāntirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: शान्ति + रूप · Vigraha: शान्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन śāntiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is peace”; in the verse: as peace, in the shape of peace
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, शान्तेः रूपम् ‘the form of peace’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to peace but to be present as peace itself.

C. Morphology

  • शान्तिरूपेण śāntirūpeṇa — शान्ति is an action noun from √शम् ‘to be calm, cease’ (divādi, 4P, शाम्यति) with -ति (क्तिन्); the nasal-final root lengthens its vowel (शम् → शान्ति), as क्षम् → क्षान्ति in 5.38-40.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of peace — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as peace: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as peace (शान्ति): the stilling of agitation in every creature.

Traditional interpretationशान्ति is also the name of the rites that avert harm; at the end of the text the Goddess herself commends the hearing of her glory in all such rites (12.16: शान्तिकर्मणि सर्वत्र). The peace praised here and the peace sought in ritual are one word.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has शान्तिः as name 447, in a run of such qualities (443 तुष्टिः, 444 पुष्टिः, 445 मतिः, 446 धृतिः). Brahmā’s hymn here names her शान्तिः too (1.80).

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • श्रद्धारूपेण śraddhārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: श्रद्धा + रूप · Vigraha: श्रद्धा एव रूपम्, तेन śraddhā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is faith”; in the verse: as faith, in the shape of faith
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, श्रद्धायाः रूपम् ‘the form of faith’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to faith but to be present as faith itself.

C. Morphology

  • श्रद्धारूपेण śraddhārūpeṇa — श्रद्धा is formed from श्रत् ‘trust, truth’ (treated like a verbal prefix) + √धा ‘to place’ (juhotyādi, 3) with the feminine suffix -ā: ‘placing (one’s heart) in what is true’. The t of श्रत् becomes d before dh (श्रद्धा).
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु श्रद्धारूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śraddhārūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of faith — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as faith: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as faith (श्रद्धा): the trust that sets the heart on what it holds true.

Traditional interpretationThe gods said in chapter 4 that she is ‘faith in the good’ (4.5: श्रद्धा सतां). In the wider Hindu tradition faith shapes the person: the Bhagavad Gītā (17.3) says ‘a person is made of faith; as one’s faith is, so is one’ (श्रद्धामयोऽयं पुरुषो यो यच्छ्रद्धः स एव सः).

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: on this reading faith is not only something a person has; it is a presence of the Goddess in the person. Trust itself becomes a place of meeting.

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • कान्तिरूपेण kāntirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: कान्ति + रूप · Vigraha: कान्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन kāntiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is loveliness”; in the verse: as loveliness, in the shape of loveliness
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, कान्तेः रूपम् ‘the form of loveliness’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to loveliness but to be present as loveliness itself.

C. Morphology

  • कान्तिरूपेण kāntirūpeṇa — कान्ति is an action noun with -ति (क्तिन्), usually derived from √कम् ‘to desire, love’ (bhvādi, 1Ā): ‘what is desired’, hence loveliness, charm; the vowel lengthens as in शान्ति. A derivation from √कन् ‘to shine’ is also given, which brings out the sense ‘radiance’.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु कान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of loveliness — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as loveliness: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as loveliness, the radiance (कान्ति) by which anything is beautiful.

Traditional interpretationBeauty is here a presence of the Goddess, not a lure away from her. The next part of the story turns on exactly this: Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa see her beauty (5.89–92) and tell Śumbha, who wants to possess what he can only worship.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has कान्तिः as name 449, in the same run as शान्तिः (447).

5.56–58Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु लक्ष्मीरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ५६। नमस्तस्यै। ५७। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ५८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | देवी | सर्वभूतेषु | लक्ष्मीरूपेण | संस्थितानमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedyā devī sarvabhūteṣu lakṣmīrūpeṇa saṃsthitā |
namastasyai | 56 | namastasyai | 57 | namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedyā | devī | sarvabhūteṣu | lakṣmīrūpeṇa | saṃsthitā
namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • लक्ष्मीरूपेण lakṣmīrūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: लक्ष्मी + रूप · Vigraha: लक्ष्मीः एव रूपम्, तेन lakṣmīḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is fortune”; in the verse: as Lakṣmī, as fortune and prosperity
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, लक्ष्म्याः रूपम् ‘the form of Lakṣmī’ — the goddess Lakṣmī as one form of the Devī. The karmadhāraya keeps the pattern of the hymn (‘in the form that is fortune’); both readings agree that Lakṣmī is the Devī herself.

C. Morphology

  • लक्ष्मीरूपेण lakṣmīrūpeṇa — लक्ष्मी is traditionally derived (uṇādi) from √लक्ष् ‘to mark’: the auspicious mark, good fortune. It is one of the few feminine ī-stems that keep the nominative -s (लक्ष्मीः, not *लक्ष्मी); inside the compound the bare stem stands.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु लक्ष्मीरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu lakṣmīrūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of fortune — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as fortune: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as Lakṣmī, fortune and prosperity, present in all beings. Prosperity is not a separate goddess’s gift here but one of her own forms.

Traditional interpretationIn chapter 4 the gods said she is ‘Śrī herself in the homes of the virtuous, and misfortune (अलक्ष्मी) in those of the wicked’ (4.5). In the Gita Press pāṭha the middle carita is assigned to Mahālakṣmī; that assignment is liturgical tradition, not a statement of this verse.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has महालक्ष्मीः as name 210. The two texts remain separate works.

5.59–61Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु वृत्तिरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ५९। नमस्तस्यै। ६०। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ६१ ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • वृत्तिरूपेण vṛttirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: वृत्ति + रूप · Vigraha: वृत्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन vṛttiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is activity”; in the verse: as activity, in the shape of activity
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, वृत्तेः रूपम् ‘the form of activity’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to activity but to be present as activity itself.

C. Morphology

  • वृत्तिरूपेण vṛttirūpeṇa — वृत्ति is an action noun from √वृत् ‘to turn, to be, to go on’ (bhvādi, 1Ā, वर्तते) with -ति (क्तिन्): a turning, a mode of being; hence conduct, occupation, livelihood — and, in the language of Yoga, a movement of the mind.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु वृत्तिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu vṛttirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of activity — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as the activity by which each lives: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as वृत्ति: the activity by which every being goes on living — its way of life, its means of sustenance.

Traditional interpretationTwo senses are current. The common one is livelihood: what supports each creature’s life. Another takes वृत्ति as the movements of the mind (the Yoga term चित्तवृत्ति), so that every mental state is her form. The hymn’s neighbours (memory, faith, delusion) allow the second; the plain sense of ‘all beings’ favours the first.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: on either reading, ordinary work and ordinary thought are not outside the sacred. The means by which one lives is named as a form of the Goddess.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: वृत्ति may be ‘livelihood, way of life’ or ‘a movement of the mind’; the edition prefers the first and notes the second.

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै

    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • स्मृतिरूपेण smṛtirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: स्मृति + रूप · Vigraha: स्मृतिः एव रूपम्, तेन smṛtiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is memory”; in the verse: as memory, in the shape of memory
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, स्मृतेः रूपम् ‘the form of memory’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to memory but to be present as memory itself.

C. Morphology

  • स्मृतिरूपेण smṛtirūpeṇa — स्मृति is an action noun from √स्मृ ‘to remember’ (bhvādi, 1P, स्मरति) with -ति (क्तिन्): memory, remembrance; also, as a term, the remembered tradition (the Smṛti texts).
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु स्मृतिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu smṛtirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of memory — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as memory: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as memory (स्मृति) in every creature.

Traditional interpretationRemembering her is the act this whole episode turns on: she had promised to destroy the gods’ calamities whenever they remembered her (5.6), and the gods’ hymn in chapter 4 says ‘remembered, you take away fear’ (4.17: दुर्गे स्मृता हरसि भीतिम्). Brahmā’s hymn calls her महास्मृतिः, the great memory (1.77). Here the power of remembering is itself her form.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has स्मृतिः as name 540.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the faculty by which one turns to the divine is already the divine at work. Memory is not only a store of the past but a way back.

  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • दयारूपेण dayārūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: दया + रूप · Vigraha: दया एव रूपम्, तेन dayā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is compassion”; in the verse: as compassion, in the shape of compassion
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, दयायाः रूपम् ‘the form of compassion’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to compassion but to be present as compassion itself.

C. Morphology

  • दयारूपेण dayārūpeṇa — दया is a feminine noun in -ā from √दय् (bhvādi, 1Ā, दयते ‘to pity, to share out’): compassion, the impulse to relieve another’s suffering.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु दयारूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu dayārūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of compassion — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as compassion: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as compassion (दया), present in every creature that pities another.

Traditional interpretationThe gods’ hymn in chapter 4 says she sends her weapons against enemies so that, purified by them, even they may reach the higher worlds (4.19), and concludes: ‘thus is compassion shown by you even to enemies’ (4.21: वैरिष्वपि प्रकटितैव दया त्वयेत्थम्). Compassion in the heart and ruthlessness in battle are found together in her alone (4.22).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has दयामूर्तिः ‘embodiment of compassion’ as name 581 — a compound rather than the bare word, but the same idea.

5.68–70Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • तुष्टिरूपेण tuṣṭirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: तुष्टि + रूप · Vigraha: तुष्टिः एव रूपम्, तेन tuṣṭiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is contentment”; in the verse: as contentment, in the shape of contentment
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, तुष्टेः रूपम् ‘the form of contentment’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to contentment but to be present as contentment itself.

C. Morphology

  • तुष्टिरूपेण tuṣṭirūpeṇa — तुष्टि is an action noun from √तुष् ‘to be pleased, satisfied’ (divādi, 4P, तुष्यति) with -ति (क्तिन्); the ṣ of the root turns the t of the suffix to ṭ (ष्टुना ष्टुः, 8.4.41).
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु तुष्टिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu tuṣṭirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of contentment — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as contentment: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as contentment (तुष्टि): the satisfaction that rests in what one has.

Traditional interpretationContentment answers the thirst of 5.35-37: the hymn names her both as the craving that drives a creature and as the satisfaction that stills it. Brahmā’s hymn already named her तुष्टिः (1.80).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has तुष्टिः as name 443, the first of a run of such qualities (443–449).

5.71–73Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु मातृरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ७१। नमस्तस्यै। ७२। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ७३ ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • मातृरूपेण mātṛrūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: मातृ + रूप · Vigraha: माता एव रूपम्, तेन mātā eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is the mother”; in the verse: as the mother, as motherhood
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, मातुः रूपम् ‘the form of the mother’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to the mother but to be present as the mother itself.

C. Morphology

  • मातृरूपेण mātṛrūpeṇa — मातृ is an ṛ-stem noun of relationship (nom. sg. माता, gen. मातुः), traditionally connected with √मा ‘to measure, to form’. In a compound the bare stem मातृ stands; the vigraha uses the nominative माता.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु मातृरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu mātṛrūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of the mother — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as mother: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘again and again’ renders the doubled नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is named as the mother present in all beings: motherhood itself, in every creature that bears and nourishes young.

Traditional interpretationThe word is singular here: not a group of goddesses but the motherhood in each being. In chapter 8 the plural Mātṛs, the Mothers, will come forth from the gods’ own powers to fight beside her (8.12–14); the tradition sees them as her emanations.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) opens with श्रीमाता (name 1) and has माता as name 457. The two texts remain separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the care of any mother for any child is named here as a form of the Goddess, not merely a picture of her.

5.74–76Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु भ्रान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता।
नमस्तस्यै। ७४। नमस्तस्यै। ७५। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ७६ ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s, as in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूतेषु sarvabhūteṣu — karmadhāraya (as in 5.35-37)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वाणि भूतानि, तेषु sarvāṇi bhūtāni, teṣu
    Literally “in all beings”; in the verse: in every creature
  • भ्रान्तिरूपेण bhrāntirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada: the first member identifies the form)
    Members: भ्रान्ति + रूप · Vigraha: भ्रान्तिः एव रूपम्, तेन bhrāntiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is error”; in the verse: as error, in the shape of error
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, भ्रान्तेः रूपम् ‘the form of error’. The karmadhāraya fits better: the Goddess is not said to own a form belonging to error but to be present as error itself.

C. Morphology

  • भ्रान्तिरूपेण bhrāntirūpeṇa — भ्रान्ति is an action noun from √भ्रम् ‘to wander, whirl’ (divādi 4P भ्राम्यति; also bhvādi 1P भ्रमति) with -ति (क्तिन्), the vowel lengthened as in शान्ति: a wandering, hence confusion, error, delusion.
  • संस्थिता saṃsthitā — Past passive participle of सम् + √स्था, as in 5.35-37: ‘(who) abides’, with अस्ति understood.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु भ्रान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu bhrāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The relative clause (या … संस्थिता) and its correlative (तस्यै), as in 5.35-37.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess who abides in all beings in the form of error — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who dwells in all beings as delusion: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

‘As’ renders the instrumental -रूपेण ‘in the form of’; ‘delusion’ is chosen for भ्रान्ति (lit. ‘wandering, error’) because of the link with Mahāmāyā’s मोह in 1.55–56; ‘again and again’ renders नमो नमः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last name of the yā devī series is error, delusion (भ्रान्ति): the confusion that takes one thing for another.

Traditional interpretationThe series ends on its hardest case. Medhas taught that this Goddess, as Mahāmāyā, forcibly draws even the minds of the wise into delusion (1.55–56: ज्ञानिनामपि चेतांसि … बलादाकृष्य मोहाय महामाया प्रयच्छति) and is also the cause of release (1.57–58). To bow to her as delusion is to acknowledge that the power that confuses is hers, and so is the power to free.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: error is not praised; it is placed under the same sovereignty as knowledge. That is the ground for hoping that error can be undone.

5.77Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इन्द्रियाणामधिष्ठात्री भूतानां चाखिलेषु या।
भूतेषु सततं तस्यै व्याप्तिदेव्यै नमो नमः ॥ ७७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इन्द्रियाणाम् | अधिष्ठात्री | भूतानाम् | च | अखिलेषु | याभूतेषु | सततम् | तस्यै | व्याप्तिदेव्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedindriyāṇāmadhiṣṭhātrī bhūtānāṃ cākhileṣu yā |
bhūteṣu satataṃ tasyai vyāptidevyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedindriyāṇām | adhiṣṭhātrī | bhūtānām | ca | akhileṣu | yā
bhūteṣu | satatam | tasyai | vyāptidevyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
इन्द्रियाणाम्indriyāṇāmof the sensesn. gen. pl. of इन्द्रिय; governed by अधिष्ठात्री
अधिष्ठात्रीadhiṣṭhātrīthe presiding one, rulerf. nom. sg. of अधिष्ठात्री, feminine (ङीप्) of the agent noun अधिष्ठातृ (अधि + √स्था + तृच्); predicate of या; governs the genitive
भूतानाम्bhūtānāmof the elements (or: of beings)n. gen. pl. of भूत; coordinated with इन्द्रियाणाम् by च
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle)
अखिलेषुakhileṣuin all, in the whole ofn. loc. pl. of अखिल (nañ-bahuvrīhi, ‘without a gap’); qualifies भूतेषु
याyāwhorelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative with तस्यै
भूतेषुbhūteṣuin beingsn. loc. pl. of भूत
सततम्satatamalways, continuallyindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत ‘continuous’)

व्याप्तिदेव्यैvyāptidevyaito the Goddess who is pervasion, the all-pervading Goddessf. dat. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to तस्यै
नमःnamaḥhomage (be)n. nom. sg. of नमस्; अस्तु understood; with the dative
नमःnamaḥhomage (again)n. nom. sg. of नमस्; doubled for intensity

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भूतानां चाखिलेषु bhūtānāṃ cākhileṣu → भूतानाम् + च + अखिलेषु
    anusvāra and vowel sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Final m becomes anusvāra before c; then a + a → ā (च + अखिलेषु → चाखिलेषु).
  • सततं तस्यै satataṃ tasyai → सततम् + तस्यै
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before the consonant t is written as anusvāra.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o, as in the yā devī refrain.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • व्याप्तिदेव्यै vyāptidevyai — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada)
    Members: व्याप्ति + देवी · Vigraha: व्याप्तिः एव देवी, तस्यै vyāptiḥ eva devī, tasyai
    Literally “to the Goddess who is pervasion”; in the verse: to the all-pervading Goddess — the name sums up the verse: she presides over the senses and is ever in all beings
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, व्याप्तेः देवी ‘the goddess of pervasion’. The karmadhāraya matches the pattern of the hymn, where each quality is her form.
  • अखिलेषु akhileṣu — nañ-bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ (नञ्) + खिल · Vigraha: न विद्यते खिलं यस्य तत्, तेषु na vidyate khilaṃ yasya tat, teṣu
    Literally “in those without a gap (remainder)”; in the verse: in all, without exception

C. Morphology

  • अधिष्ठात्री adhiṣṭhātrī — Agent noun in -तृ (तृच्) from अधि + √स्था ‘to stand over, preside’, made feminine with -ई (ङीप्): अधिष्ठातृ → अधिष्ठात्री. The s of the root turns to ṣ after the i of the prefix, and the following th becomes ṭh by ष्टुना ष्टुः (8.4.41): अधिष्ठा-. Like other agent nouns it takes the genitive of what is ruled (इन्द्रियाणाम्, भूतानाम्).
  • व्याप्तिदेव्यै vyāptidevyai — व्याप्ति is an action noun from वि + √आप् ‘to reach, pervade’ (svādi, 5P) with -ति (क्तिन्); i of वि becomes y before ā (इको यणचि, 6.1.77). देव्यै is the dative singular of the ī-stem देवी.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या इन्द्रियाणाम् भूतानाम् च अधिष्ठात्री, अखिलेषु भूतेषु सततम् (व्याप्य स्थिता), तस्यै व्याप्तिदेव्यै नमः नमः।

yā indriyāṇām bhūtānām ca adhiṣṭhātrī, akhileṣu bhūteṣu satatam (vyāpya sthitā), tasyai vyāptidevyai namaḥ namaḥ |

A relative clause (या … अधिष्ठात्री, … भूतेषु सततम्) with the correlative तस्यै व्याप्तिदेव्यै; the verb of being or pervading is understood and is supplied by the name व्याप्ति. भूत occurs twice: first (genitive, with इन्द्रियाणाम्) best as ‘the elements’, then (locative) as ‘beings’. Another construal takes भूतानाम् with इन्द्रियाणाम् — ‘ruler of the senses of beings’ — and च as joining the second clause.

6Translation

LiteralShe who is the presiding power of the senses and of the elements, and who is always in all beings — to her, the Goddess who is pervasion, homage, homage.

ReadableTo the Goddess who rules the senses and the elements, and who ever pervades all beings — to her, the all-pervading Goddess, salutation again and again.

‘Pervades’ is supplied from the name व्याप्तिदेवी; the Sanskrit has no verb in the second clause.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter the twenty-one yā devī mantras, a full two-line śloka sums them up: the Goddess presides over the senses and the elements and is constantly present in every being. Her name here is व्याप्तिदेवी, the pervading Goddess.

Traditional interpretationIn the Gita Press count this is a single mantra (a śloka), not a three-part one, though it ends with the same नमो नमः. The pairing of senses (इन्द्रिय) and elements (भूत) recalls the Sāṃkhya list of principles from which body and world are made; the verse places a single presiding power over both.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has व्यापिनी ‘the pervading one’ as name 400 — the same idea in a different form of the word.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: भूतानाम् may be ‘of the elements’ (coordinated with इन्द्रियाणाम्) or ‘of beings’ (‘the senses of beings’); the edition prefers the first because of च.

5.78–80Three-part mantraanuṣṭubh line + refrain

1Original śloka

चितिरूपेण या कृत्स्नमेतद् व्याप्य स्थिता जगत् ।
नमस्तस्यै। ७८। नमस्तस्यै। ७९। नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥ ८० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

चितिरूपेण | या | कृत्स्नम् | एतत् | व्याप्य | स्थिता | जगत्नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | तस्यै | नमः | नमः

3IAST

As recitedcitirūpeṇa yā kṛtsnametad vyāpya sthitā jagat |
namastasyai | 78 | namastasyai | 79 | namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

Separatedcitirūpeṇa | yā | kṛtsnam | etat | vyāpya | sthitā | jagat
namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | tasyai | namaḥ | namaḥ

4Word by word

चितिरूपेणcitirūpeṇain the form of consciousnessn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
याyāwhorelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative with तस्यै
कृत्स्नम्kṛtsnamentire, wholen. acc. sg. of कृत्स्न; qualifies जगत्
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. acc. sg.; qualifies जगत्
व्याप्यvyāpyahaving pervaded, pervadingabsolutive (ल्यप्) of वि + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to reach’)

नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; the salutation repeated (second mantra of the three)
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; dative with नमः
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; the salutation repeated (third mantra of the three)
तस्यैtasyaito herpronoun तद्, f. dat. sg.; dative with नमः
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; with the next word a doubled salutation
नमःnamaḥhomage (again)n. nom. sg. of नमस्; the repetition (नमो नमः) expresses intensity: ‘homage, homage’, i.e. again and again

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एतद् व्याप्य etad vyāpya → एतत् + व्याप्य
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced v; the Gita Press writes the d with a virāma and a space.
  • नमस्तस्यै namastasyai → नमः + तस्यै
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    As in 5.35-37.
  • नमो नमः namo namaḥ → नमः + नमः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    As in 5.35-37.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • चितिरूपेण citirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa-pūrvapada)
    Members: चिति + रूप · Vigraha: चितिः एव रूपम्, तेन citiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is consciousness”; in the verse: as consciousness itself
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, चितेः रूपम् ‘the form of consciousness’; the karmadhāraya fits the hymn’s pattern.

C. Morphology

  • व्याप्य vyāpya — Absolutive of वि + √आप्: with a prefix the absolutive takes -य (ल्यप्) instead of -त्वा. The i of वि becomes y before ā (व्याप्य). ‘Having pervaded’ — here simply ‘pervading’, since the pervading and the abiding are not successive.
  • स्थिता sthitā — Past passive participle of √स्था (ā → i before -त), as संस्थिता in the yā devī mantras but without the prefix: ‘(who) stands, abides’, with अस्ति understood.
  • चितिरूपेण citirūpeṇa — चिति is an action noun from √चित् ‘to perceive, be aware’ (bhvādi, 1P, चेतति) with -ति (क्तिन्): consciousness, awareness.

D. Anvaya — prose order

या चितिरूपेण एतत् कृत्स्नम् जगत् व्याप्य स्थिता (अस्ति), तस्यै नमः (अस्तु), तस्यै नमः, तस्यै नमः, नमः नमः।

yā citirūpeṇa etat kṛtsnam jagat vyāpya sthitā (asti), tasyai namaḥ (astu), tasyai namaḥ, tasyai namaḥ, namaḥ namaḥ |

The yā devī form returns, but the relative clause is new: या … व्याप्य स्थिता, with जगत् as the object of the absolutive. The second line is the familiar three mantras (78, 79, 80).

6Translation

LiteralShe who, in the form of consciousness, abides pervading this entire world — homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

ReadableTo her who, as consciousness, pervades this whole world and abides in it: salutation to her, salutation to her, salutation to her — salutation again and again.

The absolutive व्याप्य ‘having pervaded’ is rendered as a present (‘pervades’), since the pervading and the abiding are simultaneous.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last three-part mantra widens the view from ‘all beings’ to ‘this whole world’ and names her most basic form: consciousness (चिति), pervading everything.

Traditional interpretationWith this mantra the three-part mantras of the chapter reach twenty-two — twenty-one yā devī mantras and this one — which the colophon counts as sixty-six mantras. The gods’ hymn in chapter 4 opened on the same thought: ‘the Goddess by whose own power this world is spread out’ (4.3: देव्या यया ततमिदं जगदात्मशक्त्या).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has चितिः as name 362. The idea that the Goddess is consciousness itself is shared across Śākta texts; the two works remain distinct.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: after naming her in hunger, thirst, faith and error, the hymn names what all of them have in common — awareness. Every state named before is a state of consciousness.

5.81Ślokavaṃśastha

1Original śloka

स्तुता सुरैः पूर्वमभीष्टसंश्रया-
त्तथा सुरेन्द्रेण दिनेषु सेविता।
करोतु सा नः शुभहेतुरीश्वरी
शुभानि भद्राण्यभिहन्तु चापदः ॥ ८१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

स्तुता | सुरैः | पूर्वम् | अभीष्टसंश्रयात्तथा | सुरेन्द्रेण | दिनेषु | सेविताकरोतु | सा | नः | शुभहेतुः | ईश्वरीशुभानि | भद्राणि | अभिहन्तु | च | आपदः

3IAST

As recitedstutā suraiḥ pūrvamabhīṣṭasaṃśrayā-
ttathā surendreṇa dineṣu sevitā |
karotu sā naḥ śubhaheturīśvarī
śubhāni bhadrāṇyabhihantu cāpadaḥ ||

Separatedstutā | suraiḥ | pūrvam | abhīṣṭasaṃśrayāt
tathā | surendreṇa | dineṣu | sevitā

śubhāni | bhadrāṇi | abhihantu | ca | āpadaḥ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संश्रयात्तथा saṃśrayāttathā → अभीष्टसंश्रयात् + तथा
    no change (t before t) · खरि च (8.4.55)
    The final t stays unvoiced before the unvoiced t of तथा. The word is broken across the printed lines with a hyphen (अभीष्टसंश्रया- / त्तथा); in the padaccheda it belongs to the first line, where it begins.
  • सुरेन्द्रेण surendreṇa → सुर + इन्द्रेण
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e.
  • शुभहेतुरीश्वरी śubhaheturīśvarī → शुभहेतुः + ईश्वरी
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā the visarga (from s, as ru) appears as r before a vowel or voiced consonant.
  • भद्राण्यभिहन्तु bhadrāṇyabhihantu → भद्राणि + अभिहन्तु
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.
  • चापदः cāpadaḥ → च + आपदः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अभीष्टसंश्रयात् abhīṣṭasaṃśrayāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अभीष्ट + संश्रय · Vigraha: अभीष्टस्य संश्रयः, तस्मात् abhīṣṭasya saṃśrayaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the resort to (attainment of) what was desired”; in the verse: because they had gained their desire through her (in the Mahiṣa episode), or: for the refuge they wanted
    Alternative: संश्रय can mean ‘attaining, coming to’ or ‘refuge, resort’. Taken as ‘refuge’ the phrase means ‘(praised) for the sake of the refuge they desired’ (अभीष्टः संश्रयः, a karmadhāraya). The ablative of cause with ‘attainment of the desired’ fits पूर्वम्: the gods praised her once their wish had been granted.
  • सुरेन्द्रेण surendreṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सुर + इन्द्र · Vigraha: सुराणाम् इन्द्रः, तेन surāṇām indraḥ, tena
    Literally “by the chief of the gods”; in the verse: by Indra
  • शुभहेतुः śubhahetuḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शुभ + हेतु · Vigraha: शुभस्य हेतुः śubhasya hetuḥ
    Literally “cause of the auspicious”; in the verse: the source of all good
    Alternative: A bahuvrīhi (शुभः हेतुः यस्याः सा ‘she whose cause/purpose is good’) is grammatically possible but weaker; the tatpuruṣa gives the plain sense ‘cause of good’.

C. Morphology

  • करोतु karotu — Imperative (loṭ) 3rd sg. of √कृ, tanādi class: stem करो- with the ending -तु. The third-person imperative expresses a wish or prayer: ‘may she do’.
  • अभिहन्तु abhihantu — Imperative 3rd sg. of अभि + √हन्, an athematic (adādi) root: हन् + तु → हन्तु. The prefix अभि ‘towards, against’ makes it ‘strike down’.
  • शुभहेतुः śubhahetuḥ — हेतु is masculine, and a tatpuruṣa keeps the gender of its last member, so the compound stays masculine (शुभहेतुः) though it describes the feminine ईश्वरी.
  • सेविता sevitā — Past passive participle of √सेव् with the connecting vowel i (इट्): सेव् + इ + त → सेवित, feminine सेविता.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पूर्वम् अभीष्टसंश्रयात् सुरैः स्तुता, तथा सुरेन्द्रेण दिनेषु सेविता, शुभहेतुः सा ईश्वरी नः शुभानि भद्राणि करोतु, आपदः च अभिहन्तु।

pūrvam abhīṣṭasaṃśrayāt suraiḥ stutā, tathā surendreṇa dineṣu sevitā, śubhahetuḥ sā īśvarī naḥ śubhāni bhadrāṇi karotu, āpadaḥ ca abhihantu |

Two participial phrases (स्तुता … सेविता) describe the subject सा ईश्वरी; the main verbs are two imperatives of prayer, करोतु and अभिहन्तु, joined by च. The sentence runs on into 5.82, whose relative clauses (या …) also describe सा.

6Translation

LiteralPraised formerly by the gods because of the attainment of their desire, and likewise served by the lord of the gods for days — may she, the Sovereign, the cause of good, do auspicious things and blessings for us, and strike down calamities.

ReadableShe whom the gods once praised when their desire was granted, and whom Indra, lord of the gods, worshipped day after day — may that Sovereign, the source of all good, bring us what is auspicious and blessed, and strike down our calamities.

‘Our’ with calamities is supplied from नः; the Sanskrit has नः once, with करोतु.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods close their hymn with a prayer. They recall that they praised her before — after the slaying of Mahiṣa, when their desire was granted (chapter 4) — and that Indra worshipped her for days; now they ask her to bring them good and strike down their calamities. The metre changes for this closing prayer: vaṃśastha (twelve syllables to the line, ja-ta-ja-ra) after the anuṣṭubh of the hymn.

Traditional interpretationThe prayer echoes the chapter 4 hymn, led by Śakra (Indra, 4.2), where the gods asked ‘may she bestow auspicious things on us’ (4.3: विदधातु शुभानि सा नः). Her promise in 5.6 — to destroy the gods’ calamities whenever they remembered her — is what they now invoke.

5.82Ślokaindravaṃśā

1Original śloka

या साम्प्रतं चोद्धतदैत्यतापितै-
रस्माभिरीशा च सुरैर्नमस्यते।
या च स्मृता तत्क्षणमेव हन्ति नः
सर्वापदो भक्तिविनम्रमूर्तिभिः ॥ ८२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

या | साम्प्रतम् | च | उद्धतदैत्यतापितैःअस्माभिः | ईशा | च | सुरैः | नमस्यतेया | च | स्मृता | तत्क्षणम् | एव | हन्ति | नःसर्वापदः | भक्तिविनम्रमूर्तिभिः

3IAST

rasmābhirīśā ca surairnamasyate |
yā ca smṛtā tatkṣaṇameva hanti naḥ
sarvāpado bhaktivinamramūrtibhiḥ ||

asmābhiḥ | īśā | ca | suraiḥ | namasyate

sarvāpadaḥ | bhaktivinamramūrtibhiḥ

चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); joins the second relative clause

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • साम्प्रतं चोद्धत sāmprataṃ coddhata → साम्प्रतम् + च + उद्धत(दैत्यतापितैः)
    anusvāra and vowel sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Final m becomes anusvāra before c; a + u → o (च + उद्धत → चोद्धत).
  • तापितैरस्माभिरीशा tāpitairasmābhirīśā → उद्धतदैत्यतापितैः + अस्माभिः + ईशा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After the vowels ai and i the visarga appears as r before a vowel. The word is broken across the printed lines (…तापितै- / रस्माभि…); it belongs to the first line.
  • सुरैर्नमस्यते surairnamasyate → सुरैः + नमस्यते
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai the visarga appears as r before the voiced n.
  • सर्वापदो भक्ति sarvāpado bhakti → सर्वापदः + भक्ति(विनम्रमूर्तिभिः)
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o. Inside the compound, सर्वा + आपदः → सर्वापदः by अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • उद्धतदैत्यतापितैः uddhatadaityatāpitaiḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (उद्धतदैत्य)
    Members: उद्धत + दैत्य + तापित · Vigraha: उद्धताः दैत्याः उद्धतदैत्याः; उद्धतदैत्यैः तापिताः, तैः uddhatāḥ daityāḥ uddhatadaityāḥ; uddhatadaityaiḥ tāpitāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “by (those) tormented by arrogant daityas”; in the verse: by us gods, harried by Śumbha and Niśumbha and their followers
  • तत्क्षणम् tatkṣaṇam — karmadhāraya (the pronoun as qualifier), used adverbially
    Members: तद् + क्षण · Vigraha: सः क्षणः, तम् saḥ kṣaṇaḥ, tam
    Literally “that moment”; in the verse: instantly
  • सर्वापदः sarvāpadaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्वा + आपद् · Vigraha: सर्वाः आपदः sarvāḥ āpadaḥ
    Literally “all calamities”; in the verse: every calamity without exception
  • भक्तिविनम्रमूर्तिभिः bhaktivinamramūrtibhiḥ — bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (भक्तिविनम्र: भक्त्या विनम्र)
    Members: भक्ति + विनम्र + मूर्ति · Vigraha: भक्त्या विनम्रा मूर्तिः येषां ते, तैः bhaktyā vinamrā mūrtiḥ yeṣāṃ te, taiḥ
    Literally “by those whose bodies are bowed with devotion”; in the verse: by devotees bowing in reverence

C. Morphology

  • नमस्यते namasyate — Denominative verb from the noun नमस् ‘homage’ with -य (क्यच्): नमस्यति ‘pays homage’. Here passive (karmaṇi), with the agents in the instrumental (अस्माभिः … सुरैः): ‘is bowed to’. The passive of the denominative coincides in form with an ātmanepada; the instrumentals show which it is.
  • स्मृता smṛtā — Past passive participle of √स्मृ, feminine: ‘remembered’. With हन्ति it has a conditional force: ‘when (as soon as) she is remembered, she destroys’.
  • हन्ति hanti — Present 3rd sg. of the athematic (adādi) root √हन्, with no class vowel: हन् + ति. Used here for what she always does.
  • ईशा īśā — Feminine of ईश ‘ruler’, nominative singular. It could also be the instrumental singular of the consonant stem ईश् ‘lord’ (‘by the lord’, i.e. Indra, with us gods); the edition takes it as a title of the Goddess, in apposition to या.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(सा ईश्वरी नः शुभानि करोतु) या साम्प्रतम् च उद्धतदैत्यतापितैः अस्माभिः सुरैः ईशा च नमस्यते, या च भक्तिविनम्रमूर्तिभिः स्मृता तत्क्षणम् एव नः सर्वापदः हन्ति।

(sā īśvarī naḥ śubhāni karotu) yā sāmpratam ca uddhatadaityatāpitaiḥ asmābhiḥ suraiḥ īśā ca namasyate, yā ca bhaktivinamramūrtibhiḥ smṛtā tatkṣaṇam eva naḥ sarvāpadaḥ hanti |

Two relative clauses (या … नमस्यते; या च … हन्ति) still describe सा ईश्वरी of 5.81, whose prayer (करोतु, अभिहन्तु) governs the whole. The first च sets ‘now’ against ‘formerly’ in 5.81; the second च goes with ईशा (‘and, as Sovereign, is bowed to’); the third joins the second clause.

6Translation

LiteralShe who now, too, is bowed to as Sovereign by us, the gods, tormented by the arrogant daityas; and who, remembered by those whose bodies are bowed in devotion, at that very moment destroys all our calamities —

ReadableShe to whom we gods, harried by the arrogant daityas, now bow as Sovereign, and who, the moment she is remembered by those bowed down in devotion, destroys all our calamities — (may she bring us good).

The bracketed close is supplied from 5.81 (करोतु … अभिहन्तु); 5.82 has no main verb of its own.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn ends with two relative clauses on the Goddess of 5.81: she is bowed to now by the gods tormented by the daityas, and she destroys every calamity the instant she is remembered in devotion. The prayer of 5.81 governs both. The metre is indravaṃśā (ta-ta-ja-ra), the sister of the vaṃśastha of 5.81, differing only in the first syllable of each line.

Traditional interpretationThe verse restates her promise of 5.6 — ‘remembered in calamities, I will destroy your greatest calamities at that very moment’ (तत्क्षणात्) — as a fact about her: स्मृता तत्क्षणमेव हन्ति. The hymn is answered at once: when the narrative resumes (5.84), Pārvatī comes to bathe in the Gaṅgā where the gods are praising.

Modern reflectionA modern reflection: the hymn moves from praise to petition only at its end. The gods first name her in everything — hunger, faith, error, consciousness — and only then ask for help.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: ईशा is taken as a feminine title (‘the Sovereign’); an instrumental of ईश् ‘lord’ (Indra) is grammatically possible. The second च is then loosely placed.

ऋषिरुवाच ॥ ८३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    After i the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel and joins it, as in 1.46.

ऋषिः उवाच।

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

ReadableThe sage said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods’ hymn on the Himālaya (5.9–82) is over, and Medhas, still speaking to King Suratha, returns to the story: what happened while the gods were praising.

5.84Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवं स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानां तत्र पार्वती।
स्नातुमभ्याययौ तोये जाह्नव्या नृपनन्दन ॥ ८४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | स्तवादियुक्तानाम् | देवानाम् | तत्र | पार्वतीस्नातुम् | अभ्याययौ | तोये | जाह्नव्याः | नृपनन्दन

3IAST

As recitedevaṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ devānāṃ tatra pārvatī |
snātumabhyāyayau toye jāhnavyā nṛpanandana ||

Separatedevam | stavādiyuktānām | devānām | tatra | pārvatī
snātum | abhyāyayau | toye | jāhnavyāḥ | nṛpanandana

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
स्तवादियुक्तानाम्stavādiyuktānāmengaged in praise and the restm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवानाम्
देवानाम्devānām(while) the gods (were)m. gen. pl. of देव; with स्तवादियुक्तानाम् a genitive absolute
तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place, from तद्)
पार्वतीpārvatīPārvatī, the daughter of the mountainf. nom. sg. of पार्वती (taddhita from पर्वत ‘mountain’); subject of अभ्याययौ
स्नातुम्snātumto batheinfinitive (tumun) of √स्ना (adādi, 2P ‘to bathe’); purpose
