तोयेtoyein the watern. loc. sg. of तोय
जाह्नव्याःjāhnavyāḥof the Jāhnavī (the Gaṅgā)f. gen. sg. of जाह्नवी (taddhita: ‘daughter of Jahnu’); qualifies तोये
नृपनन्दनnṛpanandanaO prince (Suratha)m. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं स्तवादियुक्तानां evaṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ → एवम् + स्तवादियुक्तानाम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so also स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानां and देवानां तत्र.
  • स्तवादि stavādi → स्तव + आदि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • जाह्नव्या नृपनन्दन jāhnavyā nṛpanandana → जाह्नव्याः + नृपनन्दन
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as रु) becomes y, and a y so produced is always dropped before a consonant, leaving जाह्नव्या.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्तवादियुक्तानाम् stavādiyuktānām — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (its first member स्तवादि is a bahuvrīhi: स्तवः आदिः येषां ते)
    Members: स्तवादि + युक्त · Vigraha: स्तवादिभिः युक्ताः, तेषाम् stavādibhiḥ yuktāḥ, teṣām
    Literally “joined with praise and the like”; in the verse: occupied with hymns and other acts of worship
  • नृपनन्दन nṛpanandana — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: नृप + नन्दन · Vigraha: नृपस्य नन्दनः nṛpasya nandanaḥ
    Literally “a king’s delight, i.e. a king’s son”; in the verse: O prince — Medhas’s address to Suratha

C. Morphology

  • अभ्याययौ abhyāyayau — Perfect of √या (ययौ, with the -au ending of ā-roots) behind two prefixes: अभि + आ, where i becomes y before ā (अभ्या-). ‘Came up to, approached’.
  • स्नातुम् snātum — Infinitive in -tum of √स्ना, expressing purpose with a verb of motion: ‘came to bathe’.
  • देवानाम् devānām — With the participle-like adjective स्तवादियुक्तानाम् it forms a genitive absolute: ‘the gods being so engaged’, i.e. ‘while the gods were praising’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

नृपनन्दन, एवं देवानां स्तवादियुक्तानां (सताम्) तत्र पार्वती जाह्नव्याः तोये स्नातुम् अभ्याययौ।

nṛpanandana, evaṃ devānāṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ (satām) tatra pārvatī jāhnavyāḥ toye snātum abhyāyayau |

A genitive absolute (एवं … देवानाम्) sets the time; the main clause is पार्वती … अभ्याययौ with the purpose infinitive स्नातुम्; नृपनन्दन is the narrator’s address.

6Translation

LiteralWhile the gods were thus engaged in praise and the rest, there Pārvatī came, O son of a king, to bathe in the water of the Jāhnavī.

ReadableWhile the gods were thus praising, O prince, Pārvatī came there to bathe in the waters of the Gaṅgā.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe scene shifts while the hymn is still in the air: Pārvatī, Śiva’s consort and the daughter of Himavat, comes down to the Gaṅgā to bathe at the very place where the gods are praying. The genitive absolute ties the two events together.

Traditional interpretationChapter 4 announced this episode in advance: the Goddess would arise again ‘from the body of Gaurī’ to kill Śumbha and Niśumbha (4.41, गौरीदेहात्). Pārvatī’s coming to the river is the occasion for that promise to be kept.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same name, पार्वती (name 246). The two works share the Śākta identification of the Goddess with Himavat’s daughter; they remain distinct texts.

5.85Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

साब्रवीत्तान् सुरान् सुभ्रूर्भवद्भिः स्तूयतेऽत्र का।
शरीरकोशतश्चास्याः समुद्भूताब्रवीच्छिवा ॥ ८५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सा | अब्रवीत् | तान् | सुरान् | सुभ्रूः | भवद्भिः | स्तूयते | अत्र | काशरीरकोशतः | च | अस्याः | समुद्भूता | अब्रवीत् | शिवा

3IAST

As recitedsābravīttān surān subhrūrbhavadbhiḥ stūyate'tra kā |
śarīrakośataścāsyāḥ samudbhūtābravīcchivā ||

Separatedsā | abravīt | tān | surān | subhrūḥ | bhavadbhiḥ | stūyate | atra | kā
śarīrakośataḥ | ca | asyāḥ | samudbhūtā | abravīt | śivā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
साsāshe (Pārvatī)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of the first अब्रवीत्
अब्रवीत्abravītsaid√ब्रू (adādi, 2U ‘to say’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तान्tānthosepronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
सुरान्surānto the godsm. acc. pl. of सुर; the person addressed (verbs of speaking take the addressee in the accusative)
सुभ्रूःsubhrūḥthe fair-browed onef. nom. sg. of सुभ्रू; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to सा
भवद्भिःbhavadbhiḥby you (sirs)honorific pronoun भवत्, m. instr. pl.; agent of the passive स्तूयते
स्तूयतेstūyateis being praised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
अत्रatrahereindeclinable (adverb of place)
काkāwho (which woman)interrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; subject of स्तूयते
शरीरकोशतःśarīrakośataḥfrom the sheath of the bodyindeclinable ablative in -tas (tasil) of the compound शरीरकोश (see Samāsa)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अस्याःasyāḥof her (Pārvatī)pronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.; depends on शरीरकोशतः
समुद्भूताsamudbhūtāhaving come forthpast participle (kta) of सम् + उद् + √भू, f. nom. sg.; qualifies शिवा
अब्रवीत्abravītsaid√ब्रू (adādi, 2U), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
शिवाśivāŚivā, the Auspicious Onef. nom. sg. of शिवा; subject of the second अब्रवीत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • साब्रवीत् sābravīt → सा + अब्रवीत्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • सुभ्रूर्भवद्भिः subhrūrbhavadbhiḥ → सुभ्रूः + भवद्भिः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After a vowel other than a/ā the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a voiced consonant.
  • स्तूयतेऽत्र stūyate'tra → स्तूयते + अत्र
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).
  • शरीरकोशतश्चास्याः śarīrakośataścāsyāḥ → शरीरकोशतः + च + अस्याः
    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś (-तश्च); then च + अस्याः → चास्याः.
  • समुद्भूताब्रवीच्छिवा samudbhūtābravīcchivā → समुद्भूता + अब्रवीत् + शिवा
    vowel sandhi, then consonant sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)
    ā + a → ā; then final t before ś becomes c, and the ś after it becomes ch: अब्रवीत् शिवा → अब्रवीच्छिवा.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सुभ्रूः subhrūḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सु + भ्रू · Vigraha: शोभने भ्रुवौ यस्याः सा śobhane bhruvau yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose brows are fair”; in the verse: the lovely-browed Goddess, Pārvatī
  • शरीरकोश śarīrakośa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शरीर + कोश · Vigraha: शरीरस्य कोशः śarīrasya kośaḥ
    Literally “the sheath of the body”; in the verse: the bodily envelope, out of which the new form steps
    Alternative: Also read as a karmadhāraya of identification, शरीरम् एव कोशः ‘the body, which is a sheath’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • अब्रवीत् abravīt — Imperfect of √ब्रू with augment a- and the connecting ī that ब्रू takes before consonant endings of the strong forms: अ-ब्रव्-ई-त्. The verse uses it twice, once for each speaker.
  • स्तूयते stūyate — Passive present of √स्तु: the root vowel is lengthened before the passive suffix -ya- (स्तू-य-ते). With the instrumental भवद्भिः: ‘who is praised by you?’.
  • शरीरकोशतः śarīrakośataḥ — The suffix -tas (tasil) makes an indeclinable with ablative sense: ‘from the body’s sheath’. It still governs a genitive (अस्याः), as a noun would.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सुभ्रूः सा तान् सुरान् अब्रवीत् — अत्र भवद्भिः का स्तूयते (इति)। अस्याः शरीरकोशतः समुद्भूता शिवा च अब्रवीत्।

subhrūḥ sā tān surān abravīt — atra bhavadbhiḥ kā stūyate (iti) | asyāḥ śarīrakośataḥ samudbhūtā śivā ca abravīt |

Two sentences of the same shape: subject, अब्रवीत्. The first carries Pārvatī’s question as direct speech; the second introduces the answer, which is given in 5.86.

6Translation

LiteralShe, the fair-browed one, said to those gods: ‘Who is being praised by you here?’ And Śivā, having come forth from the sheath of her body, said:

ReadableThe lovely-browed Goddess asked the gods, ‘Whom are you praising here?’ And from the sheath of her own body Śivā came forth and answered:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningPārvatī asks whom the gods are praising, and the answer comes not from them but from a goddess who steps out of Pārvatī’s own body. The one who asks and the one who answers are of one substance; the reply follows in 5.86.

Traditional interpretationTraditionally the question is read as the Goddess’s play (līlā): she asks as though she did not know, so that she herself may reveal that the gods’ hymn was addressed to her.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names the Goddess सुभ्रूः (name 461) and शिवा (name 53). The shared epithets belong to a common Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate.

5.86Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स्तोत्रं ममैतत् क्रियते शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः।
देवैः समेतैः समरे निशुम्भेन पराजितैः ॥ ८६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

स्तोत्रम् | मम | एतत् | क्रियते | शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैःदेवैः | समेतैः | समरे | निशुम्भेन | पराजितैः

3IAST

As recitedstotraṃ mamaitat kriyate śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ |
devaiḥ sametaiḥ samare niśumbhena parājitaiḥ ||

Separatedstotram | mama | etat | kriyate | śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ
devaiḥ | sametaiḥ | samare | niśumbhena | parājitaiḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
स्तोत्रम्stotramhymn of praisen. nom. sg. of स्तोत्र (√स्तु + ṣṭran); subject of the passive क्रियते
ममmamaof me, to mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; the one praised
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies स्तोत्रम्
क्रियतेkriyateis being made, is offered√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैःśumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥdriven out by the demon Śumbham. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवैः
देवैःdevaiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of देव; agent of क्रियते
समेतैःsametaiḥassembled togetherpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √इ ‘to come together’, m. instr. pl.; qualifies देवैः
समरेsamarein battlem. loc. sg. of समर
निशुम्भेनniśumbhenaby Niśumbham. instr. sg. of निशुम्भ; agent of पराजितैः
पराजितैःparājitaiḥdefeatedpast participle (kta) of परा + √जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), m. instr. pl.; qualifies देवैः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स्तोत्रं ममैतत् stotraṃ mamaitat → स्तोत्रम् + मम + एतत्
    anusvāra sandhi, then vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; a + e → ai (मम + एतत् → ममैतत्).
  • दैत्यनिराकृतैः daityanirākṛtaiḥ → दैत्य + निर् + आ + कृतैः
    consonant change inside a compound (the prefix निस्) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    The prefix निस् appears as निर् before the vowel of आ (its s treated as रु and sounded r): निराकृत ‘driven away, rejected’.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (its first member शुम्भदैत्य is a karmadhāraya: शुम्भः च असौ दैत्यः च)
    Members: शुम्भदैत्य + निराकृत · Vigraha: शुम्भदैत्येन निराकृताः, तैः śumbhadaityena nirākṛtāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally “rejected by Śumbha the daitya”; in the verse: the gods whom the demon Śumbha has thrown out of their realms (5.2–5)

C. Morphology

  • क्रियते kriyate — Passive present of √कृ: before the passive -ya- the root’s ṛ becomes ri (क्रि-य-ते). ‘Is being made’.
  • समेतैः sametaiḥ — सम् + आ + √इ + kta: आ + इत → एत by guṇa (6.1.87), so समेत ‘come together, assembled’.
  • पराजितैः parājitaiḥ — Past participle of परा + √जि, ‘conquered, defeated’, construed with the agent instrumental निशुम्भेन.

D. Anvaya — prose order

शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः समरे निशुम्भेन पराजितैः समेतैः देवैः एतत् मम स्तोत्रं क्रियते।

śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ samare niśumbhena parājitaiḥ sametaiḥ devaiḥ etat mama stotraṃ kriyate |

A passive sentence: subject स्तोत्रम्, verb क्रियते, agent देवैः with three qualifiers (निराकृतैः, समेतैः, पराजितैः).

6Translation

LiteralThis hymn to me is being made by the gods, assembled, driven out by the daitya Śumbha and defeated in battle by Niśumbha.

Readable‘This hymn is offered to me by the gods, who have gathered here after the demon Śumbha drove them out and Niśumbha defeated them in battle.’

‘After’ makes explicit the order of events implied by the three participles.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚivā answers Pārvatī’s question: the hymn is hers. The gods are named by their plight — expelled by Śumbha, beaten in battle by Niśumbha — which recalls the opening of the chapter (5.2–5).

Traditional interpretationThe tradition reads the episode as a statement of identity: the Goddess the gods invoked as dwelling in all beings (5.14–80) answers from within Pārvatī herself. Asking and answering, she shows that the hymn and its object are hers.

5.87Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शरीरकोशाद्यत्तस्याः पार्वत्या निःसृताम्बिका।
कौशिकीति समस्तेषु ततो लोकेषु गीयते ॥ ८७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शरीरकोशात् | यत् | तस्याः | पार्वत्याः | निःसृता | अम्बिकाकौशिकी | इति | समस्तेषु | ततः | लोकेषु | गीयते

3IAST

As recitedśarīrakośādyattasyāḥ pārvatyā niḥsṛtāmbikā |
kauśikīti samasteṣu tato lokeṣu gīyate ||

Separatedśarīrakośāt | yat | tasyāḥ | pārvatyāḥ | niḥsṛtā | ambikā
kauśikī | iti | samasteṣu | tataḥ | lokeṣu | gīyate

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शरीरकोशात्śarīrakośātfrom the sheath of the bodym. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.85)
यत्yatsince, becauseindeclinable (conjunction; correlative of ततः)
तस्याःtasyāḥof thatpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.; qualifies पार्वत्याः
पार्वत्याःpārvatyāḥof Pārvatīf. gen. sg. of पार्वती; depends on शरीरकोशात्
निःसृताniḥsṛtācame forthpast participle (kta) of निस् + √सृ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to flow, move’), f. nom. sg.; used as the verb of the clause
अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject
कौशिकीkauśikīKauśikī, ‘she of the sheath’f. nom. sg. of कौशिकी (taddhita from कोश); the name quoted by इति
इतिitias, by the nameindeclinable (marks the preceding word as a name)
समस्तेषुsamasteṣuin allm. loc. pl. of समस्त; qualifies लोकेषु
ततःtataḥthereforeindeclinable (adverb, correlative of यत्)
लोकेषुlokeṣuin the worldsm. loc. pl. of लोक
गीयतेgīyateis sung of, is celebrated√गै (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sing’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शरीरकोशाद्यत्तस्याः śarīrakośādyattasyāḥ → शरीरकोशात् + यत् + तस्याः
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced y; the t of यत् stays t before the t of तस्याः.
  • पार्वत्या निःसृताम्बिका pārvatyā niḥsṛtāmbikā → पार्वत्याः + निःसृता + अम्बिका
    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga (रु → y, dropped before the consonant); then ā + a → ā (निःसृताम्बिका).
  • कौशिकीति kauśikīti → कौशिकी + इति
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ī + i → ī.
  • ततो लोकेषु tato lokeṣu → ततः + लोकेषु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शरीरकोशात् śarīrakośāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: शरीर + कोश · Vigraha: शरीरस्य कोशः, तस्मात् śarīrasya kośaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the sheath of the body”; in the verse: as in 5.85, here in the ablative case form

C. Morphology

  • कौशिकी kauśikī — Taddhita: कोश + the affix -ika with vṛddhi of the first vowel (को → कौ), feminine in -ī: ‘she who belongs to (came from) the sheath’. The verse itself gives the etymology.
  • गीयते gīyate — Passive present of √गै: the root appears as गी before the passive -ya- (गी-य-ते), ‘is sung, is celebrated’.
  • निःसृता niḥsṛtā — निस् + √सृ + kta, feminine: ‘issued forth’. The participle carries the action without an auxiliary verb.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत् तस्याः पार्वत्याः शरीरकोशात् अम्बिका निःसृता, ततः (सा) समस्तेषु लोकेषु कौशिकी इति गीयते।

yat tasyāḥ pārvatyāḥ śarīrakośāt ambikā niḥsṛtā, tataḥ (sā) samasteṣu lokeṣu kauśikī iti gīyate |

A causal pair: यत् … निःसृता (‘since she came forth …’) and ततः … गीयते (‘therefore she is sung of …’).

6Translation

LiteralSince Ambikā came forth from the sheath of the body of that Pārvatī, she is therefore sung of in all the worlds as ‘Kauśikī’.

ReadableBecause Ambikā emerged from the sheath of Pārvatī’s body, she is celebrated in all the worlds as Kauśikī.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas explains the name: the goddess who emerged (Śivā of 5.85, now called Ambikā) is Kauśikī because she came out of Pārvatī’s bodily sheath (kośa). From here the text mostly calls her Ambikā or Devī.

Traditional interpretationIn the Gita Press pāṭha, the dhyāna printed before chapter 5 describes the presiding Goddess of the third carita, Mahāsarasvatī, as गौरीदेहसमुद्भवाम् ‘born from the body of Gaurī’ — this very emergence. The assignment of Mahāsarasvatī to the carita is liturgical tradition.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also calls the Goddess अम्बिका (name 295). The Devī Māhātmya’s own story of Kauśikī belongs to this text; the shared name does not import the other hymn’s theology.

5.88Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

तस्यां विनिर्गतायां तु कृष्णाभूत्सापि पार्वती।

2Padaccheda — the words separated

तस्याम् | विनिर्गतायाम् | तु | कृष्णा | अभूत् | सा | अपि | पार्वतीकालिका | इति | समाख्याता | हिमाचलकृताश्रया

kāliketi samākhyātā himācalakṛtāśrayā ||

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • तस्यां विनिर्गतायां तु tasyāṃ vinirgatāyāṃ tu → तस्याम् + विनिर्गतायाम् + तु
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.
  • कृष्णाभूत्सापि kṛṣṇābhūtsāpi → कृष्णा + अभूत् + सा + अपि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā twice (कृष्णाभूत्, सापि); the t of अभूत् stays unvoiced before s and is only written joined.
  • कालिकेति kāliketi → कालिका + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + i → e.
  • हिमाचल himācala → हिम + अचल
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā; so also कृत + आश्रया → कृताश्रया.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • हिमाचलकृताश्रया himācalakṛtāśrayā — bahuvrīhi (feminine, -ā)
    Members: हिमाचल + कृत + आश्रय · Vigraha: हिमाचले कृतः आश्रयः यया सा himācale kṛtaḥ āśrayaḥ yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom a dwelling has been made on the snow-mountain”; in the verse: abiding on the Himālaya
  • हिमाचल himācala — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अचल ‘the immovable’, i.e. a mountain, is itself a nañ tatpuruṣa: न चलः)
    Members: हिम + अचल · Vigraha: हिमस्य अचलः himasya acalaḥ
    Literally “the mountain of snow”; in the verse: the Himālaya

C. Morphology

  • विनिर्गतायाम् vinirgatāyām — Locative absolute (with तस्याम्): ‘she having gone out’, i.e. ‘when she had emerged’. The participle of वि + निर् + √गम् loses the root’s m before -ta (गत).
  • अभूत् abhūt — Root aorist of √भू with a predicate adjective: कृष्णा अभूत् ‘became dark’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तस्यां तु विनिर्गतायां सा पार्वती अपि कृष्णा अभूत्, (सा) कालिका इति समाख्याता हिमाचलकृताश्रया (च अभूत्)।

tasyāṃ tu vinirgatāyāṃ sā pārvatī api kṛṣṇā abhūt, (sā) kālikā iti samākhyātā himācalakṛtāśrayā (ca abhūt) |

A locative absolute (तस्यां विनिर्गतायाम्) gives the moment; the main clause is सा पार्वती … कृष्णा अभूत्, with two further qualifiers of Pārvatī in the second line.

6Translation

LiteralAnd when she had gone out, that Pārvatī too became dark; called ‘Kālikā’, she made her dwelling on the Himālaya.

ReadableWhen Ambikā had come forth, Pārvatī herself turned dark. Known as Kālikā, she took up her abode on the Himālaya.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningOnce the radiant Ambikā has left her, Pārvatī becomes dark and is called Kālikā; she remains on the Himālaya. The narrative now follows Ambikā; Kālikā does not act again in this chapter.

Traditional interpretationKālikā here is Pārvatī after the emergence, not the Kālī who later springs from Ambikā’s brow to fight Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (7.5–6). Other Purāṇic tellings reverse the colours — the dark sheath becomes Kauśikī and Pārvatī becomes fair Gaurī — but this text says plainly that Ambikā came out and Pārvatī became dark.

5.89Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततोऽम्बिकां परं रूपं बिभ्राणां सुमनोहरम्।
ददर्श चण्डो मुण्डश्च भृत्यौ शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥ ८९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | अम्बिकाम् | परम् | रूपम् | बिभ्राणाम् | सुमनोहरम्ददर्श | चण्डः | मुण्डः | च | भृत्यौ | शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः

3IAST

As recitedtato'mbikāṃ paraṃ rūpaṃ bibhrāṇāṃ sumanoharam |
dadarśa caṇḍo muṇḍaśca bhṛtyau śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

Separatedtataḥ | ambikām | param | rūpam | bibhrāṇām | sumanoharam
dadarśa | caṇḍaḥ | muṇḍaḥ | ca | bhṛtyau | śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
अम्बिकाम्ambikāmAmbikāf. acc. sg. of अम्बिका; object of ददर्श
परम्paramsupreme, surpassingn. acc. sg. of पर; qualifies रूपम्
रूपम्rūpamform, beautyn. acc. sg. of रूप; object of बिभ्राणाम्
बिभ्राणाम्bibhrāṇāmbearing, possessingpresent participle (śānac) of √भृ (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to bear’), f. acc. sg.; qualifies अम्बिकाम्
सुमनोहरम्sumanoharammost captivatingn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपम्
ददर्शdadarśasaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चण्डःcaṇḍaḥCaṇḍam. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
मुण्डःmuṇḍaḥMuṇḍam. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
भृत्यौbhṛtyauthe two servantsm. nom. du. of भृत्य (gerundive of √भृ: ‘one to be maintained’); in apposition to चण्डः मुण्डः
शुम्भनिशुम्भयोःśumbhaniśumbhayoḥof Śumbha and Niśumbham. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on भृत्यौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततोऽम्बिकां tato'mbikāṃ → ततः + अम्बिकाम्
    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a: the visarga (as रु) becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha): ततोऽम्बिकाम्.
  • परं रूपं paraṃ rūpaṃ → परम् + रूपम्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so also रूपं बिभ्राणां सुमनोहरम्.
  • चण्डो मुण्डश्च caṇḍo muṇḍaśca → चण्डः + मुण्डः + च
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o; -aḥ before c becomes -aś.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सुमनोहरम् sumanoharam — prādi tatpuruṣa (an indeclinable prefix as first member)
    Members: सु + मनोहर · Vigraha: सुष्ठु मनोहरम् suṣṭhu manoharam
    Literally “well heart-stealing”; in the verse: exceedingly lovely
  • मनोहर manohara — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: मनस् + हर · Vigraha: मनः हरति इति manaḥ harati iti
    Literally “that which steals the mind”; in the verse: captivating; मनस् + हर → मनोहर by the visarga rule हशि च (6.1.114)
  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: शुम्भ + निशुम्भ · Vigraha: शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, तयोः śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally “of Śumbha and Niśumbha”; in the verse: the two demon brothers

C. Morphology

  • बिभ्राणाम् bibhrāṇām — Present participle (ātmanepada, -āna) of the reduplicating root √भृ: बि-भ्र-आण, with ṇ for n after r. ‘Bearing’ a form, i.e. having it.
  • ददर्श dadarśa — Perfect of √दृश् (द-दर्श, with guṇa). The verb is singular though it has two subjects joined by च; a verb that stands first often agrees with the nearer subject in Purāṇic narrative.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः भृत्यौ चण्डः मुण्डः च परं सुमनोहरं रूपं बिभ्राणाम् अम्बिकां ददर्श।

tataḥ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ bhṛtyau caṇḍaḥ muṇḍaḥ ca paraṃ sumanoharaṃ rūpaṃ bibhrāṇām ambikāṃ dadarśa |

Verb ददर्श, its object अम्बिकाम् with the participle phrase परं रूपं बिभ्राणाम् सुमनोहरम्, and the double subject चण्डः मुण्डः च with the apposition भृत्यौ.

6Translation

LiteralThen Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, the two servants of Śumbha and Niśumbha, saw Ambikā bearing a supreme, most captivating form.

ReadableThen Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, servants of Śumbha and Niśumbha, saw Ambikā in her surpassing, enchanting beauty.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningCaṇḍa and Muṇḍa enter the story as servants of the demon brothers. Their seeing of the Goddess sets the whole uttama carita in motion: report, embassy and war.

Traditional interpretationThe same two servants lead the first army against her (7.2) and die by Kālī; Caṇḍikā then names Kālī ‘Cāmuṇḍā’ after them (7.27). The pair who first look on her beauty become the occasion of one of her best-known names.

5.90Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ताभ्यां शुम्भाय चाख्याता अतीव सुमनोहरा।
काप्यास्ते स्त्री महाराज भासयन्ती हिमाचलम् ॥ ९० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ताभ्याम् | शुम्भाय | च | आख्याता | अतीव | सुमनोहराका | अपि | आस्ते | स्त्री | महाराज | भासयन्ती | हिमाचलम्

3IAST

As recitedtābhyāṃ śumbhāya cākhyātā atīva sumanoharā |
kāpyāste strī mahārāja bhāsayantī himācalam ||

Separatedtābhyām | śumbhāya | ca | ākhyātā | atīva | sumanoharā
kā | api | āste | strī | mahārāja | bhāsayantī | himācalam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ताभ्याम्tābhyāmby those two (Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa)pronoun तद्, m. instr. du.; agent of आख्याता
शुम्भायśumbhāyato Śumbham. dat. sg. of शुम्भ; recipient of the report
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction, linking to 5.89)
आख्याताākhyātā(she) was reportedpast participle (kta) of आ + √ख्या (adādi, 2P ‘to tell’), f. nom. sg.; the understood subject is Ambikā
अतीवatīvaexceedinglyindeclinable (adverb: अति + इव)
सुमनोहराsumanoharālovely, captivatingf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.89); qualifies स्त्री
काkāsome, a certainpronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; with अपि indefinite
अपिapi(makes का indefinite: ‘some … or other’)indeclinable (particle)
आस्तेāsteis staying, sits√आस् (adādi, 2Ā ‘to sit, stay’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
स्त्रीstrīwomanf. nom. sg. of स्त्री; subject of आस्ते
महाराजmahārājaO great kingm. voc. sg. of महाराज (compound: see Samāsa)
भासयन्तीbhāsayantīilluminatingpresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √भास् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to shine’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies स्त्री
हिमाचलम्himācalamthe Himālayam. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.88); object of भासयन्ती

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ताभ्यां शुम्भाय tābhyāṃ śumbhāya → ताभ्याम् + शुम्भाय
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • चाख्याता cākhyātā → च + आख्याता
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • काप्यास्ते kāpyāste → का + अपि + आस्ते
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha, then yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + a → ā (काप्-); then i before ā becomes y (अपि आस्ते → अप्यास्ते).
  • अतीव atīva → अति + इव
    vowel sandhi inside a fixed indeclinable (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    i + i → ī: ‘over-like’, used as ‘exceedingly’.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महाराज mahārāja — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + राजन् · Vigraha: महान् राजा mahān rājā
    Literally “great king”; in the verse: Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa’s address to Śumbha; in the compound महत् becomes महा- and राजन् becomes राज (an a-stem)

C. Morphology

  • आख्याता ākhyātā — Feminine past participle used as a finite verb: ‘(she) was reported’. The subject, Ambikā, is understood from 5.89; what they said follows as direct speech.
  • आस्ते āste — Present of the ātmanepada root √आस्: ‘sits, stays, is there’.
  • भासयन्ती bhāsayantī — Causative stem भासय- (‘make shine’) + śatṛ, feminine -ī: ‘making the Himālaya shine’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ताभ्यां च शुम्भाय (सा) आख्याता — महाराज, अतीव सुमनोहरा का अपि स्त्री हिमाचलं भासयन्ती आस्ते।

tābhyāṃ ca śumbhāya (sā) ākhyātā — mahārāja, atīva sumanoharā kā api strī himācalaṃ bhāsayantī āste |

A passive report clause (ताभ्यां … आख्याता), then the beginning of the servants’ speech: a woman (स्त्री, with three qualifiers) is (आस्ते).

Left unjoined: आख्याता | अतीव — Hiatus at the pāda boundary (ताभ्यां शुम्भाय चाख्याता is a complete eight-syllable pāda), which is also where the reported speech begins. Purāṇic verse often leaves vowels unjoined across the pāda break; the printed text keeps them apart.

6Translation

LiteralAnd she was reported by those two to Śumbha: ‘Some exceedingly captivating woman, O great king, is staying (there), illuminating the Himālaya.’

ReadableThey told Śumbha of her: ‘Great king, there is a woman of surpassing beauty dwelling on the Himālaya, lighting up the mountain.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe servants’ report to Śumbha begins; it runs to 5.100. They know nothing of who she is — she is ‘some woman’ (का अपि स्त्री) — only that her radiance lights up the mountain.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the demons see the Goddess only as a beautiful woman, and the whole embassy that follows rests on that misreading — the divine taken for an object to be acquired.

5.91Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नैव तादृक् क्वचिद्रूपं दृष्टं केनचिदुत्तमम्।
ज्ञायतां काप्यसौ देवी गृह्यतां चासुरेश्वर ॥ ९१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

न | एव | तादृक् | क्वचित् | रूपम् | दृष्टम् | केनचित् | उत्तमम्ज्ञायताम् | का | अपि | असौ | देवी | गृह्यताम् | च | असुरेश्वर

3IAST

As recitednaiva tādṛk kvacidrūpaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ kenaciduttamam |
jñāyatāṃ kāpyasau devī gṛhyatāṃ cāsureśvara ||

Separatedna | eva | tādṛk | kvacit | rūpam | dṛṣṭam | kenacit | uttamam
jñāyatām | kā | api | asau | devī | gṛhyatām | ca | asureśvara

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
एवevaat all, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
तादृक्tādṛksuchn. nom. sg. of तादृश्; qualifies रूपम्
क्वचित्kvacitanywhere, everindeclinable (indefinite adverb of place)
रूपम्rūpambeauty, formn. nom. sg. of रूप; subject of (अस्ति) with दृष्टम्
दृष्टम्dṛṣṭamhas been seenpast participle (kta) of √दृश् (bhvādi, 1P), n. nom. sg.
केनचित्kenacitby anyoneindefinite pronoun कश्चित्, m. instr. sg.; agent of दृष्टम्
उत्तमम्uttamamsupreme, excellentn. nom. sg. of उत्तम; qualifies रूपम्
ज्ञायताम्jñāyatāmlet it be found out√ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U ‘to know’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
काkāwhointerrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.
अपिapi(with का: ‘who … at all’, ‘whoever’)indeclinable (particle)
असौasauthis, that (yonder)pronoun अदस्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
देवीdevīgoddessf. nom. sg. of देवी
गृह्यताम्gṛhyatāmlet her be seized√ग्रह् (kryādi, 9U ‘to seize’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
असुरेश्वरasureśvaraO lord of the asurasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नैव naiva → न + एव
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + e → ai.
  • क्वचिद्रूपं kvacidrūpaṃ → क्वचित् + रूपम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced r; so also केनचिदुत्तमम् before a vowel.
  • काप्यसौ kāpyasau → का + अपि + असौ
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha, then yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    ā + a → ā; i before a becomes y.
  • चासुरेश्वर cāsureśvara → च + असुरेश्वर
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā. Inside the compound, असुर + ईश्वर → असुरेश्वर by guṇa (6.1.87).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरेश्वर asureśvara — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + ईश्वर · Vigraha: असुराणाम् ईश्वरः asurāṇām īśvaraḥ
    Literally “lord of the asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha

C. Morphology

  • तादृक् tādṛk — Nominative-accusative neuter of तादृश् ‘such’: the final palatal ś appears as k at the end of a word.
  • ज्ञायताम् jñāyatām — Passive imperative (third person): ‘let it be known’ — a polite command to a king, avoiding the direct second person.
  • गृह्यताम् gṛhyatām — Passive imperative of √ग्रह्, with samprasāraṇa (ग्रह् → गृह्) before -ya-: ‘let her be taken’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तादृक् उत्तमं रूपं केनचित् क्वचित् न एव दृष्टम्। असुरेश्वर, असौ देवी का अपि (इति) ज्ञायतां, (सा) गृह्यतां च।

tādṛk uttamaṃ rūpaṃ kenacit kvacit na eva dṛṣṭam | asureśvara, asau devī kā api (iti) jñāyatāṃ, (sā) gṛhyatāṃ ca |

A negative statement (न एव … दृष्टम्) and two passive imperatives (ज्ञायताम्, गृह्यताम्), with the vocative असुरेश्वर.

6Translation

LiteralSuch supreme beauty has never been seen by anyone anywhere. Let it be found out who this goddess is, and let her be seized, O lord of the asuras.

Readable‘Never has anyone, anywhere, seen beauty so perfect. Find out who this goddess may be, lord of the asuras, and take her.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe servants press their case: her beauty is beyond anything ever seen, so Śumbha should discover who she is and take her. They call her देवी, ‘goddess’, without understanding the word.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the servants move in one breath from wonder to seizure — ‘find out who she is, and take her’. The story will show how little that grasping understands.

5.92Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स्त्रीरत्नमतिचार्वङ्गी द्योतयन्ती दिशस्त्विषा।
सा तु तिष्ठति दैत्येन्द्र तां भवान् द्रष्टुमर्हति ॥ ९२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

स्त्रीरत्नम् | अतिचार्वङ्गी | द्योतयन्ती | दिशः | त्विषासा | तु | तिष्ठति | दैत्येन्द्र | ताम् | भवान् | द्रष्टुम् | अर्हति

3IAST

As recitedstrīratnamaticārvaṅgī dyotayantī diśastviṣā |
sā tu tiṣṭhati daityendra tāṃ bhavān draṣṭumarhati ||

Separatedstrīratnam | aticārvaṅgī | dyotayantī | diśaḥ | tviṣā
sā | tu | tiṣṭhati | daityendra | tām | bhavān | draṣṭum | arhati

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
स्त्रीरत्नम्strīratnama jewel among womenn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate in apposition to सा
अतिचार्वङ्गीaticārvaṅgīexceedingly lovely of limbf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सा
द्योतयन्तीdyotayantīlighting uppresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √द्युत् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to shine’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies सा
दिशःdiśaḥthe quarters, all directionsf. acc. pl. of दिश्; object of द्योतयन्ती

तुtuindeed, nowindeclinable (particle)
तिष्ठतिtiṣṭhatiis (there), remains√स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
दैत्येन्द्रdaityendraO lord of the daityasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

द्रष्टुम्draṣṭumto seeinfinitive (tumun) of √दृश्
अर्हतिarhatiought, should√अर्ह् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to deserve, be fit’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • चार्वङ्गी cārvaṅgī → चारु + अङ्गी
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    u before a vowel becomes v.
  • दिशस्त्विषा diśastviṣā → दिशः + त्विषा
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • तां भवान् tāṃ bhavān → ताम् + भवान्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्त्रीरत्नम् strīratnam — saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection: ‘the jewel among women’)
    Members: स्त्री + रत्न · Vigraha: स्त्रीषु रत्नम् strīṣu ratnam
    Literally “a jewel among women”; in the verse: the finest of women — the demons’ way of valuing her as a treasure
    Alternative: Also analysed as ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, स्त्रीणां रत्नम्; the sense is the same.
  • अतिचार्वङ्गी aticārvaṅgī — bahuvrīhi (feminine in -ī, as compounds in -अङ्ग take)
    Members: अति + चारु + अङ्ग · Vigraha: अतिचारूणि अङ्गानि यस्याः सा aticārūṇi aṅgāni yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose limbs are exceedingly lovely”; in the verse: of surpassing grace of form
  • दैत्येन्द्र daityendra — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दैत्य + इन्द्र · Vigraha: दैत्यानाम् इन्द्रः daityānām indraḥ
    Literally “Indra (chief) of the daityas”; in the verse: lord of the demons — Śumbha; a + i → e by guṇa (6.1.87)

C. Morphology

  • द्रष्टुम् draṣṭum — Infinitive of √दृश् with metathesis of the vowel (दर्श् → द्रष्): द्रष्टुम्. With अर्हति: ‘deserves to see’, i.e. ‘you ought to see’.
  • भवान् bhavān — The honorific भवत् takes a third-person verb (भवान् … अर्हति), as in 1.60.
  • तिष्ठति tiṣṭhati — Present of √स्था with the reduplicated stem तिष्ठ-: here ‘is there, stays’, not ‘stands’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

दैत्येन्द्र, स्त्रीरत्नम् अतिचार्वङ्गी त्विषा दिशः द्योतयन्ती सा तु (तत्र) तिष्ठति; भवान् तां द्रष्टुम् अर्हति।

daityendra, strīratnam aticārvaṅgī tviṣā diśaḥ dyotayantī sā tu (tatra) tiṣṭhati; bhavān tāṃ draṣṭum arhati |

Main clause सा तु तिष्ठति with three descriptions of her in the first line; a second clause भवान् … अर्हति with its infinitive.

6Translation

LiteralA jewel among women, exceedingly lovely of limb, lighting up the quarters with her radiance — she is there, O lord of the daityas; your honour ought to see her.

Readable‘A jewel among women, perfect in every limb, she lights the whole horizon with her splendour. There she stays, lord of the demons — you should see her.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe servants describe her a second time and urge Śumbha to go and look. The phrase स्त्रीरत्नम् ‘jewel among women’ prepares the argument that follows: Śumbha owns every jewel of the three worlds (5.93–99), so this one too should be his.

Traditional interpretationThe word ratna governs the whole report and the messenger’s speech that follows (5.109–113): Śumbha is a ‘consumer of jewels’ (रत्नभुजः, 5.112). The Goddess’s light, which the hymn praised, is seen by the demons only as a treasure.

5.93Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

यानि रत्नानि मणयो गजाश्वादीनि वै प्रभो।
त्रैलोक्ये तु समस्तानि साम्प्रतं भान्ति ते गृहे ॥ ९३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

यानि | रत्नानि | मणयः | गजाश्वादीनि | वै | प्रभोत्रैलोक्ये | तु | समस्तानि | साम्प्रतम् | भान्ति | ते | गृहे

3IAST

As recitedyāni ratnāni maṇayo gajāśvādīni vai prabho |
trailokye tu samastāni sāmprataṃ bhānti te gṛhe ||

Separatedyāni | ratnāni | maṇayaḥ | gajāśvādīni | vai | prabho
trailokye | tu | samastāni | sāmpratam | bhānti | te | gṛhe

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
यानिyāniwhatever, whichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.; correlative of the understood तानि
रत्नानिratnānijewels, treasuresn. nom. pl. of रत्न
मणयःmaṇayaḥgemsm. nom. pl. of मणि (i-stem)
गजाश्वादीनिgajāśvādīnielephants, horses and the restn. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
प्रभोprabhoO lordm. voc. sg. of प्रभु (u-stem)
त्रैलोक्येtrailokyein the three worldsn. loc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोक)
तुtuindeedindeclinable (particle; here emphatic, filling the pāda)
समस्तानिsamastāniall of themn. nom. pl. of समस्त; subject of भान्ति
साम्प्रतम्sāmpratamnow, at presentindeclinable (adverb of time)
भान्तिbhāntishine√भा (adādi, 2P ‘to shine’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
गृहेgṛhein the housen. loc. sg. of गृह

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मणयो गजाश्वादीनि maṇayo gajāśvādīni → मणयः + गजाश्वादीनि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.
  • गजाश्वादीनि gajāśvādīni → गज + अश्व + आदीनि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā and a + ā → ā.
  • साम्प्रतं भान्ति sāmprataṃ bhānti → साम्प्रतम् + भान्ति
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गजाश्वादीनि gajāśvādīni — bahuvrīhi (its first member गजाश्व is an itaretara dvandva: गजाः च अश्वाः च)
    Members: गजाश्व + आदि · Vigraha: गजाश्वाः आदयः येषां तानि gajāśvāḥ ādayaḥ yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally “those of which elephants and horses are the first”; in the verse: elephants, horses and other such treasures

C. Morphology

  • यानि yāni — Neuter plural, though the nouns it gathers are mixed (रत्नानि n., मणयः m.): a relative covering things of different genders is put in the neuter. Its correlative (तानि) is understood with समस्तानि.
  • भान्ति bhānti — Present of the athematic √भा: भा + अन्ति → भान्ति, ‘they shine’.
  • त्रैलोक्ये trailokye — Taddhita abstract/collective in -ya with vṛddhi (त्रि → त्रै): त्रैलोक्य ‘the triad of worlds’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

प्रभो, त्रैलोक्ये यानि रत्नानि मणयः गजाश्वादीनि वै (सन्ति), (तानि) समस्तानि तु साम्प्रतं ते गृहे भान्ति।

prabho, trailokye yāni ratnāni maṇayaḥ gajāśvādīni vai (santi), (tāni) samastāni tu sāmprataṃ te gṛhe bhānti |

A relative clause (यानि … त्रैलोक्ये) gathers the treasures; the main clause (समस्तानि … भान्ति) says where they are now.

6Translation

LiteralWhatever jewels, gems, elephants, horses and the like there are in the three worlds, O lord, all of them now shine in your house.

Readable‘Lord, every treasure of the three worlds — jewels, gems, elephants, horses and all the rest — now shines in your palace.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe servants begin the catalogue of Śumbha’s plunder (5.93–99). The general statement comes first: every treasure of the three worlds is now in his house.

Traditional interpretationThe list fills out what the chapter’s opening stated briefly: the brothers took over the offices of Indra, the Sun, the Moon, Kubera, Yama, Varuṇa, Vāyu and Agni (5.2–4). Each treasure named below belonged to one of those gods.

5.94Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ऐरावतः समानीतो गजरत्नं पुरन्दरात्।
पारिजाततरुश्चायं तथैवोच्चैःश्रवा हयः ॥ ९४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऐरावतः | समानीतः | गजरत्नम् | पुरन्दरात्पारिजाततरुः | च | अयम् | तथा | एव | उच्चैःश्रवाः | हयः

3IAST

As recitedairāvataḥ samānīto gajaratnaṃ purandarāt |
pārijātataruścāyaṃ tathaivoccaiḥśravā hayaḥ ||

Separatedairāvataḥ | samānītaḥ | gajaratnam | purandarāt
pārijātataruḥ | ca | ayam | tathā | eva | uccaiḥśravāḥ | hayaḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऐरावतःairāvataḥAirāvatam. nom. sg.; proper name of Indra’s elephant; subject of समानीतः
समानीतःsamānītaḥhas been broughtpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √नी (bhvādi, 1U ‘to lead’), m. nom. sg.
गजरत्नम्gajaratnamthe jewel of elephantsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ऐरावतः
पुरन्दरात्purandarātfrom Purandara (Indra)m. abl. sg. of पुरन्दर; compound (see Samāsa)
पारिजाततरुःpārijātataruḥthe Pārijāta treem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अयम्ayamthis (here)pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies पारिजाततरुः
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एवevajust so, tooindeclinable (emphatic particle)
उच्चैःश्रवाःuccaiḥśravāḥUccaiḥśravasm. nom. sg. of उच्चैःश्रवस् (s-stem); compound (see Samāsa); proper name, in apposition to हयः
हयःhayaḥthe horsem. nom. sg. of हय

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समानीतो गजरत्नं samānīto gajaratnaṃ → समानीतः + गजरत्नम्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced g becomes -o. (ऐरावतः समानीतः keeps its visarga before s.)
  • पारिजाततरुश्चायं pārijātataruścāyaṃ → पारिजाततरुः + च + अयम्
    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    Visarga before c becomes s, then ś; च + अयम् → चायम्; the final m becomes anusvāra before t (8.3.23).
  • तथैवोच्चैःश्रवा हयः tathaivoccaiḥśravā hayaḥ → तथा + एव + उच्चैःश्रवाः + हयः
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, guṇa), then visarga sandhi · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); आद्गुणः (6.1.87); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    ā + e → ai (तथैव); a + u → o (एवोच्चैः); -āḥ before the voiced h loses its visarga (उच्चैःश्रवा हयः).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गजरत्नम् gajaratnam — saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)
    Members: गज + रत्न · Vigraha: गजेषु रत्नम् gajeṣu ratnam
    Literally “jewel among elephants”; in the verse: the finest of elephants
    Alternative: Or ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, गजानां रत्नम्, with the same sense.
  • पुरन्दरात् purandarāt — upapada tatpuruṣa (with the augment m: पुरम्-दर)
    Members: पुर + दर · Vigraha: पुरः दारयति इति puraḥ dārayati iti
    Literally “the breaker of cities (forts)”; in the verse: an epithet of Indra
  • पारिजाततरुः pārijātataruḥ — karmadhāraya (a name with its class noun)
    Members: पारिजात + तरु · Vigraha: पारिजातः च असौ तरुः च pārijātaḥ ca asau taruḥ ca
    Literally “the tree Pārijāta”; in the verse: the wish-granting tree of Indra’s heaven
  • उच्चैःश्रवाः uccaiḥśravāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: उच्चैस् + श्रवस् · Vigraha: उच्चैः श्रवसी यस्य सः uccaiḥ śravasī yasya saḥ
    Literally “he whose ears are (held) high”; in the verse: the name of the divine horse; traditionally also explained as ‘loud-neighing’ (श्रवस् ‘sound, fame’)

C. Morphology

  • समानीतः samānītaḥ — सम् + आ + √नी + kta: ‘brought together, fetched’. The copula is understood: ‘(has been) brought’.
  • उच्चैःश्रवाः uccaiḥśravāḥ — Nominative singular of the s-stem उच्चैःश्रवस्: the final -as becomes -āḥ (cf. वेधाः, चन्द्रमाः).

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऐरावतः गजरत्नं पुरन्दरात् समानीतः; अयं पारिजाततरुः च, तथा एव उच्चैःश्रवाः हयः (समानीतः)।

airāvataḥ gajaratnaṃ purandarāt samānītaḥ; ayaṃ pārijātataruḥ ca, tathā eva uccaiḥśravāḥ hayaḥ (samānītaḥ) |

One participle (समानीतः) serves three subjects; the copula and its repetition are understood.

6Translation

LiteralAirāvata, the jewel among elephants, has been brought from Purandara; and this Pārijāta tree, and likewise the horse Uccaiḥśravas.

Readable‘Airāvata, finest of elephants, has been taken from Indra; so too this Pārijāta tree, and the horse Uccaiḥśravas.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first spoils named are Indra’s: his elephant Airāvata, the Pārijāta tree of his heaven, and the horse Uccaiḥśravas.

Traditional interpretationAll three are treasures that came up from the churning of the ocean; the messenger himself says so of the horse (5.110, क्षीरोदमथनोद्भूतम्). Possessing them, Śumbha has taken the visible marks of Indra’s kingship.

अङ्गणेaṅgaṇein the courtyardn. loc. sg. of अङ्गण
रत्नभूतम्ratnabhūtama very jeweln. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies विमानम्
इहihahereindeclinable (adverb of place)
आनीतम्ānītambroughtpast participle (kta) of आ + √नी (bhvādi, 1U), n. nom. sg.; qualifies विमानम्
यत्yatwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.
आसीत्āsītwas√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
वेधसःvedhasaḥof Vedhas (Brahmā, the Creator)m. gen. sg. of वेधस् (s-stem)
अद्भुतम्adbhutamwonder, marveln. nom. sg. of अद्भुत; predicate of आसीत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विमानं हंससंयुक्तमेतत्तिष्ठति vimānaṃ haṃsasaṃyuktametattiṣṭhati → विमानम् + हंससंयुक्तम् + एतत् + तिष्ठति
    anusvāra sandhi; m and t before a vowel and t · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m becomes anusvāra before h; before the vowel of एतत् it stays m; एतत् and तिष्ठति are simply written together.
  • तेऽङ्गणे te'ṅgaṇe → ते + अङ्गणे
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’- (avagraha).
  • इहानीतं ihānītaṃ → इह + आनीतम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • यदासीद्वेधसोऽद्भुतम् yadāsīdvedhaso'dbhutam → यत् + आसीत् + वेधसः + अद्भुतम्
    consonant sandhi (voicing), then visarga sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel and before v; -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is elided (वेधसोऽद्भुतम्).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • हंससंयुक्तम् haṃsasaṃyuktam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: हंस + संयुक्त · Vigraha: हंसैः संयुक्तम् haṃsaiḥ saṃyuktam
    Literally “joined with swans”; in the verse: drawn by swans — Brahmā’s vehicle
  • रत्नभूतम् ratnabhūtam — karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, become’ as final member marks identity)
    Members: रत्न + भूत · Vigraha: रत्नं भूतम् ratnaṃ bhūtam
    Literally “being a jewel”; in the verse: a veritable treasure
    Alternative: भूत at the end of a compound is sometimes taken as ‘like’ (रत्नम् इव); here identity fits better, since it is being counted among the jewels.

C. Morphology

  • आसीत् āsīt — Imperfect of √अस् with augment and the inserted ī of this root before -t: आस्-ई-त्, ‘was’.
  • वेधसः vedhasaḥ — Genitive singular of the s-stem वेधस् ‘the Ordainer’, a name of Brahmā (nom. वेधाः).

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत् वेधसः अद्भुतम् आसीत्, एतत् हंससंयुक्तं रत्नभूतं विमानम् इह आनीतं ते अङ्गणे तिष्ठति।

yat vedhasaḥ adbhutam āsīt, etat haṃsasaṃyuktaṃ ratnabhūtaṃ vimānam iha ānītaṃ te aṅgaṇe tiṣṭhati |

Main clause विमानम् … तिष्ठति with its qualifiers; a relative clause (यत् … आसीत्) identifies the car’s former owner.

6Translation

LiteralThis swan-yoked aerial car, a very jewel, brought here, stands in your courtyard — that which was the wonder of Vedhas.

Readable‘In your courtyard stands this swan-drawn chariot of the skies, a true treasure brought here — once the marvel of Brahmā.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningNext is Brahmā’s own vehicle: the aerial car drawn by swans, now parked in Śumbha’s courtyard. Even the Creator has been stripped.

Traditional interpretationThe swan (haṃsa) is Brahmā’s mount in Purāṇic iconography; its car here shows that the demons’ conquest reached beyond Indra’s heaven to the Creator himself.

5.96Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

निधिरेष महापद्मः समानीतो धनेश्वरात्।
किञ्जल्किनीं ददौ चाब्धिर्मालामम्लानपङ्कजाम् ॥ ९६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

निधिः | एषः | महापद्मः | समानीतः | धनेश्वरात्किञ्जल्किनीम् | ददौ | च | अब्धिः | मालाम् | अम्लानपङ्कजाम्

3IAST

As recitednidhireṣa mahāpadmaḥ samānīto dhaneśvarāt |
kiñjalkinīṃ dadau cābdhirmālāmamlānapaṅkajām ||

Separatednidhiḥ | eṣaḥ | mahāpadmaḥ | samānītaḥ | dhaneśvarāt
kiñjalkinīm | dadau | ca | abdhiḥ | mālām | amlānapaṅkajām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
निधिःnidhiḥtreasurem. nom. sg. of निधि (i-stem); subject of समानीतः
एषःeṣaḥthispronoun एतद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies निधिः
महापद्मःmahāpadmaḥMahāpadmam. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); name of the treasure, in apposition to निधिः
समानीतःsamānītaḥhas been broughtpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √नी, m. nom. sg.
धनेश्वरात्dhaneśvarātfrom the lord of wealth (Kubera)m. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
किञ्जल्किनीम्kiñjalkinīmfull of filamentsf. acc. sg. of किञ्जल्किन् (taddhita: ‘having filaments’); qualifies मालाम्
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अब्धिःabdhiḥthe Oceanm. nom. sg. of अब्धि (i-stem); subject of ददौ
मालाम्mālāmgarlandf. acc. sg. of माला; object of ददौ
अम्लानपङ्कजाम्amlānapaṅkajāmof unfading lotusesf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मालाम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • निधिरेष nidhireṣa → निधिः + एषः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel.
  • एष महापद्मः eṣa mahāpadmaḥ → एषः + महापद्मः
    visarga sandhi (special rule for एषः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of एषः (as of सः) is dropped before any consonant.
  • समानीतो धनेश्वरात् samānīto dhaneśvarāt → समानीतः + धनेश्वरात्
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced dh becomes -o.
  • चाब्धिर्मालाम् cābdhirmālām → च + अब्धिः + मालाम्
    vowel sandhi, then visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    a + a → ā; then after i the visarga is sounded r before the voiced m.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महापद्मः mahāpadmaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + पद्म · Vigraha: महान् पद्मः mahān padmaḥ
    Literally “the great lotus”; in the verse: the name of one of Kubera’s treasures (nidhi)
  • धनेश्वरात् dhaneśvarāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: धन + ईश्वर · Vigraha: धनस्य ईश्वरः, तस्मात् dhanasya īśvaraḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the lord of wealth”; in the verse: from Kubera; a + ī → e by guṇa (6.1.87)
  • अम्लानपङ्कजाम् amlānapaṅkajām — bahuvrīhi (अम्लान is a nañ tatpuruṣa, न म्लानम्; पङ्कज an upapada tatpuruṣa, पङ्के जायते इति)
    Members: अम्लान + पङ्कज · Vigraha: अम्लानानि पङ्कजानि यस्यां सा, ताम् amlānāni paṅkajāni yasyāṃ sā, tām
    Literally “(a garland) in which the mud-born (lotuses) are unwithered”; in the verse: a garland of lotuses that never fade

C. Morphology

  • ददौ dadau — Perfect of √दा: reduplicated द-दौ, with the -au ending that ā-roots take in the third singular.
  • किञ्जल्किनीम् kiñjalkinīm — Possessive taddhita in -in (किञ्जल्क ‘filament of a lotus’ + इनि), feminine -inī: ‘having filaments’, i.e. of fresh, full-blown flowers.

D. Anvaya — prose order

एषः महापद्मः निधिः धनेश्वरात् समानीतः; अब्धिः च किञ्जल्किनीम् अम्लानपङ्कजां मालां ददौ।

eṣaḥ mahāpadmaḥ nidhiḥ dhaneśvarāt samānītaḥ; abdhiḥ ca kiñjalkinīm amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālāṃ dadau |

Two clauses: a participial one (निधिः … समानीतः) and a finite one with ददौ, whose object मालाम् has two qualifiers.

6Translation

LiteralThis treasure, Mahāpadma, has been brought from the lord of wealth; and the Ocean gave a garland full of filaments, of unfading lotuses.

Readable‘This treasure, the Mahāpadma, was taken from Kubera; and the Ocean gave you a garland of never-fading lotuses, fresh with their filaments.’

‘You’ is supplied: the Ocean’s gift is to Śumbha, as the context makes clear.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningKubera, lord of wealth, has lost the Mahāpadma, and the Ocean has given a garland of unfading lotuses. The Ocean’s gift is phrased as a gift (ददौ), not a seizure: the conquered gods now pay tribute.

Traditional interpretationClassical lists of Kubera’s nine treasures (nidhi) include Mahāpadma. Its loss marks the seizure of Kubera’s office, one of those named at the chapter’s opening (कौबेरम्, 5.3).

5.97Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

छत्रं ते वारुणं गेहे काञ्चनस्रावि तिष्ठति।
तथायं स्यन्दनवरो यः पुराऽऽसीत्प्रजापतेः ॥ ९७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

छत्रम् | ते | वारुणम् | गेहे | काञ्चनस्रावि | तिष्ठतितथा | अयम् | स्यन्दनवरः | यः | पुरा | आसीत् | प्रजापतेः

3IAST

As recitedchatraṃ te vāruṇaṃ gehe kāñcanasrāvi tiṣṭhati |
tathāyaṃ syandanavaro yaḥ purā''sītprajāpateḥ ||

Separatedchatram | te | vāruṇam | gehe | kāñcanasrāvi | tiṣṭhati
tathā | ayam | syandanavaraḥ | yaḥ | purā | āsīt | prajāpateḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
छत्रम्chatramparasol, royal umbrellan. nom. sg. of छत्र; subject of तिष्ठति
तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
वारुणम्vāruṇamVaruṇa’sn. nom. sg. of वारुण (taddhita: ‘belonging to Varuṇa’); qualifies छत्रम्
गेहेgehein the housen. loc. sg. of गेह
काञ्चनस्राविkāñcanasrāvistreaming goldn. nom. sg. of काञ्चनस्राविन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies छत्रम्
तिष्ठतिtiṣṭhatistands√स्था (bhvādi, 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
अयम्ayamthis (here)pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies स्यन्दनवरः
स्यन्दनवरःsyandanavaraḥfinest of chariotsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
यःyaḥwhichrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.
पुराpurāformerlyindeclinable (adverb of time)
आसीत्āsītwas√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
प्रजापतेःprajāpateḥPrajāpati’sm. gen. sg. of प्रजापति (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); predicate (possessor) with आसीत्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • छत्रं ते वारुणं गेहे chatraṃ te vāruṇaṃ gehe → छत्रम् + ते + वारुणम् + गेहे
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.
  • तथायं tathāyaṃ → तथा + अयम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • स्यन्दनवरो यः syandanavaro yaḥ → स्यन्दनवरः + यः
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.
  • पुराऽऽसीत् purā''sīt → पुरा + आसीत्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that a long ā has been absorbed; it is not pronounced.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • काञ्चनस्रावि kāñcanasrāvi — upapada tatpuruṣa (with the agent affix -in, ṇini)
    Members: काञ्चन + स्राविन् · Vigraha: काञ्चनं स्रवति इति kāñcanaṃ sravati iti
    Literally “that which streams gold”; in the verse: showering gold — Varuṇa’s parasol
  • स्यन्दनवरः syandanavaraḥ — saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)
    Members: स्यन्दन + वर · Vigraha: स्यन्दनेषु वरः syandaneṣu varaḥ
    Literally “the best among chariots”; in the verse: a superb chariot
  • प्रजापतेः prajāpateḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: प्रजा + पति · Vigraha: प्रजानां पतिः, तस्य prajānāṃ patiḥ, tasya
    Literally “of the lord of creatures”; in the verse: Prajāpati, the creator-lord

C. Morphology

  • काञ्चनस्रावि kāñcanasrāvi — Neuter nominative of the -in stem स्राविन् (√स्रु + ṇini, with vṛddhi): the final n is dropped, leaving -स्रावि.
  • वारुणम् vāruṇam — Taddhita in -a with vṛddhi of the first vowel (वरुण → वारुण): ‘of Varuṇa’, cf. कौबेरम्, याम्यम् in 5.3.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ते गेहे वारुणं काञ्चनस्रावि छत्रं तिष्ठति; तथा यः पुरा प्रजापतेः आसीत्, अयं स्यन्दनवरः (ते गेहे तिष्ठति)।

te gehe vāruṇaṃ kāñcanasrāvi chatraṃ tiṣṭhati; tathā yaḥ purā prajāpateḥ āsīt, ayaṃ syandanavaraḥ (te gehe tiṣṭhati) |

Two parallel statements of place; in the second the verb is understood, and a relative clause names the chariot’s former owner.

6Translation

LiteralVaruṇa’s parasol, streaming gold, stands in your house; likewise this finest of chariots, which formerly was Prajāpati’s.

Readable‘Varuṇa’s parasol, raining gold, stands in your palace, and so does this superb chariot that once belonged to Prajāpati.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningVaruṇa’s gold-showering parasol and Prajāpati’s chariot complete the list of royal insignia. The parasol is the classic sign of sovereignty; its owner has lost his rule (वरुणस्य, 5.3).

5.98Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मृत्योरुत्क्रान्तिदा नाम शक्तिरीश त्वया हृता।
पाशः सलिलराजस्य भ्रातुस्तव परिग्रहे ॥ ९८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मृत्योः | उत्क्रान्तिदा | नाम | शक्तिः | ईश | त्वया | हृतापाशः | सलिलराजस्य | भ्रातुः | तव | परिग्रहे

3IAST

As recitedmṛtyorutkrāntidā nāma śaktirīśa tvayā hṛtā |
pāśaḥ salilarājasya bhrātustava parigrahe ||

Separatedmṛtyoḥ | utkrāntidā | nāma | śaktiḥ | īśa | tvayā | hṛtā
pāśaḥ | salilarājasya | bhrātuḥ | tava | parigrahe

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मृत्योःmṛtyoḥof Death (Yama)m. gen. sg. of मृत्यु (u-stem); possessor of शक्तिः
उत्क्रान्तिदाutkrāntidāUtkrāntidā, ‘she who gives departure (of life)’f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the weapon’s name
नामnāmanamed, by nameindeclinable
शक्तिःśaktiḥspear (śakti weapon)f. nom. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); subject of हृता
ईशīśaO lordm. voc. sg. of ईश
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of हृता
हृताhṛtāhas been takenpast participle (kta) of √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take, carry off’), f. nom. sg.
पाशःpāśaḥthe noosem. nom. sg. of पाश; subject (with अस्ति understood)
सलिलराजस्यsalilarājasyaof the king of the waters (Varuṇa)m. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
भ्रातुःbhrātuḥof the brotherm. gen. sg. of भ्रातृ (ṛ-stem); depends on परिग्रहे

परिग्रहेparigrahein the possessionm. loc. sg. of परिग्रह

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मृत्योरुत्क्रान्तिदा mṛtyorutkrāntidā → मृत्योः + उत्क्रान्तिदा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After o (not a/ā) the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel.
  • शक्तिरीश śaktirīśa → शक्तिः + ईश
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga is sounded r before a vowel.
  • भ्रातुस्तव bhrātustava → भ्रातुः + तव
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • उत्क्रान्तिदा utkrāntidā — upapada tatpuruṣa (√दा with the affix -a, feminine -ā)
    Members: उत्क्रान्ति + दा · Vigraha: उत्क्रान्तिं ददाति इति utkrāntiṃ dadāti iti
    Literally “giver of the going-out”; in the verse: the spear that makes the life-breath depart — Yama’s weapon
  • सलिलराजस्य salilarājasya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (राजन् becomes the a-stem राज at the end of a compound)
    Members: सलिल + राजन् · Vigraha: सलिलानां राजा, तस्य salilānāṃ rājā, tasya
    Literally “of the king of the waters”; in the verse: of Varuṇa

C. Morphology

  • भ्रातुः bhrātuḥ — Genitive singular of the ṛ-stem भ्रातृ: -ṛ + -as → -uḥ (cf. पितुः, मातुः).
  • हृता hṛtā — Feminine past participle of √हृ agreeing with शक्तिः, used with the agent instrumental त्वया as a passive past: ‘was taken by you’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ईश, मृत्योः उत्क्रान्तिदा नाम शक्तिः त्वया हृता; सलिलराजस्य पाशः तव भ्रातुः परिग्रहे (अस्ति)।

īśa, mṛtyoḥ utkrāntidā nāma śaktiḥ tvayā hṛtā; salilarājasya pāśaḥ tava bhrātuḥ parigrahe (asti) |

Two clauses: a passive past (शक्तिः … त्वया हृता) and a nominal sentence of place with the copula understood (पाशः … परिग्रहे).

6Translation

LiteralThe spear of Death named Utkrāntidā has been taken by you, O lord; the noose of the king of the waters is in your brother’s possession.

Readable‘You have taken, lord, Death’s spear called Utkrāntidā; and the noose of Varuṇa, king of the waters, is held by your brother.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningNow the weapons: Yama’s spear Utkrāntidā, ‘giver of the soul’s departure’, taken by Śumbha, and Varuṇa’s noose, held by Niśumbha. Śumbha and his brother between them hold the instruments of death and of binding.

Traditional interpretationBoth seizures fill out the opening statement of the chapter, that the brothers took the offices of Yama (याम्यम्) and Varuṇa (वरुणस्य) (5.3).

5.99Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

निशुम्भस्याब्धिजाताश्च समस्ता रत्नजातयः।
वह्निरपि ददौ तुभ्यमग्निशौचे च वाससी ॥ ९९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

निशुम्भस्य | अब्धिजाताः | च | समस्ताः | रत्नजातयःवह्निः | अपि | ददौ | तुभ्यम् | अग्निशौचे | च | वाससी

3IAST

As recitedniśumbhasyābdhijātāśca samastā ratnajātayaḥ |
vahnirapi dadau tubhyamagniśauce ca vāsasī ||

Separatedniśumbhasya | abdhijātāḥ | ca | samastāḥ | ratnajātayaḥ
vahniḥ | api | dadau | tubhyam | agniśauce | ca | vāsasī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
निशुम्भस्यniśumbhasyaNiśumbha’s, (in the possession) of Niśumbham. gen. sg. of निशुम्भ; predicate of possession
अब्धिजाताःabdhijātāḥborn of the oceanf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रत्नजातयः
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
समस्ताःsamastāḥallf. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies रत्नजातयः
रत्नजातयःratnajātayaḥkinds of jewelsf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
वह्निःvahniḥFire (Agni)m. nom. sg. of वह्नि (i-stem); subject of ददौ
अपिapitoo, evenindeclinable (particle)
ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तुभ्यम्tubhyamto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg.
अग्निशौचेagniśaucepurified by firen. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वाससी
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
वाससीvāsasītwo garmentsn. acc. du. of वासस् (s-stem); object of ददौ

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • निशुम्भस्याब्धिजाताश्च niśumbhasyābdhijātāśca → निशुम्भस्य + अब्धिजाताः + च
    vowel sandhi, then visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    a + a → ā; visarga before c becomes s, then ś.
  • समस्ता रत्नजातयः samastā ratnajātayaḥ → समस्ताः + रत्नजातयः
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga (रु → y, dropped before the consonant).
  • वह्निरपि vahnirapi → वह्निः + अपि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After i the visarga is sounded r before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अब्धिजाताः abdhijātāḥ — pañcamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अब्धि + जात · Vigraha: अब्धेः जाताः abdheḥ jātāḥ
    Literally “born from the ocean”; in the verse: ocean-born gems (pearls, coral and the like)
    Alternative: Also saptamī tatpuruṣa, अब्धौ जाताः ‘born in the ocean’; the sense is the same.
  • रत्नजातयः ratnajātayaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: रत्न + जाति · Vigraha: रत्नानां जातयः ratnānāṃ jātayaḥ
    Literally “the kinds of jewels”; in the verse: every sort of precious stone
  • अग्निशौचे agniśauce — bahuvrīhi
    Members: अग्नि + शौच · Vigraha: अग्निना शौचं ययोः ते agninā śaucaṃ yayoḥ te
    Literally “(two garments) whose cleansing is by fire”; in the verse: garments purified by fire, a mark of divine cloth

C. Morphology

  • वाससी vāsasī — Neuter dual of the s-stem वासस् ‘garment’: -as + -ī → -असी, ‘a pair of garments’ (upper and lower cloth).
  • तुभ्यम् tubhyam — Dative singular of युष्मद् — the full form, beside the enclitic ते used in 5.93–97.

D. Anvaya — prose order

अब्धिजाताः समस्ताः रत्नजातयः च निशुम्भस्य (सन्ति); वह्निः अपि तुभ्यम् अग्निशौचे वाससी च ददौ।

abdhijātāḥ samastāḥ ratnajātayaḥ ca niśumbhasya (santi); vahniḥ api tubhyam agniśauce vāsasī ca dadau |

A nominal sentence of possession (genitive निशुम्भस्य with the copula understood), then a finite clause with ददौ and its dative and object.

6Translation

LiteralAnd all the kinds of jewels born of the ocean are Niśumbha’s; Fire too gave you a pair of garments purified by fire.

Readable‘Every kind of gem the ocean yields belongs to Niśumbha; and even Agni has given you two garments purified by fire.’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe catalogue ends with the ocean’s gems, held by Niśumbha, and Agni’s gift of fire-cleansed garments to Śumbha. Like the Ocean in 5.96, Agni gives: the conquered gods now serve. In the digital text a footnote asterisk of the printed book followed वह्निरपि; it has been removed (corrections.json, encoding).

Traditional interpretationAgni’s office (वह्निकर्म, 5.4) was among those the brothers seized. Garments ‘purified by fire’ are traditionally understood as cloth that fire cleanses without burning.

5.100Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवं दैत्येन्द्र रत्नानि समस्तान्याहृतानि ते।
स्त्रीरत्नमेषा कल्याणी त्वया कस्मान्न गृह्यते ॥ १०० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | दैत्येन्द्र | रत्नानि | समस्तानि | आहृतानि | तेस्त्रीरत्नम् | एषा | कल्याणी | त्वया | कस्मात् | न | गृह्यते

3IAST

As recitedevaṃ daityendra ratnāni samastānyāhṛtāni te |
strīratnameṣā kalyāṇī tvayā kasmānna gṛhyate ||

Separatedevam | daityendra | ratnāni | samastāni | āhṛtāni | te
strīratnam | eṣā | kalyāṇī | tvayā | kasmāt | na | gṛhyate

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamthusindeclinable (adverb of manner)
दैत्येन्द्रdaityendraO lord of the daityasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.92)
रत्नानिratnānijewels, treasuresn. nom. pl. of रत्न; subject
समस्तानिsamastānialln. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies रत्नानि
आहृतानिāhṛtānihave been broughtpast participle (kta) of आ + √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to fetch’), n. nom. pl.
तेtefor you, to yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic dat./gen. sg.
स्त्रीरत्नम्strīratnama jewel among womenn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.92); in apposition to एषा
एषाeṣāthis (woman)pronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of गृह्यते
कल्याणीkalyāṇīlovely, auspiciousf. nom. sg. of कल्याण (feminine in -ī); qualifies एषा
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of गृह्यते
कस्मात्kasmātwhyinterrogative pronoun किम्, abl. sg., used adverbially (‘from what cause’)
नnanotindeclinable (negation)
गृह्यतेgṛhyateis taken√ग्रह् (kryādi, 9U), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं दैत्येन्द्र evaṃ daityendra → एवम् + दैत्येन्द्र
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • समस्तान्याहृतानि samastānyāhṛtāni → समस्तानि + आहृतानि
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.
  • कस्मान्न kasmānna → कस्मात् + न
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t before a nasal becomes the nasal n (optional, but usual).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्येन्द्र daityendra — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दैत्य + इन्द्र · Vigraha: दैत्यानाम् इन्द्रः daityānām indraḥ
    Literally “chief of the daityas”; in the verse: Śumbha (as in 5.92)
  • स्त्रीरत्नम् strīratnam — saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)
    Members: स्त्री + रत्न · Vigraha: स्त्रीषु रत्नम् strīṣu ratnam
    Literally “a jewel among women”; in the verse: as in 5.92

C. Morphology

  • कस्मात् kasmāt — Ablative of किम् used as ‘why’ (from what cause), with the negative: ‘why … not?’.
  • गृह्यते gṛhyate — Passive present of √ग्रह् with samprasāraṇa (ग्रह् → गृह्-य-ते), as in गृह्यताम् (5.91).

D. Anvaya — prose order

दैत्येन्द्र, एवं समस्तानि रत्नानि ते आहृतानि; एषा कल्याणी स्त्रीरत्नं त्वया कस्मात् न गृह्यते?

daityendra, evaṃ samastāni ratnāni te āhṛtāni; eṣā kalyāṇī strīratnaṃ tvayā kasmāt na gṛhyate?

A summary statement (रत्नानि … आहृतानि) and a rhetorical question in the passive (एषा … कस्मात् न गृह्यते).

6Translation

LiteralThus, O lord of the daityas, all the jewels have been brought to you; why is this lovely one, a jewel among women, not taken by you?

Readable‘So, lord of the demons, every treasure has been brought to you. Why then do you not take this lovely one, the jewel among women?’

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe servants close their report with its conclusion: every ratna is already Śumbha’s, so the one ratna still missing, the Goddess, should be taken too. The speech that began in 5.90 ends here.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the argument from possession is the demons’ undoing. Having taken everything, they assume the Goddess can be taken like the rest.

5.102Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

निशम्येति वचः शुम्भः स तदा चण्डमुण्डयोः।
प्रेषयामास सुग्रीवं दूतं देव्या महासुरम् ॥ १०२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

निशम्य | इति | वचः | शुम्भः | सः | तदा | चण्डमुण्डयोःप्रेषयामास | सुग्रीवम् | दूतम् | देव्याः | महासुरम्

3IAST

As recitedniśamyeti vacaḥ śumbhaḥ sa tadā caṇḍamuṇḍayoḥ |
preṣayāmāsa sugrīvaṃ dūtaṃ devyā mahāsuram ||

Separatedniśamya | iti | vacaḥ | śumbhaḥ | saḥ | tadā | caṇḍamuṇḍayoḥ
preṣayāmāsa | sugrīvam | dūtam | devyāḥ | mahāsuram

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
निशम्यniśamyahaving heardabsolutive (lyap) of नि + √शम्
इतिitithus, these (words)indeclinable (refers back to the speech just reported)
वचःvacaḥspeech, wordsn. acc. sg. of वचस्; object of निशम्य
शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject of प्रेषयामास
सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; resumes शुम्भः
तदाtadāthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
चण्डमुण्डयोःcaṇḍamuṇḍayoḥof Caṇḍa and Muṇḍam. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on वचः
प्रेषयामासpreṣayāmāsasentcausative of प्र + √इष् (divādi, 4P ‘to send forth’), periphrastic perfect (āmantaliṭ), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
सुग्रीवम्sugrīvamSugrīvam. acc. sg.; proper name; object of प्रेषयामास
दूतम्dūtamas messengerm. acc. sg. of दूत; predicative accusative with सुग्रीवम्
देव्याःdevyāḥto the Devīf. gen. sg. of देवी; with दूतम् ‘messenger to the Devī’
महासुरम्mahāsuramthe great asuram. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुग्रीवम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • निशम्येति niśamyeti → निशम्य + इति
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e.
  • वचः शुम्भः स तदा vacaḥ śumbhaḥ sa tadā → वचः + शुम्भः + सः + तदा
    visarga sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    Before the sibilants ś and s the visarga of वचः and शुम्भः is kept (an optional alternative to s/ś, which the Gita Press prefers); the visarga of सः is dropped before the consonant t.
  • सुग्रीवं दूतं देव्या महासुरम् sugrīvaṃ dūtaṃ devyā mahāsuram → सुग्रीवम् + दूतम् + देव्याः + महासुरम्
    anusvāra sandhi; visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; -āḥ before the voiced m loses its visarga.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • चण्डमुण्डयोः caṇḍamuṇḍayoḥ — itaretara dvandva
    Members: चण्ड + मुण्ड · Vigraha: चण्डः च मुण्डः च, तयोः caṇḍaḥ ca muṇḍaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally “of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa”; in the verse: the two servants who made the report
  • सुग्रीवम् sugrīvam — bahuvrīhi (ग्रीवा shortened to -ग्रीव at the end)
    Members: सु + ग्रीवा · Vigraha: शोभना ग्रीवा यस्य सः, तम् śobhanā grīvā yasya saḥ, tam
    Literally “he whose neck is handsome”; in the verse: a proper name: the demon messenger (not the monkey-king of the Rāmāyaṇa)
  • महासुरम् mahāsuram — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान् असुरः, तम् mahān asuraḥ, tam
    Literally “a great asura”; in the verse: a mighty demon

C. Morphology

  • प्रेषयामास preṣayāmāsa — Periphrastic perfect of the causative stem प्रेषय- (प्र + √इष्, ‘send’): stem + -आम् + the perfect of √अस् (आस). Derived stems form their perfect this way.
  • निशम्य niśamya — Absolutive with a prefix, so -ya (lyap) instead of -tvā: ‘having heard’. नि + √शम्, as in निशामय (1.2).
  • देव्याः devyāḥ — Genitive with दूतम्: the person to whom a messenger is sent may be put in the genitive (‘messenger to the Devī’).

D. Anvaya — prose order

चण्डमुण्डयोः इति वचः निशम्य सः शुम्भः तदा महासुरं सुग्रीवं देव्याः दूतं प्रेषयामास।

caṇḍamuṇḍayoḥ iti vacaḥ niśamya saḥ śumbhaḥ tadā mahāsuraṃ sugrīvaṃ devyāḥ dūtaṃ preṣayāmāsa |

An absolutive clause (निशम्य … वचः), then subject (शुम्भः, resumed by सः), verb प्रेषयामास, and object सुग्रीवम् with the predicate दूतम्.

6Translation

LiteralHaving heard these words of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, Śumbha then sent the great asura Sugrīva as messenger to the Devī.

ReadableHearing what Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa said, Śumbha sent the mighty demon Sugrīva as his envoy to the Goddess.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningŚumbha acts at once on the report: he does not go to see her, as the servants urged (5.92), but sends an envoy, the asura Sugrīva. His instructions follow in 5.103.

Traditional interpretationThe embassy gives the chapter its name in the colophon, देव्या दूतसंवादः ‘the Goddess’s dialogue with the messenger’. Sugrīva the demon is a different figure from the monkey-king of the Rāmāyaṇa who bears the same name.
