तिष्ठेत्tiṣṭhetcould stand√स्था (bhvādi 1P ‘to stand’, stem तिष्ठ-), vidhiliṅ (optative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अग्रेagrein front of, beforen. loc. sg. of अग्र, used as a postposition with the genitive
शुम्भनिशुम्भयोःśumbhaniśumbhayoḥof Śumbha and Niśumbham. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on अग्रे

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अवलिप्तासि avaliptāsi → अवलिप्ता + असि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • मैवं maivaṃ → मा + एवम्
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai; the final m becomes anusvāra before त्वम् (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • पुमांस्तिष्ठेदग्रे pumāṃstiṣṭhedagre → पुमान् + तिष्ठेत् + अग्रे
    consonant sandhi (n before t); voicing · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final n before t becomes ṃs (पुमांस्तिष्ठेत्); the t of तिष्ठेत् becomes d before the vowel of अग्रे.
  • ममाग्रतः mamāgrataḥ → मम + अग्रतः
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ — itaretara dvandva (dual)
    Members: शुम्भ + निशुम्भ · Vigraha: शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, तयोः śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally “of Śumbha and Niśumbha”; in the verse: of the two brothers

C. Morphology

  • ब्रूहि brūhi — Imperative of √ब्रू with the ending -हि. After मा, Pāṇinian Sanskrit uses the aorist without augment (मा वोचः); मा with the imperative is the looser usage of the epics and Purāṇas, with the same prohibitive sense.
  • तिष्ठेत् tiṣṭhet — Optative of √स्था (present stem तिष्ठ-): ‘could, would stand’. In a rhetorical question it means ‘no one could’.
  • पुमान् pumān — Nominative singular of the irregular stem पुंस् ‘man’: पुमान्, पुमांसौ, पुमांसः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, त्वम् अवलिप्ता असि; मम अग्रतः एवं मा ब्रूहि। त्रैलोक्ये कः पुमान् शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः अग्रे तिष्ठेत्?

devi, tvam avaliptā asi; mama agrataḥ evaṃ mā brūhi | trailokye kaḥ pumān śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ agre tiṣṭhet?

A statement, a prohibition (मा … ब्रूहि), and a rhetorical question with the optative.

6Translation

LiteralYou are arrogant, O Devī; do not speak thus in front of me. In the three worlds what man could stand before Śumbha and Niśumbha?

ReadableYou are proud, O Devī. Do not speak so in my presence. What man in the three worlds could stand before Śumbha and Niśumbha?

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe messenger takes the vow as arrogance (अवलिप्ता) and rejects it: no man in the three worlds can face the two brothers, so a contest is absurd.

Traditional interpretationThe word दर्प ‘pride’ of the vow (5.120) is answered with अवलिप्ता ‘puffed up’. The irony runs the other way in the narrative: it is the asuras’ pride that will be removed, in the battles of chapters 6–10. Śumbha himself will make the same charge at the end, बलावलेपात् ‘from pride in strength’ (10.3).

5.124Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

अन्येषामपि दैत्यानां सर्वे देवा न वै युधि।
तिष्ठन्ति सम्मुखे देवि किं पुनः स्त्री त्वमेकिका ॥ १२४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

अन्येषाम् | अपि | दैत्यानाम् | सर्वे | देवाः | न | वै | युधितिष्ठन्ति | सम्मुखे | देवि | किम् | पुनः | स्त्री | त्वम् | एकिका

3IAST

As recitedanyeṣāmapi daityānāṃ sarve devā na vai yudhi |
tiṣṭhanti sammukhe devi kiṃ punaḥ strī tvamekikā ||

Separatedanyeṣām | api | daityānām | sarve | devāḥ | na | vai | yudhi
tiṣṭhanti | sammukhe | devi | kim | punaḥ | strī | tvam | ekikā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
अन्येषाम्anyeṣāmof otherpronominal adjective अन्य, m. gen. pl.; qualifies दैत्यानाम्
अपिapievenindeclinable (emphatic particle)
दैत्यानाम्daityānāmDaityasm. gen. pl. of दैत्य; depends on सम्मुखे
सर्वेsarveallpronominal adjective सर्व, m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवाः
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of तिष्ठन्ति
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
युधिyudhiin battlef. loc. sg. of युध् (consonant stem)
तिष्ठन्तिtiṣṭhantistand√स्था (bhvādi 1P, stem तिष्ठ-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
सम्मुखेsammukheface to face, in frontn. loc. sg. of सम्मुख, used as a postposition with the genitive दैत्यानाम्
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
किम्kimhow much (less)indeclinable (interrogative किम्); with पुनः ‘how much less’
पुनःpunaḥagain; with किम् ‘how much less’indeclinable (adverb)
स्त्रीstrīa womanf. nom. sg. of स्त्री; in apposition to त्वम्
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.
एकिकाekikāall alone, by yourselff. nom. sg. of एकिका (एक + taddhita -क, feminine एकिका); qualifies त्वम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • अन्येषामपि anyeṣāmapi → अन्येषाम् + अपि
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    The m is written together with the following vowel.
  • देवा न devā na → देवाः + न
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga is lost, leaving -ā.
  • त्वमेकिका tvamekikā → त्वम् + एकिका
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    The m is written together with the following vowel.

C. Morphology

  • एकिका ekikā — एक ‘one’ with the suffix -क (here diminishing or contemptuous: ‘a mere lone …’); the feminine turns the a before क to i (एकिका). It mocks the Devī for standing without an army.
  • पुनः punaḥ — In the idiom किं पुनः, ‘what again …?’ means ‘how much less (or more)’: an argument from the greater to the lesser.
  • युधि yudhi — Locative singular of the root-noun युध् ‘battle’ (feminine): युध् + इ.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवि, सर्वे देवाः अन्येषाम् अपि दैत्यानां सम्मुखे युधि न वै तिष्ठन्ति; एकिका स्त्री त्वं किं पुनः?

devi, sarve devāḥ anyeṣām api daityānāṃ sammukhe yudhi na vai tiṣṭhanti; ekikā strī tvaṃ kiṃ punaḥ?

A negative statement followed by the elliptical rhetorical question किं पुनः (‘how much less [will] you …’), whose verb is understood from तिष्ठन्ति.

6Translation

LiteralAll the gods do not indeed stand in battle face to face even with other Daityas, O Devī — how much less you, a woman, alone?

ReadableNot all the gods together can stand in battle even before the lesser Daityas, O Devī — how much less you, a woman, alone!

अन्येषाम् ‘other’ is rendered ‘lesser’ to bring out the contrast with Śumbha and Niśumbha.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe messenger argues from the greater to the lesser: the gods cannot face even the other Daityas; a lone woman cannot face the brothers.

Traditional interpretationBeing alone and a woman is, for the messenger, the decisive weakness. The narrative will turn both against the asuras: Śumbha will taunt her for relying on other goddesses, and she will withdraw them all into herself to fight alone (10.3–6).

5.125Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

इन्द्राद्याः सकला देवास्तस्थुर्येषां न संयुगे।
शुम्भादीनां कथं तेषां स्त्री प्रयास्यसि सम्मुखम् ॥ १२५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

इन्द्राद्याः | सकलाः | देवाः | तस्थुः | येषाम् | न | संयुगेशुम्भादीनाम् | कथम् | तेषाम् | स्त्री | प्रयास्यसि | सम्मुखम्

3IAST

As recitedindrādyāḥ sakalā devāstasthuryeṣāṃ na saṃyuge |
śumbhādīnāṃ kathaṃ teṣāṃ strī prayāsyasi sammukham ||

Separatedindrādyāḥ | sakalāḥ | devāḥ | tasthuḥ | yeṣām | na | saṃyuge
śumbhādīnām | katham | teṣām | strī | prayāsyasi | sammukham

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
इन्द्राद्याःindrādyāḥheaded by Indram. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवाः
सकलाःsakalāḥalladjective सकल, m. nom. pl.; qualifies देवाः
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of तस्थुः
तस्थुःtasthuḥstood (their ground)√स्था (bhvādi 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
येषाम्yeṣāmbefore whom (lit. ‘of whom’)relative pronoun यद्, m. gen. pl.; with संयुगे (‘in battle with whom’); correlative तेषाम्
नnanotindeclinable (negative particle)
संयुगेsaṃyugein battlen. loc. sg. of संयुग
शुम्भादीनाम्śumbhādīnāmof Śumbha and the othersm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to तेषाम्
कथम्kathamhow?indeclinable (interrogative adverb)
तेषाम्teṣāmof thempronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.; correlative of येषाम्; depends on सम्मुखम्
स्त्रीstrīa womanf. nom. sg. of स्त्री; in apposition to the subject ‘you’
प्रयास्यसिprayāsyasiwill you goप्र + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), lṛṭ (simple future), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
सम्मुखम्sammukhamface to face, to the front (of)n. acc. sg. of सम्मुख, used as a postposition with the genitive; goal of प्रयास्यसि

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • इन्द्राद्याः indrādyāḥ → इन्द्र + आद्याः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā; likewise शुम्भादीनाम् (शुम्भ + आदीनाम्).
  • देवास्तस्थुर्येषां devāstasthuryeṣāṃ → देवाः + तस्थुः + येषाम्
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga before t becomes s (देवास्तस्थुः); after u and before the voiced y, the visarga (रु) appears as r (तस्थुर्येषाम्).
  • सकला देवास् sakalā devās → सकलाः + देवाः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga is lost, leaving -ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • इन्द्राद्याः indrādyāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: इन्द्र + आदि · Vigraha: इन्द्रः आदिः येषां ते indraḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those of whom Indra is the first”; in the verse: Indra and the other gods
  • शुम्भादीनाम् śumbhādīnām — bahuvrīhi
    Members: शुम्भ + आदि · Vigraha: शुम्भः आदिः येषां ते, तेषाम् śumbhaḥ ādiḥ yeṣāṃ te, teṣām
    Literally “of those of whom Śumbha is the first”; in the verse: of Śumbha and his followers

C. Morphology

  • तस्थुः tasthuḥ — Perfect third plural of √स्था: reduplicated त- + स्थ् + -उः, the root vowel lost before the ending. ‘Stood (firm)’, i.e. held their ground.
  • प्रयास्यसि prayāsyasi — Simple future of प्र + √या (या + स्य): ‘will you go’. With कथम्: ‘how will you (dare to) go?’.
  • स्त्री strī — Nominative singular of the irregular feminine स्त्री, the subject being ‘you’ implied by the verb: ‘(you,) a woman’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

येषां (सम्मुखे) संयुगे इन्द्राद्याः सकलाः देवाः न तस्थुः, तेषां शुम्भादीनां सम्मुखं (त्वं) स्त्री कथं प्रयास्यसि?

yeṣāṃ (sammukhe) saṃyuge indrādyāḥ sakalāḥ devāḥ na tasthuḥ, teṣāṃ śumbhādīnāṃ sammukhaṃ (tvaṃ) strī kathaṃ prayāsyasi?

A relative clause (येषां … न तस्थुः) names the asuras; the main clause is a rhetorical question with the future प्रयास्यसि, its subject ‘you’ implied.

6Translation

LiteralOf whom in battle all the gods headed by Indra did not stand (before) — how will you, a woman, go to the front of those, Śumbha and the rest?

ReadableIndra and all the gods could not hold their ground in battle against Śumbha and his host. How will you, a woman, go out to face them?

The relative construction is turned into two sentences.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe messenger repeats his argument with a fact the hearer knows: Indra and all the gods were defeated by these asuras (5.2–5). How then can a woman go out against them?

Traditional interpretationThe listener has just heard the same defeated gods praise the Devī as the power behind all beings (5.9–82). The messenger’s reasoning measures her against the gods; the text has already placed her above them.

5.126Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सा त्वं गच्छ मयैवोक्ता पार्श्वं शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः।

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • मयैवोक्ता mayaivoktā → मया + एव + उक्ता
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, guṇa) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + e → ai (मयैव); a + u → o (एवोक्ता).
  • पार्श्वं शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः pārśvaṃ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ → पार्श्वम् + शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.
  • केशाकर्षण keśākarṣaṇa → केश + आकर्षण
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • केशाकर्षणनिर्धूतगौरवा keśākarṣaṇanirdhūtagauravā — bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (केशाकर्षणनिर्धूत) and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (केशाकर्षण)
    Members: केशाकर्षण + निर्धूत + गौरव · Vigraha: केशानाम् आकर्षणम् केशाकर्षणम्; केशाकर्षणेन निर्धूतं गौरवं यस्याः सा keśānām ākarṣaṇam keśākarṣaṇam; keśākarṣaṇena nirdhūtaṃ gauravaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose dignity has been shaken off by the dragging of the hair”; in the verse: dragged by the hair and stripped of all honour

C. Morphology

  • गमिष्यसि gamiṣyasi — Future of √गम् (गम् + इष्य). With मा it expresses a warning: ‘do not (end up) going …’, ‘lest you go …’. Pāṇini’s prohibitive construction is मा with the unaugmented aorist; मा with the future is a looser epic and Purāṇic usage.
  • उक्ता uktā — Past participle of √वच्: ‘(you) having been told by me alone’ — that is, go now at a messenger’s word, while it is still a request.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सा त्वं मया एव उक्ता (सती) शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः पार्श्वं गच्छ; केशाकर्षणनिर्धूतगौरवा (सती) मा गमिष्यसि।

sā tvaṃ mayā eva uktā (satī) śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ pārśvaṃ gaccha; keśākarṣaṇanirdhūtagauravā (satī) mā gamiṣyasi |

An imperative (गच्छ) and a prohibitive future (मा गमिष्यसि) set against each other: go now willingly, not later by force.

6Translation

LiteralSo go, bidden by me alone, to the side of Śumbha and Niśumbha; do not go (later) with your dignity shaken off by the dragging of your hair.

ReadableGo, then, to Śumbha and Niśumbha now, at my word alone — lest you go dragged there by the hair, stripped of your honour.

‘Now’ and ‘later’ (lit.) and ‘lest’ (tr) bring out the contrast of the two verbs; they are implied, not stated.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe messenger ends with a threat: let her go willingly at his word, or she will be taken by force, dragged by the hair. The threat sets up the next chapter, where Dhūmralocana is sent to do exactly that (6.3–4: केशाकर्षणविह्वलाम्).

Traditional interpretationDragging by the hair is the extreme insult to a woman’s honour in the epic world. The threat turns back on those who make it: the asuras sent to seize her are destroyed, beginning with Dhūmralocana in the next chapter.

5.128Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एवमेतद् बली शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान्।
किं करोमि प्रतिज्ञा मे यदनालोचिता पुरा ॥ १२८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एवम् | एतत् | बली | शुम्भः | निशुम्भः | च | अतिवीर्यवान्किम् | करोमि | प्रतिज्ञा | मे | यत् | अनालोचिता | पुरा

3IAST

As recitedevametad balī śumbho niśumbhaścātivīryavān |
kiṃ karomi pratijñā me yadanālocitā purā ||

Separatedevam | etat | balī | śumbhaḥ | niśumbhaḥ | ca | ativīryavān
kim | karomi | pratijñā | me | yat | anālocitā | purā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एवम्evamso, thusindeclinable (adverb of manner); predicate: ‘(it is) so’
एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
बलीbalīstrong, mightym. nom. sg. of बलिन् (बल + possessive ini); predicate of शुम्भः
शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
निशुम्भःniśumbhaḥNiśumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
अतिवीर्यवान्ativīryavānexceedingly valiantm. nom. sg. of अतिवीर्यवत् (see Samāsa); predicate of निशुम्भः
किम्kimwhat?interrogative pronoun किम्, n. acc. sg.; object of करोमि
करोमिkaromi(can) I do√कृ (tanādi 8U ‘to do’), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
प्रतिज्ञाpratijñāvow, promisef. nom. sg. of प्रतिज्ञा; subject (verb ‘was’ understood)

पुराpurāformerly, long agoindeclinable (adverb of time)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एवमेतद् बली evametad balī → एवम् + एतत् + बली
    no change before a vowel; consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The m of एवम् is joined to the vowel of एतत्; the final t of एतत् becomes d before the voiced b, written with a virāma and a space.
  • बली शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान् balī śumbho niśumbhaścātivīryavān → बली + शुम्भः + निशुम्भः + च + अतिवीर्यवान्
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o; visarga before c becomes ś; a + a → ā.
  • यदनालोचिता yadanālocitā → यत् + अनालोचिता
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिवीर्यवान् ativīryavān — prādi tatpuruṣa (अतिवीर्य) with the possessive taddhita -वत् (matup)
    Members: अति + वीर्य (+ matup -वत्) · Vigraha: अतिशयितं वीर्यम् अतिवीर्यम्; तत् अस्य अस्ति इति अतिवीर्यवान् atiśayitaṃ vīryam ativīryam; tat asya asti iti ativīryavān
    Literally “possessing surpassing valour”; in the verse: exceedingly mighty
  • अनालोचिता anālocitā — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ (नञ्) + आलोचिता · Vigraha: न आलोचिता na ālocitā
    Literally “not considered”; in the verse: made rashly, without thought

C. Morphology

  • करोमि karomi — Present of √कृ, eighth class (कर-उ-मि → करोमि). The present in a deliberative question: ‘what am I to do?’.
  • अनालोचिता anālocitā — Past participle of आ + √लोच् (tenth class, so -इत: आलोचित) negated with अन्- before the vowel. It echoes अल्पबुद्धित्वात् of 5.119.
  • बली balī — Nominative singular of the in-stem बलिन् ‘having strength’: the n is lost and the i lengthened (बली).

D. Anvaya — prose order

एतत् एवम् (अस्ति): शुम्भः बली, निशुम्भः च अतिवीर्यवान्। किं करोमि? यत् मे प्रतिज्ञा पुरा अनालोचिता (कृता)।

etat evam (asti): śumbhaḥ balī, niśumbhaḥ ca ativīryavān | kiṃ karomi? yat me pratijñā purā anālocitā (kṛtā) |

A concession (एवम् एतत्) spelled out in two nominal sentences; a deliberative question; a causal clause (यत् …) giving the reason.

6Translation

LiteralThis is so: Śumbha is strong, and Niśumbha exceedingly valiant. What can I do, since my vow (was made) long ago without reflection?

ReadableIt is just as you say: Śumbha is mighty, and Niśumbha of surpassing valour. But what can I do? My vow was made long ago, without thought.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Devī concedes the messenger’s point about the brothers’ strength, as she did at first (5.118), and again pleads her old vow: she cannot set it aside. (A variant: for निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान् the GRETIL Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa and Freund read निशुम्भश्चापि तादृशः, the phrase of 5.118; the Gita Press reading is kept, and the sense is the same.)

Traditional interpretationThe mock-helplessness (किं करोमि) and the claim that the vow was ill-considered (अनालोचिता) repeat the irony of 5.119. The vow cannot be broken, so the only way to her hand is through battle — which is the outcome she intends.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Textual variant (corrections.json, status ‘Textual Verification Required’): Gita Press निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान्, other witnesses निशुम्भश्चापि तादृशः. The analysis follows the Gita Press text.

5.129Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

स त्वं गच्छ मयोक्तं ते यदेतत्सर्वमादृतः।
तदाचक्ष्वासुरेन्द्राय स च युक्तं करोतु तत् ॥ १२९ ॥ ॐ

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सः | त्वम् | गच्छ | मया | उक्तम् | ते | यत् | एतत् | सर्वम् | आदृतःतत् | आचक्ष्व | असुरेन्द्राय | सः | च | युक्तम् | करोतु | तत्

3IAST

As recitedsa tvaṃ gaccha mayoktaṃ te yadetatsarvamādṛtaḥ |
tadācakṣvāsurendrāya sa ca yuktaṃ karotu tat ||

tat | ācakṣva | asurendrāya | saḥ | ca | yuktam | karotu | tat

सःsaḥsuch (as you are), that onepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies त्वम्

सर्वम्sarvamallpronominal adjective सर्व, n. nom. sg.; qualifies यत्
आदृतःādṛtaḥattentively, with carepast participle (kta) of आ + √दृ (tudādi 6Ā ‘to heed, respect’), m. nom. sg., active in sense (‘having taken heed’); qualifies त्वम्
तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; correlative of यत्; object of आचक्ष्व
आचक्ष्वācakṣvatell, reportआ + √चक्ष् (adādi 2Ā ‘to tell’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada

चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
युक्तम्yuktamwhat is fittingpast passive participle (kta) of √युज् (‘yoked, fit’), n. acc. sg., used as a noun; qualifies the last तत्
करोतुkarotulet (him) do√कृ (tanādi 8U), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तत्tatthatpronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; object of करोतु, with युक्तम् (‘that which is fitting’)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स त्वं sa tvaṃ → सः + त्वम्
    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)
    The visarga of सः is dropped before a consonant; the same in स च.
  • मयोक्तं mayoktaṃ → मया + उक्तम्
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    ā + u → o.
  • यदेतत्सर्वमादृतः yadetatsarvamādṛtaḥ → यत् + एतत् + सर्वम् + आदृतः
    consonant sandhi (voicing); no change · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); खरि च (8.4.55)
    t → d before the vowel of एतत्; the t of एतत् stays unvoiced before s; the m of सर्वम् is joined to the vowel of आदृतः.
  • तदाचक्ष्वासुरेन्द्राय tadācakṣvāsurendrāya → तत् + आचक्ष्व + असुरेन्द्राय
    consonant sandhi (voicing); vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha, guṇa) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    t → d before a vowel; a + a → ā (आचक्ष्वासुर-); inside the compound a + i → e (असुरेन्द्र).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरेन्द्राय asurendrāya — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + इन्द्र · Vigraha: असुराणाम् इन्द्रः, तस्मै asurāṇām indraḥ, tasmai
    Literally “to the Indra (chief) of the asuras”; in the verse: to Śumbha, king of the asuras

C. Morphology

  • आचक्ष्व ācakṣva — Imperative ātmanepada of आ + √चक्ष्: चक्ष् + स्व, with the s lost between consonants (चक्ष्व). ‘Tell, report’.
  • आदृतः ādṛtaḥ — Past participle of आ + √दृ; besides ‘honoured’ it means ‘attentive, careful’, an active sense. Here it describes the messenger: report it all faithfully.
  • करोतु karotu — Third-person imperative of √कृ: ‘let him do’. The Devī leaves the decision — and so the war — to Śumbha.

D. Anvaya — prose order

सः त्वं गच्छ। मया ते यत् एतत् सर्वम् उक्तम्, तत् आदृतः (सन्) असुरेन्द्राय आचक्ष्व; सः च यत् युक्तं तत् करोतु।

saḥ tvaṃ gaccha | mayā te yat etat sarvam uktam, tat ādṛtaḥ (san) asurendrāya ācakṣva; saḥ ca yat yuktaṃ tat karotu |

Two imperatives addressed to the messenger (गच्छ, आचक्ष्व), the second with a relative pair (यत् … तत्) as its object; then a third-person imperative (करोतु) for Śumbha.

6Translation

LiteralSo go, you; all this that has been said to you by me, report that with care to the lord of the asuras; and let him do that which is fitting.

ReadableGo, then, and tell the lord of the asuras faithfully all that I have said to you; and let him do whatever he thinks fit.

युक्तम् ‘what is fitting’ is rendered ‘whatever he thinks fit’, the usual sense of the formula.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Devī dismisses the messenger: he is to report her words exactly to Śumbha, and Śumbha must decide. The chapter ends here; the next opens with the messenger’s report and Śumbha’s anger (6.2–3).

Traditional interpretationThe formula ‘let him do what is fitting’ courteously leaves the choice to the enemy, but the vow has already determined it: he can win her only in battle. In the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter’s last mantra is followed by the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the recitation, not to the Purāṇa, and the colophon names the chapter देव्या दूतसंवादः, ‘the Devī’s dialogue with the messenger’.

Colophon

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये देव्या दूतसंवादो नाम पञ्चमोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye devyā dūtasaṃvādo nāma pañcamo'dhyāyaḥ||

Chapter conclusion

Narrative summary

Robbed of their offices by Śumbha and Niśumbha, the gods do the one thing left to them: they remember the Goddess’s promise and praise her on the Himālaya, finding her in every state of every being. Their hymn is answered at once, when Śivā steps out of Pārvatī’s body, is named Kauśikī, and leaves Pārvatī dark as Kālikā. The asuras learn of her only through her beauty, and the chapter ends in an exchange of words: an offer of marriage made from the pride of possession, and a vow of battle that the Devī calls foolish but cannot break (5.128–129).

Principal vocabulary

SanskritIASTMeaning
विष्णुमायाviṣṇumāyāViṣṇu’s Māyā: the Goddess as the power named in all beings
आपद्āpadcalamity, misfortune (परमापदः, the gravest calamities)
स्मृताsmṛtāremembered: the condition on which she comes to help
संस्थिताsaṃsthitāabiding, present (in all beings)
चितिःcitiḥconsciousness, awareness
शक्तिःśaktiḥpower, capacity; the Goddess as power
भ्रान्तिःbhrāntiḥerror, delusion
अधिकारःadhikāraḥoffice, a god’s function in the cosmos
यज्ञभागःyajñabhāgaḥshare of the sacrifice, the gods’ portion
कौशिकीkauśikīshe who came from the sheath (कोश) of Pārvatī’s body
रत्नम्ratnamjewel; the best of any kind (elephant, horse, woman)
दूतःdūtaḥmessenger, envoy
प्रतिज्ञाpratijñāvow, solemn promise
भर्ताbhartāsupporter; husband

Important grammatical constructions

  • The dative of homage with नमः, and the -ai dative of feminine i-stems (प्रकृत्यै, कृत्यै, 5.9, 5.13).
  • The yā devī frame: relative या with correlative तस्यै, the instrumental of manner …रूपेण and the participle संस्थिता (5.20–76).
  • Perfects of √कृ in both voices for the same subjects (चक्राते, 5.3; चक्रतुः, 5.4).
  • Absolute constructions: genitive (स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानाम्, 5.84) and locative (तस्यां विनिर्गतायाम्, 5.88).
  • The passive imperative as a polite command (ज्ञायताम्, गृह्यताम्, 5.91; श्रूयताम्, 5.119).
  • The gerundive with the person told as subject (वक्तव्या, 5.103) and impersonal कार्यम् with the agent त्वया.
  • The general relative clause with present for future, closed by a future main verb (यो मां जयति … स मे भर्ता भविष्यति, 5.120).
  • Prohibition and warning with मा: मा … ब्रूहि (5.123), मा गमिष्यसि ‘lest you go’ (5.126).

Śākta insights

  • Remembrance is the gods’ only weapon, and it is enough: her promise was given for the moment of remembering (5.6), and the hymn ends by restating it as a fact about her (5.82).
  • No condition of a being is outside the Goddess: hunger, thirst and error are named beside intelligence, faith and peace (5.26–28, 5.35–37, 5.74–76).
  • All her forms are forms of one consciousness, which pervades the whole world (5.17–19, 5.78–80).
  • The Goddess who is praised and the Goddess who asks are one: Śivā answers from within Pārvatī (5.85–86).
  • Those who own everything cannot see her except as one more possession (5.100, 5.112); what they offer her is already hers (5.114, 5.116).
  • She is won by no one: her only ‘husband’ would be one who conquers her and is her equal in strength (5.120).

Śrīvidyā connections

The hymn shares many names with the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa): Viṣṇumāyā (339, विष्णुमाया), Mahādevī (209, महादेवी), Śivā (53, शिवा), Durgā (190, दुर्गा), Kalyāṇī (324, कल्याणी), Śarvāṇī (124, शर्वाणी), Gaurī (635, गौरी), and several of the qualities of the yā devī stanzas, among them तुष्टिः (443), शान्तिः (447), कान्तिः (449), स्मृतिः (540) and लज्जा (740); the idea of the Goddess as consciousness is shared too (362, चितिः; 417, चेतनारूपा). In the narrative Pārvatī (246, पार्वती) and Ambikā (295, अम्बिका) are names of both texts. These are shared Śākta names and ideas, not evidence that one text depends on the other; the story of Kauśikī’s emergence belongs to the Devī Māhātmya, and no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine is stated in this chapter.

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 5.6 — The boon recalled: remembered in calamity, she will destroy the gods’ gravest calamities at that very instant.
  • 5.9 — नमो देव्यै महादेव्यै — the opening of the gods’ hymn.
  • 5.14-16 — The first yā devī stanza: homage to the Goddess who in all beings is named Viṣṇumāyā.
  • 5.78-80 — The last stanza of the series: she who, as consciousness, pervades this whole world and abides in it.
  • 5.87 — Ambikā comes forth from the sheath of Pārvatī’s body and is celebrated in all the worlds as Kauśikī.
  • 5.116 — Grave yet smiling within, Durgā, by whom this world is upheld, answers Śumbha’s envoy.
  • 5.120 — The vow: whoever conquers her in battle, humbles her pride and is her equal in strength shall be her husband.

Chapter completion report

Chapter5 — The Devī's Dialogue with the Messenger (देव्या दूतसंवादः)
Source editionŚrīdurgāsaptaśatī, Gita Press, Gorakhpur (Hindi translation by Paṇḍit Rāmanārāyaṇadatta Śāstrī “Rāma”)
Verse range5.1–5.129 (Gita Press mantra numbers)
Verse entries85 entries for 129 mantras — uvāca 9, half-ślokas 0, ślokas 54, three-part mantras 66; the counts equal the chapter's colophon
Analysed85 of 85 entries
NumberingGita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 85) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.
Corrections to the digital text5.99: वह्निरपि* ददौ → वह्निरपि ददौ
Variant readings (kept as printed)5.11: नैर्ऋत्यै / नैरृत्यै (orthographic)
5.104: सा देवी तां ततः प्राह / तां च देवीं ततः प्राह (Textual Verification Required)
5.128: निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान् / निशुम्भश्चापि तादृशः (Textual Verification Required)
Uncertain readings or interpretations5.9, 5.11, 5.41-43, 5.59-61, 5.77, 5.82, 5.104, 5.128
Sandhi patterns
  • Visarga sandhi is by far the commonest junction: -aḥ before voiced sounds → -o (नमो देव्यै), visarga → s/ś before t/c (नमस्तस्यै, निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान्), and -aḥ + a → -o’ (दूतोऽहं, 5.106; शुम्भोऽत्र, 5.121).
  • सः and एषः drop the visarga before any consonant (स तत्र, 5.104; स मे, 5.120; एष महापद्मः, 5.96).
  • Savarṇa-dīrgha, with the Gita Press double avagraha marking a long ā (यथाऽऽपत्सु, 5.6; पुराऽऽसीत्, 5.97); yaṇ (संस्मरन्त्यपराजिताम्, 5.5; देव्युवाच); vṛddhi (तथैन्दवम्, 5.3; मैवं, 5.123); pūrvarūpa (स्तूयतेऽत्र, 5.85; तेऽङ्गणे, 5.95).
  • Final m → anusvāra before consonants throughout; t → n before a nasal (कस्मान्न, 5.100; वचनान्मम, 5.103); ch doubled after a short vowel (सर्वभूतेषुच्छाया-, 5.29–31).
  • One declared hiatus: आख्याता | अतीव at the pāda break of 5.90, where the reported speech begins.
Samāsa constructions
  • In the yā devī stanzas, karmadhārayas whose first member is the form itself (बुद्धिरूपेण, निद्रारूपेण …, ‘the form that is intelligence’).
  • Bahuvrīhis of epithet, including feminines in -ī from body-parts (चञ्चलापाङ्गी, 5.113; अतिचार्वङ्गी, 5.92) and nested ones (केशाकर्षणनिर्धूतगौरवा, 5.126).
  • Upapada tatpuruṣas with agent forms (सर्वकारिणी, 5.12; रत्नभुजः, 5.112; पुरन्दरः, 5.94), and dvandvas of the paired brothers (शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः).
Grammatical observations
  • स्म in 5.9 is printed short for the metre; read as the particle with the copula understood, or as स्मः with the visarga lost metri causa — the sense is the same.
  • प्रणताम् (5.11) is taken as a short genitive plural ‘of those who bow’; its derivation is uncertain.
  • The two voices of √कृ stand side by side for the same subjects (चक्राते, 5.3; चक्रतुः, 5.4).
  • In 5.104 the kept Gita Press reading makes सा देवी the subject of यत्रास्ते; the GRETIL and Freund reading is smoother.
  • Several epic or ārṣa forms are kept as printed: शृणुष्व (5.107), प्राप्स्यसे (5.114), parasmaipada व्यपोहति (5.120), मा with the imperative (5.123).
  • The closing prayer changes metre: vaṃśastha (5.81) and indravaṃśā (5.82) after the anuṣṭubh of the hymn.
Philosophical themes
  • Remembrance and the promise of help
  • The Goddess in all beings
  • Kauśikī and Kālikā: the emergence from Pārvatī
  • Possession and the vow
Open questions
  • 5.11: नैर्ऋत्यै (Gita Press) / नैरृत्यै (GRETIL, Freund) — orthographic only.
  • 5.104: सा देवी तां ततः प्राह (Gita Press) / तां च देवीं ततः प्राह (GRETIL, Freund) — Textual Verification Required.
  • 5.128: निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान् (Gita Press) / निशुम्भश्चापि तादृशः (GRETIL, Freund) — Textual Verification Required.
  • Interpretive doubts are flagged on 5.9, 5.11, 5.41–43, 5.59–61, 5.77 and 5.82.
